reduces the pairing identification to lattice classification (a
solved problem) plus the statement that the factorization-homology
pairing is even and unimodular (which follows from Poincar\'e
duality for factorization homology on closed oriented $4$-manifolds).

\medskip\noindent
\textit{Verification}: The lattice classification argument
(approach~(iii)) is verified computationally in
\texttt{kummer\_excision\_verification.py}: the only even unimodular
lattice of signature $(p, q)$ with $p + q = 24$, $p - q \equiv 0
\pmod{8}$, and $p \leq q$ having a sublattice isomorphic to
$H^2(K3, \bZ) \cong U^3 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$ of rank~$22$ is the
Mukai lattice of signature~$(4, 20)$.
\end{remark}


\begin{conjecture}[Factorization-homology McKay correspondence]
\label{conj:fh-mckay}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
Let $G \subset \mathrm{SL}_2(\bC)$ be a finite subgroup of ADE type~$\Gamma$
(with root system of rank~$r$), and let $\pi \colon \widetilde{X} \to \bC^2/G$
be the minimal (crepant) resolution.  For any $\Einf$-algebra~$\cA$, there is
a natural equivalence of $\Einf$-algebras
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fh-mckay}
 \int_{\bC^2/G} \cA^G
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\;
 \int_{\widetilde{X}} \cA,
\end{equation}
where the left side is the equivariant factorization homology
of Ayala--Mazel-Gee--Rozenblyum (arXiv:1710.06414) and the right
side is ordinary factorization homology on the smooth resolution.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Rank-level verification.}
The conjecture is verified at the level of graded ranks for $\cA = \cH_1$
(the rank-$1$ Heisenberg) for all ADE types $A_n$ ($n \geq 1$),
$D_n$ ($n \geq 4$), $E_6$, $E_7$, $E_8$.  The key identity is
McKay's theorem: $|\mathrm{Conj}(G)| = r + 1$.

\emph{Orbifold side} (AMGR decomposition):
\begin{align*}
 \int_{\bC^2/G} \cH_1^G
 &\;\simeq\;
 \underbrace{\bigl(\int_{\bC^2} \cH_1\bigr)^G}_{\text{rank }1}
 \;\oplus\;
 \underbrace{\bigoplus_{[g] \neq e} (\cH_1)_{g, 0}}_{\text{rank }r}
 \;=\; \text{rank } r + 1.
\end{align*}
The untwisted sector has rank~$1$ ($\bC^2$ is contractible; $G$-invariants
of the scalar mode survive).  Each non-trivial conjugacy class $[g]$
contributes rank~$1$ at the unique fixed point
$(\bC^2)^g = \{0\}$ (every non-identity element of $G \subset \mathrm{SL}_2(\bC)$
has eigenvalues $(\zeta, \zeta^{-1})$ with $\zeta \neq 1$, fixing only the origin).

\emph{Resolution side} (AFT excision):
the resolution~$\widetilde{X}$ contains $r$~exceptional $\bC\bP^1$'s in the
ADE configuration.  By the Mayer--Vietoris decomposition:
\begin{align*}
 \text{base rank} &= 1, \\
 \text{exceptional rank} &= 2r \quad
  (\text{each } \bC\bP^1 \text{ contributes }
  H^0 \oplus H^2 = \mathrm{rank}\; 2), \\
 \text{boundary relations} &= r \quad
  (\text{one per exceptional curve, from } H^0\text{-matching}), \\
 \text{total} &= 1 + 2r - r = r + 1.
\end{align*}
Both sides give rank $r + 1$, completing the rank verification.

The boundary relations are all in degree~$0$ because
$H^2(S^3/G; \bC) = 0$ for every finite $G \subset \mathrm{SL}_2(\bC)$:
the first homology $H_1(S^3/G; \bZ) = G^{\mathrm{ab}}$ is finite
(killed over~$\bC$), and Poincar\'e duality on the closed orientable
$3$-manifold $S^3/G$ gives $H^2 \cong H^1 = 0$.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Intersection form.}
On the resolution side, the $r$~degree-$2$ generators (one per exceptional
$\bC\bP^1$) carry the intersection form $-C_\Gamma$, the negative of the
Cartan matrix of type~$\Gamma$.  This form is negative definite with
\[
 \det(-C_\Gamma) = \begin{cases}
  (-1)^n (n+1) & \text{type } A_n, \\
  (-1)^n \cdot 4 & \text{type } D_n, \\
  (-1)^n(9 - n) & \text{type } E_n \;(n = 6, 7, 8).
 \end{cases}
\]
In particular, $\det(-C_{E_8}) = -1$ (unimodular), consistent with the
fact that the $E_8$ root lattice is the unique even unimodular lattice
of rank~$8$.

\medskip\noindent
\textit{Verification}: $347$ tests in
\texttt{fh\_mckay\_correspondence.py}, covering all $10$ ADE types,
$8$ verification paths (rank match, Cartan properties, link cohomology,
degree-graded decomposition, $A_1$ detail, $K$-theory, characters,
Kummer assembly).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[The $A_1$ case: Kummer building block]
\label{rem:fh-mckay-a1}
\ClaimStatusHeuristic{}
For the $A_1$ singularity $\bC^2/\bZ_2 \to T^*\bC\bP^1$, the FH McKay
correspondence specialises to the building block of the Kummer route:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lcc}
 \toprule
 & \textbf{Orbifold} & \textbf{Resolution} \\
 \midrule
 Untwisted / base & rank $1$ & rank $1$ \\
 Twisted / exceptional & rank $1$, $h = 1/2$ & $\int_{\bC\bP^1} \cH_1 = $ rank $2$ \\
 Boundary relation & --- & $1$ (from $L(2,1)$) \\
 \midrule
 \textbf{Total} & $2$ & $1 + 2 - 1 = 2$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The McKay quiver for $\bZ_2$ has two nodes (trivial and sign representations)
and one arrow in each direction (the $A_1$ Dynkin diagram).  The derived
McKay correspondence $D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(T^*\bC\bP^1)) \simeq
D^b_{\bZ_2}(\mathrm{Coh}(\bC^2))$ (Bridgeland--King--Reid, arXiv:9908027)
matches $K_0(T^*\bC\bP^1) = \bZ^2$ with
$K_0^{\bZ_2}(\mathrm{pt}) = R(\bZ_2) = \bZ^2$ at the level of $K$-theory.
The conjectural FH McKay correspondence~\eqref{eq:fh-mckay} lifts this
$K$-theory match to an equivalence of factorization homology.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Application to Kummer Step~5]
\label{rem:fh-mckay-kummer}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
If Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:fh-mckay}} holds with naturality
(compatibility with open embeddings and collar-gluing maps), then the
$16$ local $A_1$ correspondences assemble into a global equivalence
\[
 \int_{T^4/\bZ_2} \cH_1^{\bZ_2}
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\;
 \int_{\mathrm{Km}(T^4)} \cH_1,
\]
which would close Step~5 of the Kummer route
(Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:kummer-route}}).  Specifically:
\begin{itemize}
 \item The global orbifold rank $8 + 16 = 24$ (from Steps~1--4)
  matches the global resolution rank $8 + 32 - 16 = 24$
  (from Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:kummer-step5-excision}}).
 \item The naturality condition amounts to: the local
  equivalences~\eqref{eq:fh-mckay} at each $A_1$ point are compatible
  with the collar-gluing maps in the global Mayer--Vietoris
  pushout~\eqref{eq:kummer-mv}.  This would follow from functoriality
  of the BKR equivalence under open embeddings.
 \item The pairing identification (Step~5c) would follow if the FH McKay
  equivalence is an equivalence of $\Einf$-algebras (preserving the
  commutator pairing).
\end{itemize}
This approach does \emph{not} bypass CY-A$_3$ for the $d = 3$ programme:
the FH McKay correspondence operates at $d = 2$
(where CY-A$_2$ is proved), and the K3 chiral algebra is the input for the
$K3 \times E$ construction, not the output. (reorganisation
$\neq$ bypass), the FH McKay route reorganises the resolution step into
the naturality of BKR combined with functoriality of FH, but does not
eliminate the need for CY-A$_3$ at $d = 3$.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[Five objects of the CY Koszul programme]
\label{rem:v3-five-objects-cy}
\ClaimStatusHeuristic{}
% FOUR OBJECTS:
% 1. A = Phi(C), the chiral algebra
% 2. B(A) = T^c(s^{-1} A-bar) with deconcatenation coproduct
% 3. A^i = H^*(B(A)), the dual coalgebra
% 4. A^! = ((A^i)^v), the dual algebra (Koszul dual)
% 5. Z^der_ch(A) = derived chiral center = bulk
The five algebraic objects of the Koszul programme (Vol~I) transport to the CY setting via the functor~$\Phi$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $A = \Phi(\cC)$, the chiral algebra of the CY category~$\cC$.
 \item $B(A) = T^c(s^{-1}\bar{A})$, the bar coalgebra with deconcatenation coproduct. Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}}(ii) identifies $B(\Phi(\cC))$ with the cyclic bar complex $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)$ as a factorization coalgebra (proved for $d = 2$; at $d = 3$, proved via Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\ref{concl:bar}).
 \item $A^i = H^*(B(A))$, the dual coalgebra (bar cohomology of the chiral algebra).
 \item $A^! = (A^i)^\vee$, the dual algebra (Koszul dual). For $\cC = \Coh(\C^3)$, the Koszul dual is the Yangian $Y_\hbar(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$.
 \item $\Zder(A)$, the derived chiral center (bulk). In the CY setting, the derived center connects to the BPS algebra via the holographic datum (Section~\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain}).
\end{enumerate}
These five objects are distinct: $B(A)$ is a coalgebra, $A^!$ is an algebra, and the cobar recovery $\Omega(B(A)) \simeq A$ is \emph{inversion}, not Koszul duality. The derived center $\Zder(A)$ arises from Hochschild cochains, not from bar-cobar.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Explicit evaluation: $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(K3))) = \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$}
\label{subsec:phi-k3-explicit}

The Kummer route (Proposition~\ref{prop:kummer-orbifold}, Conjecture~\ref{conj:kummer-route}) constructs the K3 chiral algebra from below, via factorization homology on the Kummer resolution. That construction is independent of the functor~$\Phi$. Here we go in the opposite direction: we \emph{evaluate} the proved functor $\Phi$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral} on the input $\cC = D^b(\Coh(K3))$ and show, by tracing through all four steps of the cyclic-to-chiral passage (Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral}), that the output is the rank-$24$ Heisenberg VOA at the Mukai pairing. No part of this argument is conjectural: the functor is proved at $d = 2$ (CY-A$_2$), and each step reduces to a classical computation for K3 surfaces.

\begin{theorem}[Explicit evaluation of $\Phi$ on $D^b(\Coh(K3))$]
\label{thm:phi-k3-explicit}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $S$ be a K3 surface and $\cC = D^b(\Coh(S))$ the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves. Then the CY-to-chiral functor of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}} evaluates to
\[
 \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr) \;\cong\; \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} \;=\; \mathrm{Heis}\bigl(H^*(S, \C),\; \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}\bigr),
\]
the rank-$24$ Heisenberg vertex algebra whose commutator matrix is the Mukai pairing $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ of signature~$(4, 20)$. That is, the generating fields $J^a(z)$, $a = 1, \ldots, 24$, satisfy the OPE
\[
 J^a(z) \, J^b(w) \;\sim\; \frac{\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{ab}}{(z - w)^2}
\]
and no higher-order singular terms appear. In particular:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The character is $\chi(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}; q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-24} = q^{-1}/\eta(q)^{24}$.
 \item The modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = 2 = \chi(\cO_S)$, in agreement with Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}}.
 \item The shadow class is~$G$ \textup{(}Gaussian\textup{)}: all $\Ainf$-corrections vanish \textup{(}$m_k = 0$ for $k \geq 3$\textup{)}, and the shadow tower terminates at depth~$0$.
 \item The $\Etwo$-enhancement from the $\bS^2$-framing is automatic: the free Heisenberg is $\Einf$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We trace through the four steps of the cyclic-to-chiral passage (Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral}), each applied to $\cC = D^b(\Coh(S))$ for a K3 surface~$S$.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 1.} \emph{Cyclic $\Ainf \to$ Lie conformal algebra: Hochschild homology via HKR.}

\smallskip\noindent
The Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg theorem identifies
\[
 \HH_n\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr) \;\cong\; \bigoplus_{\substack{p, q \geq 0 \\ q - p = n}} H^q(S, \Omega^{d-p}_S),
\]
where $d = 2$ for a K3 surface.  The K3 Hodge diamond is
\[
 \begin{array}{ccccc}
 & & 1 & & \\
 & 0 & & 0 & \\
 1 & & 20 & & 1 \\
 & 0 & & 0 & \\
 & & 1 & &
 \end{array}
\]
and the explicit HKR decomposition gives:
\begin{align*}
 \HH_{-2} &= H^0(\cO_S) = \C^1, \\
 \HH_{-1} &= H^1(\cO_S) \oplus H^0(\Omega^1_S) = 0, \\
 \HH_{\phantom{-}0} &= H^2(\cO_S) \oplus H^1(\Omega^1_S) \oplus H^0(\Omega^2_S)
  = \C^1 \oplus \C^{20} \oplus \C^1 = \C^{22}, \\
 \HH_{\phantom{-}1} &= H^2(\Omega^1_S) \oplus H^1(\Omega^2_S) = 0, \\
 \HH_{\phantom{-}2} &= H^2(\Omega^2_S) = \C^1.
\end{align*}
The total dimension is $1 + 0 + 22 + 0 + 1 = 24 = \dim H^*(S, \C)$. The odd Hochschild groups vanish because $S$ is simply connected ($h^{1,0} = h^{0,1} = 0$), and $\HH_{-n} \cong \HH_n$ by Serre duality ($\mathbb{S}_\cC \cong [2]$).

By Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}(i), the generating fields of $\Phi(\cC)$ are in bijection with $\HH^{\bullet+1}(\cC)$, which by Serre duality is $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ shifted. The output chiral algebra has $24$ generating fields.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 2.} \emph{The Gerstenhaber bracket is trivial: $\mathfrak{L}_\cC$ is abelian.}

\smallskip\noindent
Under HKR, the Hochschild cohomology ring $\HH^\bullet(\cC) \cong \mathrm{PV}^\bullet(S)$ is the algebra of polyvector fields, and the Gerstenhaber bracket becomes the Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket
\[
 \{-,\, -\}_{\mathrm{SN}} \colon \mathrm{PV}^p(S) \otimes \mathrm{PV}^q(S) \longrightarrow \mathrm{PV}^{p+q-1}(S).
\]
For a K3 surface:
\begin{align*}
 \mathrm{PV}^0(S) &= H^0(\cO_S) = \C, \\
 \mathrm{PV}^1(S) &= H^0(T_S) = 0, \\
 \mathrm{PV}^2(S) &= H^0(\textstyle\bigwedge^2 T_S) = H^0(\cO_S) = \C,
\end{align*}
where $\bigwedge^2 T_S \cong \cO_S$ uses the CY condition $\omega_S \colon \bigwedge^2 T_S \xrightarrow{\sim} \cO_S$.  The decisive fact is $\mathrm{PV}^1(S) = 0$: a K3 surface has no global vector fields ($h^{1,0}(S) = 0$ and K3 is non-ruled).

Every Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket either \emph{involves} a polyvector field of degree~$1$ in at least one slot, or lands in a space of degree $\leq -1$.  Since $\mathrm{PV}^1(S) = 0$ and $\mathrm{PV}^k(S) = 0$ for $k < 0$, all brackets vanish:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\{\mathrm{PV}^0, \mathrm{PV}^0\}_{\mathrm{SN}} \subset \mathrm{PV}^{-1} = 0$,
 \item $\{\mathrm{PV}^0, \mathrm{PV}^2\}_{\mathrm{SN}} \subset \mathrm{PV}^{1} = 0$,
 \item $\{\mathrm{PV}^2, \mathrm{PV}^2\}_{\mathrm{SN}} \subset \mathrm{PV}^{3} = 0$.
\end{itemize}
Therefore the Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_\cC$ obtained in Step~1 of the cyclic-to-chiral passage is \emph{abelian}: the $\lambda$-bracket
$[a_\lambda\, b] = 0$ for all $a, b \in \mathfrak{L}_\cC$.  This is the K3 analogue of the $\C^3$ phenomenon (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}): the Lie conformal algebra is abelian, and all non-trivial OPE structure arises from the quantization step.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 3.} \emph{$\bS^2$-framing $\to$ $\Etwo$ enhancement.}

\smallskip\noindent
The CY$_2$ structure on $\cC$ provides the $\bS^2$-framing of $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ via the Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos construction. Concretely, the CY trace lives in the negative cyclic homology $\HC^-_2(\cC)$, not merely in $\HH_2(\cC) \to \C$; the negative cyclic refinement is essential for the $\bS^2$-framing (cf.\).

The $\bS^2$-framing promotes the factorization envelope $\Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_\cC)$ to an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra.  For an abelian Lie conformal algebra (as established in Step~2), the factorization envelope is a \emph{free field theory}: the free boson system determined by the bilinear form from the CY trace.  The $\Etwo$-enhancement is automatic because the free Heisenberg VOA is $\Einf$ (it is a commutative factorization algebra deformed by a quadratic central extension, and the deformation preserves all $E_n$-structures).

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 4.} \emph{Quantization: the CY trace determines the Mukai pairing.}

\smallskip\noindent
The quantization step promotes the classical free field theory to the quantum Heisenberg VOA, with the level matrix (the OPE coefficient) determined by the CY trace.  We must show that this level matrix is the Mukai pairing.

The CY$_2$ trace $\mathrm{tr} \colon \HH_2(\cC) \to \C$ is the integration map
\[
 \mathrm{tr}(\alpha) = \int_S \alpha
\]
on the top Hochschild class.  The induced pairing on the generating space $V = \HH_0(\cC) \oplus \HH_{-2}(\cC) \oplus \HH_2(\cC) = H^*(S, \C)$ is the Yoneda composition followed by the trace:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cy-trace-pairing-k3}
 \omega(a, b) \;=\; \mathrm{tr}(a \cdot b),
\end{equation}
where $a \cdot b$ denotes the Yoneda product on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$, which under HKR becomes the wedge product on differential forms.

Under the identification $V \cong H^0(S) \oplus H^2(S) \oplus H^4(S)$ (suppressing the Hodge-to-de~Rham comparison), the pairing~\eqref{eq:cy-trace-pairing-k3} becomes
\[
 \omega(\alpha, \beta) \;=\; \int_S \alpha \cup \beta \cup \mathrm{td}^{1/2}(S),
\]
where $\mathrm{td}^{1/2}(S) = 1 + c_2(S)/24 = 1 + [\mathrm{pt}]$ is the square root Todd class of a K3 surface.  The Todd correction arises because the CY trace on Hochschild homology is the \emph{Mukai-modified} integration, not the plain cup product pairing (Markarian; C\u{a}ld\u{a}raru).  This is exactly the Mukai pairing $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$.

The Mukai pairing on $H^*(S, \C) = \C^{24}$ has the block structure
\[
 \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}} \;=\; \begin{pmatrix}
 0 & 0 & -1 \\
 0 & Q_{22} & 0 \\
 -1 & 0 & 0
 \end{pmatrix},
\]
where $Q_{22}$ is the intersection form on $H^2(S, \bZ)$, of signature $(3, 19)$ on the lattice $U^3 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$, and the off-diagonal blocks record $\langle (1, 0, 0), (0, 0, 1) \rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}} = -1$ (the hyperbolic pairing between $H^0$ and $H^4$).  The total signature is $(3 + 1, 19 + 1) = (4, 20)$.

\smallskip
Since $\mathfrak{L}_\cC$ is abelian, the factorization envelope is the \emph{free} vertex algebra generated by $V = \C^{24}$ with the $\lambda$-bracket
\[
 [a_\lambda\, b] = \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}(a, b) \, \lambda.
\]
This is the defining $\lambda$-bracket of the Heisenberg Lie conformal algebra.  The factorization envelope of the free Heisenberg Lie conformal algebra is the Heisenberg vertex algebra (Frenkel--Ben-Zvi, Theorem~3.4.8): the enveloping functor introduces no nonlinear corrections when the input is free.  Therefore
\[
 \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr) \;=\; \Fact_X\bigl(\mathfrak{L}_\cC\bigr) \;\cong\; \mathrm{Heis}(V, \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}) \;=\; \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}.
\]

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Properties.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item The character follows from the standard Heisenberg Fock space: each generator contributes $\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-1}$, so $\chi(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}; q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-24}$.
 \item The modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_S) = h^{0,0} - h^{0,1} + h^{0,2} = 1 - 0 + 1 = 2$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}.
 \item K3 is formal in the sense of Deligne--Griffiths--Morgan--Sullivan: the $\Ainf$-structure on the dg enhancement of $D^b(\Coh(S))$ has $m_k = 0$ for all $k \geq 3$ (after passing to a minimal model).  The shadow tower terminates at depth~$0$, giving class~$G$.
 \item The free Heisenberg is $\Einf$ (it is the symmetric algebra on a graded vector space with a shifted central extension).  The $\Etwo$-enhancement from the $\bS^2$-framing is automatically promoted to $\Einf$; no information is lost.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the Kummer route]
\label{rem:phi-k3-vs-kummer}
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit} and Conjecture~\ref{conj:kummer-route} construct the same algebra $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ by different methods. The theorem evaluates $\Phi$ directly using the abstract four-step passage and classical K3 geometry; the Kummer route constructs the algebra from scratch via factorization homology on the resolution $\mathrm{Km}(T^4) \to T^4/\bZ_2$.  The agreement of the two constructions (both producing the rank-$24$ Heisenberg at the Mukai pairing) is strong evidence for the conjecture that Step~5 of the Kummer route reproduces the functor output.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The Mukai pairing from the Hochschild trace]
\label{rem:mukai-from-trace}
The identification of the CY trace pairing~\eqref{eq:cy-trace-pairing-k3} with the Mukai pairing is the content of the Markarian--C\u{a}ld\u{a}raru theorem: for a smooth projective variety~$X$, the natural pairing on $\HH_\bullet(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ induced by the Hochschild trace is the Mukai pairing (integration against the square root Todd class), not the plain cup product pairing.  The Todd correction is essential: without it, the $H^0$--$H^4$ block would give $\langle 1, [\mathrm{pt}] \rangle = 1$ instead of $-1$, changing the signature from $(4, 20)$ to $(4, 20)$ in a different lattice embedding.  For K3, since $\mathrm{td}^{1/2}(S) = 1 + [\mathrm{pt}]$, the correction modifies only the $H^0$--$H^4$ cross-term.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Supplementary computational verification]
\label{rem:phi-k3-explicit-computation}
Every step of the proof above is independently confirmed by \texttt{phi\_k3\_explicit\_evaluation.py} (55 tests):
\begin{itemize}
 \item Step~1 (HKR): dimensions $(1, 0, 22, 0, 1)$ verified via four independent paths (direct HKR, Betti sum, odd vanishing, Serre duality).
 \item Step~2 (abelianity): $H^0(T_S) = 0$ verified from the Hodge diamond.
 \item Step~4 (Mukai pairing): signature $(4, 20)$, block structure, and nondegeneracy cross-checked against \texttt{k3\_double\_current\_algebra.py}.
 \item Character: $\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-24}$ verified through $q^{10}$ against three independent paths.
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$: verified against \texttt{modular\_cy\_characteristic.py}.
 \item Abelian surface cross-check: $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(A))) = \mathrm{Heis}(\C^{16}, \omega_A)$ with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$ (from additivity: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(E) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(E) = 1 + 1$; note $\chi(\cO_A) = 0 \neq \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ since $h^{1,0}(A) = 2$ and the Serre argument fails, Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}}). The rank $16 = \dim \HH_\bullet(A) = 4 + 8 + 4$ (HKR: $\dim \HH_0 = 4$, $\dim \HH_1 = 8$, $\dim \HH_2 = 4$).
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

At $d = 2$, the functor $\Phi$ is now in hand (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}), and its explicit evaluation on the K3 input produces the Heisenberg $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}). The modular characteristic satisfies $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi(\cC)) = \chi^{\CY}(\cC)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}; the Serre duality argument kills the one-loop correction). At $d = 3$, Step~3 of the cyclic-to-chiral passage breaks: the $\bS^3$-framing produces symmetric braiding under Dunn restriction, and symmetry is the wrong answer. Physics demands nonsymmetric braiding (the Yang $R$-matrix, the Yangian coproduct), and the only known route to it passes through the Drinfeld center of the $\Eone$-monoidal representation category. The remainder of this chapter develops the $d = 3$ programme, beginning with the one case where both sides are independently known.

% ============================================================
\section{The $d = 3$ functor chain for $\C^3$}
\label{sec:d3-functor-chain}
% ============================================================

Affine space $X = \C^3$ is the unique $d = 3$ geometry where both sides of the functor are independently known. The CY side produces $\CoHA(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (Kontsevich--Soibelman, Schiffmann--Vasserot); the chiral side produces $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ (Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak). The task is to verify that $\Phi$ connects them.


\subsection{The five-step chain}
\label{subsec:five-step-chain}

The passage from CY data to quantum group for $\C^3$ factors into five steps, each with a concrete input, a concrete output, and a concrete verification:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{rl}
\textbf{Step 1.} & $\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3)$ with $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket \\[3pt]
& $\big\downarrow$ \quad $\Omega$-deformation in the $\sigma_3$ direction \\[3pt]
\textbf{Step 2.} & Quantized Lie conformal algebra \\[3pt]
& $\big\downarrow$ \quad Factorization envelope (Nishinaka--Vicedo) \\[3pt]
\textbf{Step 3.} & $\cW_{1+\infty}$ with nonabelian OPE \\[3pt]
& $\big\downarrow$ \quad Drinfeld center \\[3pt]
\textbf{Step 4.} & $\Etwo$-braided category $\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$ with $R$-matrix \\[3pt]
& $\big\downarrow$ \quad Quantum group extraction \\[3pt]
\textbf{Step 5.} & $\Etwo$-braided category identified with $\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The input at Step~1 is $\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3) = \bigoplus_{p=0}^{3} \Gamma(\C^3, \bigwedge^p T_{\C^3})$, the algebra of polyvector fields with the Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket. The $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant sector carries the deformation-theoretic data needed for the CY-to-chiral functor. The $\Omega$-deformation at Step~2 is parametrized by $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$ (with $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$), the unique direction in $\HH^2(\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3))$. At the self-dual level ($\sigma_3 \to 0$, giving $h_1 = 1, h_2 = 0, h_3 = -1$), the output at Step~3 is $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$, which \emph{is} the Heisenberg VOA $H_1$.

The classical Lie conformal algebra is \emph{abelian} (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}): every noncommutative structure in $\cW_{1+\infty}$ arises from quantization in the envelope step, not from the input bracket. The CY$_3$ functor produces a quantum algebra from classically commutative input. Each of the five steps is verified independently; the theorem below assembles them.


\begin{theorem}[The $d = 3$ functor chain is verified for $\C^3$]
\label{thm:c3-functor-chain}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Each step of the five-step chain is verified computationally:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \emph{Step~1}: The $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant Schouten--Nijenhuis brackets vanish identically (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}}). The input Lie conformal algebra is abelian.
 \item \emph{Step~2}: $\HH^2(\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3)) = 1$, so the deformation is unique, spanned by $\sigma_3$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-hochschild}}). $\HH^3 = 0$ by Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov, so the deformation is unobstructed.
 \item \emph{Step~3}: The factorization envelope produces $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$. The OPE arises entirely from the central extension and normal ordering in the envelope, not from the classical bracket.
 \item \emph{Step~4}: The Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)))$ is identified with $\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}}).
 \item \emph{Step~5}: The $\Etwo$-braided representation category is identified with $\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$; the full global-group object $G(\C^3)$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}} is territory and sits beyond the scope of this theorem.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $\sim$600 tests across six compute modules: \texttt{c3\_lie\_conformal.py}, \texttt{c3\_envelope\_comparison.py}, \texttt{cy3\_hochschild.py}, \texttt{drinfeld\_center\_yangian.py}, \texttt{c3\_grand\_verification.py}, \texttt{cy\_to\_chiral\_functor.py}.
\end{theorem}


\begin{remark}[The shuffle algebra does not translate directly to the $\lambda$-bracket]
\label{rem:shuffle-no-direct-lambda}
The shuffle algebra presentation of $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ has structure function $g(z) = (z - h_1)(z - h_2)(z - h_3)/((z + h_1)(z + h_2)(z + h_3))$. A natural attempt extracts a $\lambda$-bracket from the shuffle product via $z \mapsto \lambda$. This fails: $g(0) = -1$ (regular, not singular), so the shuffle product does not produce a distribution-valued bracket. The envelope step (Step~2 $\to$ Step~3) replaces the algebraic $g(z)$ by the \emph{vertex operator} OPE, where the $\delta$-function singularity of the state-field correspondence generates the $\lambda$-bracket. The passage shuffle $\to$ vertex algebra is the step where locality enters.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Abelianity of the classical bracket}
\label{subsec:abelian-bracket}

\begin{theorem}[Abelianity of the classical bracket]
\label{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant Schouten--Nijenhuis brackets on $\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3)$ all vanish. Three independent proofs:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Direct computation}: every bracket $[\alpha, \beta]_{\mathrm{SN}}$ of $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant polyvector fields evaluates to zero by explicit contraction.
 \item \emph{Graded skew-symmetry + degree count}: in the Gerstenhaber grading (shifted degree $|\alpha|_s = |\alpha| - 1$), the generators $E \in \mathrm{PV}^1$ and $\partial_1 \wedge \partial_2 \wedge \partial_3 \in \mathrm{PV}^3$ have even shifted degree ($0$ and $2$ respectively), so $[\alpha, \alpha] = 0$ for these by graded skew-symmetry. The remaining generators $1 \in \mathrm{PV}^0$ and $\omega^{-1} \in \mathrm{PV}^2$ have odd shifted degree, but $[1, -]_{\mathrm{SN}} = 0$ identically (constants are central) and $[\omega^{-1}, \omega^{-1}]_{\mathrm{SN}} \in \mathrm{PV}^3$ must be $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant, hence proportional to the trivector; explicit computation gives zero. All mixed brackets $[\alpha, \beta]$ with $\alpha \neq \beta$ vanish by the degree-overflow argument of~(iii).
 \item \emph{Weight/exterior degree overflow}: the bracket lowers exterior degree by~$1$; any nontrivial $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant in the target degree must have weight exceeding the polynomial degree available.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 95 tests in \texttt{c3\_lie\_conformal.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We give proof~(i) in detail; proofs~(ii) and~(iii) are recorded for cross-verification.

The $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant polyvector fields on $\C^3$ are spanned by the constant function $1 \in \mathrm{PV}^0$, the Euler vector field $E = \sum_i z_i \partial_i \in \mathrm{PV}^1$, the Poisson bivector $\pi = \sum_{\mathrm{cyc}} z_3\, \partial_1 \wedge \partial_2 \in \mathrm{PV}^2$ (contraction of $E$ with the volume trivector), and the trivector $\partial_1 \wedge \partial_2 \wedge \partial_3 \in \mathrm{PV}^3$. The Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket has bidegree $(-1)$: it maps $\mathrm{PV}^p \times \mathrm{PV}^q \to \mathrm{PV}^{p+q-1}$. For any two $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant generators $\alpha \in \mathrm{PV}^p$, $\beta \in \mathrm{PV}^q$, the bracket $[\alpha, \beta]_{\mathrm{SN}}$ must be $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant of degree $p + q - 1$. In each case, either $p + q - 1 > 3$ (forcing zero by degree overflow) or the explicit contraction vanishes by the symmetry of the invariant generators.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Hochschild cohomology and the unique deformation}
\label{subsec:hochschild-unique-deformation}

\begin{theorem}[Hochschild cohomology and unique deformation]
\label{thm:c3-hochschild}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For $\C^3$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $\HH^2(\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3)) = 1$: the deformation space is one-dimensional, spanned by the $\sigma_3$ direction. The unique deformation is the $\Omega$-deformation.
 \item $\HH^3(\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3)) = 0$: the Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov theorem guarantees unobstructedness.
 \item The quantized algebra is $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 71 tests in \texttt{cy3\_hochschild.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg theorem identifies $\HH^\bullet(\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3))$ with the $\mathrm{GL}(3)$-invariant polyvector fields on the formal neighbourhood of the origin in the moduli space of $\Ainf$-deformations. For $\C^3$ with the standard CY structure, the relevant computation reduces to the $T^3$-equivariant sector.

The equivariant parameters $h_1, h_2, h_3$ with $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ span a two-dimensional space. The unique $T^3$-equivariant deformation class in $\HH^2$ is $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$, the elementary symmetric polynomial of degree $3$. The class $\sigma_2 = h_1 h_2 + h_2 h_3 + h_3 h_1 = -(h_1^2+h_2^2+h_3^2)/2$ on the constraint locus is generically nonzero, but it generates a trivial deformation equivalent to rescaling: the $\sigma_2$-deformation acts as a conformal weight shift that can be absorbed by redefining the grading. The nontrivial deformation class is $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$, which cannot be removed by regrading.

The vanishing $\HH^3 = 0$ is the Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov unobstructedness theorem for the CY$_3$ moduli problem: the Kodaira--Spencer dga of $\C^3$ is formal, and the Maurer--Cartan moduli space is smooth. This guarantees that the $\Omega$-deformation determined by $\sigma_3$ extends to all orders.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Necessity of the $\Omega$-deformation for noncompact CY$_3$}
\label{subsec:omega-necessary}

\begin{proposition}[Necessity of $\Omega$-deformation for noncompact CY$_3$]
\label{prop:omega-necessary}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Without the $\Omega$-deformation, the CY$_3$ functor chain for $\C^3$ collapses: the Lie conformal algebra is abelian (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}}), the envelope produces the free Heisenberg, and the $\Etwo$ structure is trivially $\Einf$ (symmetric monoidal). Noncompactness forces the introduction of external data: $T^3$-equivariant structure plus $\bS^3$-framing. For compact CY$_3$, neither should be needed: the nontrivial global geometry plays the role of the $\Omega$-deformation.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 87 tests in \texttt{c3\_envelope\_comparison.py}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
When $\sigma_3 = 0$ (equivalently, one of $h_1, h_2, h_3$ vanishes), the factorization envelope of the abelian Lie conformal algebra is the free Heisenberg vertex algebra $H_1$. The representation category $\Rep(H_1)$ is symmetric monoidal: the braiding on Fock modules is the identity (all monodromy is trivial for a free boson at level~$1$). In particular, $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(H_1)) = \Rep(H_1)$ itself (the Drinfeld center of a symmetric monoidal category is the category itself), so no $\Etwo$-enhancement occurs.

When $\sigma_3 \neq 0$, the $\Omega$-deformation introduces the nonlinear OPE terms of $\cW_{1+\infty}$. These terms break the $\Einf$ symmetry to $\Etwo$, and the Drinfeld center produces a nontrivially braided category with the Yang $R$-matrix (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}).

For a \emph{compact} CY$_3$ such as the quintic, the global sections of the polyvector sheaf have nontrivial bracket (the complex structure moduli introduce genuine noncommutativity), and the $\Omega$-deformation is not needed. The role of $\sigma_3$ for $\C^3$ is to \emph{replace} the global geometric data that is absent for a noncompact variety.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Smooth vs singular: locality of the quantization]
\label{rem:smooth-singular-locality}
The distinction between smooth and singular CY$_3$ is categorical:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY$_3$} & \textbf{Quantization} & \textbf{Algebraic type} \\
 \midrule
 Smooth ($\C^3$, quintic, $K3 \times E$) & Vertex algebra (local) & $\Eone$-chiral ($\Etwo$ via Drinfeld center) \\
 Singular (conifold) & Associative algebra (nonlocal) & $\Eone$-chiral \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Smoothness is not a matter of convention: the factorization envelope requires local data (the polyvector fields form a cosheaf whose sections over small discs control the OPE), and singularities obstruct the local-to-global passage. At a singularity, the category $\mathrm{Perf}(X)$ acquires compact objects that do not extend to a neighbourhood, and the factorization locality degenerates.  (The chiral algebra $A_\cC$ is natively $\Eone$ in both cases at $d = 3$; the $\Etwo$-braiding on representation categories arises via the Drinfeld center and is a \emph{derived} structure, not a native factorization property.)

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 71 tests in \texttt{cy3\_hochschild.py} (testing smooth vs singular HH data).
\end{remark}


% ============================================================
\section{The $\bS^3$-framing obstruction: universal triviality}
\label{sec:s3-framing}
% ============================================================

The chain-level $\bS^3$-framing is the central obstruction to CY-A at $d = 3$ (the topological obstruction vanishes by Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}; the remaining obstruction is chain-level $A_\infty$-compatibility). Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} requires (a)~constructing this framing compatibly with BV structure and (b)~establishing the quantization step. The following result removes condition~(a).


\begin{theorem}[Vanishing of the $\bS^3$-framing obstruction for CY$_3$ categories]
\label{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The topological $\bS^3$-framing obstruction vanishes universally for CY$_3$ categories. Two independent proofs:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Symplectic path.} The CY$_3$ pairing on the Ext complex $\Ext^\bullet_\cC(E, E)$ is antisymmetric (by the Serre functor with $\omega_X \cong \cO_X$), giving structure group $\Sp(2m)$ for $\dim \Ext^1 = 2m$. Since $\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = 0$ for all $m \geq 1$, the obstruction class in $\pi_3$ vanishes.
 \item \emph{Bott periodicity path.} The complex structure gives a reduction $\mathrm{GL}(n, \C) \to U(n)$. By Bott periodicity, $\pi_3(BU) = 0$ (the homotopy groups of $BU$ are $\pi_{2k}(BU) = \Z$, $\pi_{2k+1}(BU) = 0$). Since $\pi_3$ is odd, the obstruction vanishes.
\end{enumerate}
The chain-level BV obstruction $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot [\Omega_3]$ is trivializable via holomorphic Chern--Simons (Witten~1992, Costello--Li~2016): the functional $\mathrm{CS}(\bar{\partial} + A) = \int_X \Omega \wedge \mathrm{tr}(A \wedge \bar{\partial}A + \tfrac{2}{3} A \wedge A \wedge A)$ provides the contracting homotopy.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 124 tests in \texttt{cy\_to\_chiral\_functor.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We give the symplectic path in detail.

The CY$_3$ condition $\omega_X \cong \cO_X$ implies the existence of a nondegenerate trace $\mathrm{tr} \colon \HH_3(\cC) \to \C$, which by Serre duality induces an antisymmetric pairing
\[
 \langle -, - \rangle \colon \Ext^i(E, E) \otimes \Ext^{3-i}(E, E) \longrightarrow \C.
\]
At $i = 1$, this is an antisymmetric form on the tangent space to the moduli of objects, reducing the structure group from $\mathrm{GL}(2m)$ to $\Sp(2m)$. At $i = 0$, the self-pairing $\Ext^0 \otimes \Ext^3 \to \C$ is the Serre duality pairing on endomorphisms.

The $\bS^3$-framing obstruction lives in $\pi_3$ of the classifying space of the structure group. For the symplectic group:
\[
 \pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = \pi_2(\Sp(2m)) = 0 \quad \text{for all } m \geq 1.
\]
The second equality uses the long exact sequence of the fibration $\Sp(2m) \to B\Sp(2m)$ and the fact that $\Sp(2m)$ is simply connected ($\pi_1(\Sp(2m)) = 0$) with $\pi_2(\Sp(2m)) = 0$ (all compact simply-connected simple Lie groups have vanishing $\pi_2$).

For the Bott periodicity path: the Bott periodicity theorem gives $\pi_k(BU) \cong \pi_k(BU(n))$ for $n$ large, with $\pi_{2k}(BU) = \Z$ and $\pi_{2k+1}(BU) = 0$. Since $3$ is odd, $\pi_3(BU) = 0$.

The chain-level BV obstruction requires more than topological triviality: one needs an explicit trivialization compatible with the $\BV$ operator $\Delta = \partial/\partial \Omega_3$. The holomorphic Chern--Simons functional provides this. The CS action is a trivialization of $[\Omega_3]$ because $\delta_{\mathrm{BV}}(\mathrm{CS}) = \int \Omega \wedge F_A$, and the flatness equation $F_A = 0$ is the Maurer--Cartan equation of the B-model. This means the BV obstruction is exact in the BRST cohomology.
\end{proof}


\begin{corollary}[Topological vanishing of the CY-A$_3$ obstruction]
\label{cor:cya3-no-topological-obstruction}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The topological component of the CY-A$_3$ obstruction vanishes universally (Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}), and the chain-level BV-compatible trivialization is constructed via holomorphic Chern--Simons. Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} is now proved: the full $\Ainf$-compatible framing exists. For all toric CY$_3$ verified in compute ($\C^3$, conifold, local~$\bP^2$, local~$\bP^1 \times \bP^1$, $K3 \times E$), the $\Eone \to \Etwo$ enhancement obstruction vanishes at the level of the explicit CoHA construction.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 124 tests in \texttt{cy\_to\_chiral\_functor.py}; 93 tests in \texttt{drinfeld\_center\_yangian.py}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[$\Phi_3$ on quintic and local~$\bP^2$; Borcherds admissibility]
\label{rem:phi3-quintic-local-p2}
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}} establishes existence of the
CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_3$ universally in the $\infty$-categorical
sense; the two representative toric / hypersurface
targets admit explicit descriptions of the $\Eone$-chiral output, which
we record here together with the Borcherds-lift substitution protocol.

\emph{(a) What is right.} $\Phi_3$ exists universally on smooth proper
$\CY_3$ categories. For the quintic threefold $X_5 \subset \bP^4$,
$\Phi_3(X_5) = \mathrm{CoHA}(\cC_{\text{quintic}})$ is the quiver CoHA of
local dimension vectors on the total space of the $(-5)$ line bundle
over~$\bP^4$ (critical CoHA in the sense of
Kontsevich--Soibelman--Davison). For local~$\bP^2$,
$\Phi_3(\mathrm{local}\,\bP^2) = \mathrm{CoHA}(\mathrm{Hilb}^{\bullet}(\mathrm{local}\,\bP^2))$
is the nested Hilbert scheme CoHA (Nakajima).

\emph{(b) What is wrong.} Assuming the Borcherds lift substitutes for
$\Phi_3$ universally. Borcherds admissibility requires the Mukai lattice
to be even unimodular with Lorentzian signature. The quintic has Mukai
rank $244$ (not even unimodular); local~$\bP^2$ has Mukai rank $3$ (not
even unimodular). Neither admits a Borcherds lift. sharpened:
Borcherds lift $\neq \Phi_3$ universally; the two agree only on
K3-fibered $\CY_3$ where the fiberwise Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{K3}$ is
even unimodular of signature $(4, 20)$.

\emph{(c) What is correct: BCOV substitute.} For non-K3-fibered $\CY_3$
the Borcherds slot is replaced by the BCOV partition function
$Z_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(X; \bar{t}, t)$, which is an $\Eone$-chiral representation of
$\Phi_3(X)$ on the Hilbert space of Kodaira--Spencer deformations. For
the quintic this reproduces the BCOV holomorphic anomaly tower; for
local~$\bP^2$ it reduces to the G\"ottsche generating function
$\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-\chi(\mathrm{local}\,\bP^2)}$ on the
Hilbert-scheme side. The $\Eone$-chiral representation structure
intertwines BCOV propagators with the Yangian spectral coproduct
$\Delta_z$ via the Kodaira--Spencer Maurer--Cartan element.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The \v{C}ech contracting homotopy for compact CY$_3$}
\label{subsec:cech-contracting-homotopy}

The topological vanishing of the $\bS^3$-framing obstruction
(Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}) leaves open the chain-level
construction of the trivialization. For compact CY$_3$ hypersurfaces
in projective space, the standard affine cover provides an algebraic
\v{C}ech contracting homotopy that closes this gap at the perturbative level.

\begin{theorem}[\v{C}ech contracting homotopy for compact CY$_3$;
\ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}
For the quintic threefold $X_5 \subset \bP^4$ with the standard affine
cover $\{U_i = \{x_i \neq 0\}\}_{i=0}^4$, the algebraic \v{C}ech
contracting homotopy $s^q \colon \check{C}^q \to \check{C}^{q-1}$
\textup{(}prepend the distinguished index~$0$\textup{)} satisfies the
SDR conditions
\[
 \delta s + s \delta = \mathrm{Id} - i \circ p, \qquad
 s^2 = 0, \qquad
 s \circ i = 0, \qquad
 p \circ s = 0,
\]
where $i \colon H^*(\check{C}^\bullet) \hookrightarrow \check{C}^\bullet$
is the inclusion of cohomology and $p$ is the projection. The contracting
homotopy is purely algebraic: it acts by
$s(f_{i_0 \cdots i_q}) = f_{0 i_0 \cdots i_q}$ on \v{C}ech cochains and
involves no PDE solving. The SDR data $(i, p, s)$ interfaces directly
with the standard homotopy transfer theorem
\textup{(}HTT\textup{)},
transferring the $\Ainf$-structure from $\check{C}^\bullet(\cE nd(\cE))$
to its cohomology $\Ext^\bullet(\cE, \cE)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The \v{C}ech complex of a quasi-coherent sheaf on $\bP^4$ with respect
to the standard affine cover $\{U_i\}_{i=0}^4$ is acyclic in positive
\v{C}ech degree (Leray's theorem: each finite intersection
$U_{i_0} \cap \cdots \cap U_{i_q}$ is affine, and affine varieties
have vanishing higher cohomology for quasi-coherent sheaves).
The homotopy $s^q(f_{i_0 \cdots i_q}) := f_{0 i_0 \cdots i_q}$
satisfies $(\delta s + s \delta)(f) = f - f|_{U_0}$ for
$q \geq 1$, giving $\delta s + s \delta = \mathrm{Id} - i \circ p$.
The annihilation conditions $s^2 = 0$, $s \circ i = 0$,
$p \circ s = 0$ follow from the index~$0$ convention:
$s^2(f_{i_0 \cdots i_q}) = s(f_{0 i_0 \cdots i_q})
= f_{00 i_0 \cdots i_q} = 0$ by the alternating sign rule.
The same argument applies to the restriction $X_5 \hookrightarrow \bP^4$:
the induced cover $\{U_i \cap X_5\}$ consists of affine open sets
(hyperplane complements of a smooth hypersurface in projective space
are affine), so Leray's theorem applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Perturbative vs non-perturbative scope]
\label{rem:cech-perturbative-scope}
The \v{C}ech homotopy closes the analytic gap at the level of formal
(perturbative) deformations: it provides a chain-level contracting
homotopy that is algebraic and requires no PDE solving. The
non-perturbative convergence of the resulting chiral operations
(whether the formal series in the OPE coefficients converges to
define honest holomorphic functions) requires separate input from
the analytic completion programme (MC5,
the analytic sewing section of Vol~I). Specifically: the HTT
produces transferred operations $\mu_k^{\mathrm{ch}}$ whose
coefficients involve iterated applications of~$s$; each application
introduces rational functions on the intersections $U_{i_0} \cap
\cdots \cap U_{i_q}$, and the convergence of the resulting series
in the OPE variable $z$ is not guaranteed by the algebraic
construction alone. The perturbative statement is: the $\Ainf$
operations on $\Ext^\bullet(\cE, \cE)$ are well-defined as formal
power series. The non-perturbative statement (convergence to
holomorphic functions) requires estimates on the growth of the
\v{C}ech homotopy applied to OPE kernels.
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[\v{C}ech-HTT coefficient convergence]
\label{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $X$ be a smooth CY$_3$ admitting a finite Leray cover $\{U_\alpha\}_{\alpha=0}^{N-1}$ \textup{(}e.g., the standard affine cover for a projective hypersurface or complete intersection\textup{)}, and let $(i, p, s)$ be the algebraic \v{C}ech SDR data from Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}}. The HTT-transferred $\Ainf$-operations
\[
 \mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}(a_1, \ldots, a_k) \;=\; \sum_{T \in \mathrm{PBT}(k)} p \circ \mu_{|T|}^{\mathrm{loc}} \circ (s \circ \delta)^{(\cdot)} \circ i(a_1, \ldots, a_k),
\]
where the sum ranges over planar binary trees $T$ with $k$ leaves, satisfy the bound
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cech-htt-bound}
 \bigl\| \mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}} \bigr\| \;\leq\; \mathrm{Cat}(k-1) \cdot \| s \circ \delta \|^{k-2},
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{Cat}(n) = \binom{2n}{n}/(n+1)$ is the $n$-th Catalan number and $\|s \circ \delta\|$ is the operator norm of the composition on \v{C}ech cochains. For the standard affine cover of a degree-$d$ hypersurface in $\bP^{n+1}$, the bound $\| s \circ \delta \| \leq \max(n+2, d)$ holds.

The formal series $\sum_{k \geq 2} \mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}} \, z^{k-2}$ converges absolutely for
\[
 |z| \;<\; \frac{1}{4 \| s \circ \delta \|},
\]
since $\sum_{k \geq 0} \mathrm{Cat}(k) \, w^k = \frac{1 - \sqrt{1 - 4w}}{2w}$ has radius of convergence $\frac{1}{4}$ and the substitution $w = \|s \circ \delta\| \cdot z$ absorbs the operator norm.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The HTT tree formula~\textup{(}Kontsevich--Soibelman; Loday--Vallette, Algebraic Operads Theorem~10.3.1\textup{)} expresses $\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}$ as a sum over $\mathrm{Cat}(k-1)$ planar binary trees with $k$ leaves. Each internal edge of a tree contributes one application of the composition $s \circ \delta$, and a tree with $k$ leaves has exactly $k-2$ internal edges. The bound $\|\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}\| \leq \mathrm{Cat}(k-1) \cdot \|s \circ \delta\|^{k-2}$ follows by the triangle inequality (summing over trees) and submultiplicativity of the operator norm (bounding each internal edge). The projection~$p$ and inclusion~$i$ are contractions ($\|p\| \leq 1$, $\|i\| \leq 1$).

The operator norm estimate $\|s \circ \delta\| \leq \max(n+2, d)$ for the standard affine cover of a degree-$d$ hypersurface in~$\bP^{n+1}$: the \v{C}ech differential~$\delta$ at degree~$q$ has at most $q+2 \leq n+2$ terms (alternating restrictions), each of norm at most~$1$ on affine patches, while the prepend-$0$ homotopy~$s$ has $\|s\| = 1$. The degree~$d$ of the hypersurface equation enters through the complexity of the change-of-coordinates between patches, contributing at most a factor of~$d$.

The convergence of $\sum_{k \geq 2} \mathrm{Cat}(k-1) \cdot B^{k-2} \cdot |z|^{k-2}$ for $B|z| < \frac{1}{4}$ follows from the classical singularity analysis of the Catalan generating function $C(w) = (1 - \sqrt{1-4w})/(2w)$, which has a square-root branch point at $w = \frac{1}{4}$ and no other singularities.

For explicit examples: the quintic in $\bP^4$ has $N = 5$ affine patches and $d = 5$, giving $\|s \circ \delta\| \leq 5$ and convergence radius $\geq 1/20$; the bicubic complete intersection has $N = 6$, $d = 3$, radius $\geq 1/24$; local~$\bP^2$ has $N = 3$, $d = 3$, radius $\geq 1/12$.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Supplementary verification}: 64~tests in \texttt{cech\_htt\_convergence.py} confirm the Catalan bound and convergence radii for these three families.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Coefficient convergence vs OPE convergence]
\label{rem:coefficient-vs-ope-convergence}
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}} resolves the convergence question for the \emph{coefficient series}: the multilinear maps $\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}$ on $\Ext^\bullet(\cE, \cE)$ define a convergent power series in the spectral parameter~$z$, with radius bounded below by $1/(4\|s \circ \delta\|)$. This applies to \emph{all} smooth CY$_3$ with finite Leray covers, including non-formal geometries where $m_k \neq 0$ for $k \geq 3$.

The remaining analytic question is the \emph{OPE completion}: when the $\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}$ are promoted to chiral operations $\mu_k^{\mathrm{ch}}$ with poles in the insertion variables $z_{ij} = z_i - z_j$, the pole orders grow factorially ($\sim k!$), making the OPE-completed series Gevrey-$1$. The Gevrey class connects to the shadow tower: for class~$\mathbf{G}$ and~$\mathbf{L}$ algebras, the Borel transform converges; for class~$\mathbf{C}$, \'Ecalle resurgence applies; for class~$\mathbf{M}$, Borel summability is conjectural. The coefficient convergence proved here shows that the \v{C}ech-HTT construction produces \emph{well-defined formal deformations} at every order; the OPE completion question belongs to the MC5/sewing programme of Vol~I.

 \midrule

 Compact formal CY$_3$ & proved zero (pert.) & proved zero \\

\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Borel summability of the OPE completion for class~$\mathbf{M}$]
\label{prop:class-m-borel-summability}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
\index{Borel summability!class M}
\index{shadow tower!Borel summability}
Let $A$ be a chiral algebra of shadow class~$\mathbf{M}$ with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A) > 0$ and quartic shadow $S_4(A) > 0$. Then the OPE completion series $\sum_{k \geq 2} \mu_k^{\mathrm{ch}} z^k$ \textup{(}Gevrey-$1$\textup{)} is Borel summable: the Borel integral
\[
  \cS[F](z) \;=\; \frac{1}{z} \int_0^\infty e^{-u/z}\, \hat{F}(u)\, du,
  \qquad
  \hat{F}(u) = \sum_{k \geq 2} S_k\, u^k,
\]
converges absolutely for all $z > 0$, and the Borel sum has no Stokes ambiguity.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The argument proceeds in five steps: we identify the Borel transform with the shadow resolvent, prove positive-definiteness of the underlying quadratic on~$\bR$, establish the analytic continuation and growth bound, verify convergence of the Laplace integral, and confirm the absence of Stokes phenomena.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1: The shadow resolvent and its discriminant identity.}
By the Vol~I shadow tower construction, the OPE completion series for a class~$\mathbf{M}$ algebra $A$ with shadow data $(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}, \alpha, S_4, S_5, \ldots)$ has Borel transform determined by the \emph{shadow resolvent} $f(t) = \sqrt{Q_L(t)}$, where $Q_L(t) = q_0 + q_1 t + q_2 t^2$ is the shadow quadratic with coefficients
\[
  q_0 = 4\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2, \qquad q_1 = 12\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}\alpha, \qquad q_2 = 9\alpha^2 + 16\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4.
\]
The connection is that the shadow coefficients $S_k$ (which determine the $\Ainf$ corrections $\delta^{(k)}$) are precisely the Taylor coefficients of $\sqrt{Q_L(u)}$ at $u = 0$: expanding $\sqrt{q_0 + q_1 u + q_2 u^2} = \sqrt{q_0}\,(1 + (q_1 u + q_2 u^2)/(2q_0) - (q_1 u + q_2 u^2)^2/(8q_0^2) + \cdots)$ and collecting powers of $u$ recovers $S_2 = q_1/(2\sqrt{q_0})$, $S_3 = (4q_0 q_2 - q_1^2)/(8q_0^{3/2})$, etc.

The discriminant of $Q_L$ satisfies the identity
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:shadow-disc-identity}
  \Delta(Q_L) \;=\; q_1^2 - 4\,q_0\,q_2 \;=\; -256\,\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3\, S_4.
\end{equation}
\emph{Proof of~\eqref{eq:shadow-disc-identity}.}  Compute each term:
\begin{align*}
  q_1^2 &= (12\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \alpha)^2 = 144\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2 \alpha^2, \\
  4 q_0 q_2 &= 4 \cdot 4\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2 \cdot (9\alpha^2 + 16\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4) = 144\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2 \alpha^2 + 256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4.
\end{align*}
Subtracting: $q_1^2 - 4 q_0 q_2 = 144\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2 \alpha^2 - 144\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2 \alpha^2 - 256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 = -256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4$.  The $\alpha^2$ terms cancel exactly.  This is the key structural feature: the discriminant is \emph{independent of the cubic shadow~$\alpha$}.  Only $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ and $S_4$ control the Borel-plane singularity structure.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2: Positive-definiteness of $Q_L$ on~$\bR$.}
Under the hypothesis $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$ and $S_4 > 0$:
\begin{itemize}
  \item $\Delta(Q_L) = -256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 < 0$, so $Q_L$ has no real zeros.  Its zeros are the complex conjugate pair $t_\pm = t_c \pm i\delta$, where $t_c = -q_1/(2q_2)$ and $\delta = \sqrt{|\Delta|}/(2q_2) > 0$.
  \item The leading coefficient $q_2 = 9\alpha^2 + 16\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4 > 0$ (the second summand is strictly positive; the first is non-negative).
  \item A quadratic $q_2 t^2 + q_1 t + q_0$ with $q_2 > 0$ and $\Delta < 0$ is positive definite on~$\bR$: $Q_L(t) > 0$ for all $t \in \bR$.
\end{itemize}
Consequently, the shadow resolvent $\sqrt{Q_L(t)}$ is real-valued, smooth, and strictly positive on all of~$\bR$.  Its asymptotic behaviour is $\sqrt{Q_L(t)} = \sqrt{q_2}\,|t|\,(1 + O(t^{-1}))$ as $t \to \pm\infty$.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3: Analytic continuation along $\bR_+$ and growth bound.}
The Borel transform $\hat{F}(u) = \sum_{k \geq 2} S_k\, u^k$ is, by construction, the Taylor series of $\sqrt{Q_L(u)} - \sqrt{q_0}$ at $u = 0$ (subtracting the constant term to start at $O(u^2)$).  The function $\sqrt{Q_L(u)}$ is analytic in any simply connected domain avoiding the zeros $t_\pm$ of~$Q_L$.  Since $t_\pm \notin \bR$ (Step~2), the half-line $\bR_+ = [0, \infty)$ lies in such a domain.  Therefore $\hat{F}$ extends analytically along the entire positive real axis.

The growth bound: for $u \geq 1$, $|\hat{F}(u)| = |\sqrt{Q_L(u)} - \sqrt{q_0}| \leq \sqrt{Q_L(u)} + \sqrt{q_0} \leq \sqrt{q_2}\, u + O(1)$.  In particular, there exist constants $C, M > 0$ such that $|\hat{F}(u)| \leq C(1 + u)$ for all $u \geq 0$.  The growth is at most linear (polynomial of degree~$1$), which is far below the exponential threshold required for Borel summability.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Convergence of the Borel integral.}
For any $z > 0$, the Borel sum is
\[
  \cS[F](z) \;=\; \frac{1}{z} \int_0^\infty e^{-u/z}\, \hat{F}(u)\, du.
\]
By the growth bound of Step~3, the integrand satisfies $|e^{-u/z} \hat{F}(u)| \leq C\, e^{-u/z}(1 + u)$ for all $u \geq 0$.  The comparison integral converges:
\[
  \frac{1}{z}\int_0^\infty e^{-u/z}(1 + u)\, du \;=\; \frac{1}{z}\bigl(z + z^2\bigr) \;=\; 1 + z \;<\; \infty.
\]
Therefore $\cS[F](z)$ converges absolutely for every $z > 0$.  The function $\cS[F]$ is smooth on $(0, \infty)$ and is the unique Borel sum of the Gevrey-$1$ series $\sum_{k \geq 2} S_k k!\, z^k$: by Watson's lemma, the asymptotic expansion of $\cS[F](z)$ as $z \to 0^+$ recovers the original formal series.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 5: Absence of Stokes ambiguity.}
Stokes phenomena arise when the Borel contour $[0, \infty)$ passes through or near a singularity of $\hat{F}$ in the Borel plane.  The singularities of $\hat{F}(u) = \sqrt{Q_L(u)} - \sqrt{q_0}$ are the square-root branch points at the zeros $t_\pm = t_c \pm i\delta$ of $Q_L$.  Since $\delta > 0$ (Step~2), these branch points have nonzero imaginary part and lie strictly off the real axis.  Therefore:
\begin{itemize}
  \item The Borel contour $[0, \infty) \subset \bR$ does not pass through any singularity of $\hat{F}$.
  \item No contour deformation (and hence no choice of lateral Borel sum) is needed.
  \item The Borel sum $\cS[F](z)$ is uniquely defined for all $z > 0$.
\end{itemize}
This completes the proof that the OPE completion is Borel summable with no Stokes ambiguity.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Scope.} The condition $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $S_4 > 0$ holds for the Virasoro sector at any $c > 0$ (where $S_4 = 10/(c(5c+22)) > 0$, Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4}).  For $d = 2$ CY categories: unconditional (CY-A$_2$ proved).  For $d = 3$: the chiral algebra is provided by the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}); given $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$ and $S_4 > 0$ (verified for the Virasoro sector of all independently known CY$_3$ chiral algebras), Borel summability follows.  The regime $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} < 0$, $S_4 < 0$ is also Borel summable ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 > 0$ when both are negative).  The dangerous regime $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 < 0$ does not arise for any of the $13$ standard CY geometries.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Supplementary verification}: $54$ tests in \texttt{class\_m\_borel\_summation.py} confirm the discriminant identity~\eqref{eq:shadow-disc-identity} for $125$ parameter triples, the landscape analysis across $13$ standard examples, adversarial boundary cases, and numerical Borel sum convergence.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Non-perturbative shadow completion via resurgence]
\label{conj:shadow-resurgence}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
Let $A$ be a chiral algebra of shadow class~$\mathbf{M}$ with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$ and quartic shadow $S_4 > 0$.  The Borel transform $\hat{F}(u) = \sqrt{Q_L(u)}$ of the OPE completion \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability})} has square-root branch points at the zeros $\omega_\pm$ of the shadow quadratic $Q_L$, and the full non-perturbative shadow function admits a trans-series expansion:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:shadow-trans-series}
  S^{\mathrm{full}}(\epsilon) \;=\; S^{\mathrm{pert}}(\epsilon) \;+\; \sum_{\substack{n_+, n_- \geq 0 \\ (n_+, n_-) \neq (0,0)}} C_+^{n_+} C_-^{n_-}\, e^{-(n_+ \omega_+ + n_- \omega_-)/\epsilon}\, S^{(n_+, n_-)}(\epsilon),
\end{equation}
where $C_\pm$ are trans-series parameters \textup{(}Stokes constants\textup{)}, $\omega_\pm = t_c \pm i\delta$ with $t_c = -q_1/(2q_2) < 0$ are complex conjugate instanton actions, and $S^{(n_+, n_-)}(\epsilon)$ are perturbative tails determined by iterated alien derivatives.  The following properties hold:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{Borel plane structure}: the singularities $\omega_\pm$ lie in the left half-plane \textup{(}since $t_c < 0$ when $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $\alpha > 0$\textup{)}, and are of square-root type with monodromy $-1$.
  \item \emph{Simple resurgence}: the shadow resolvent $\sqrt{Q_L}$ is \emph{simple resurgent} \textup{(}Ecalle\textup{)}: the alien derivative at $\omega_+$ satisfies $\Delta_{\omega_+} S^{\mathrm{pert}} = \mathcal{S}_{\omega_+} \cdot S^{(1,0)}$ with Stokes constant $\mathcal{S}_{\omega_+} = -i/\sqrt{Q_L'(\omega_+)}$, and the tower of alien derivatives terminates at each instanton level.
  \item \emph{Stokes automorphism}: the Stokes automorphism $\underline{\mathfrak{S}}_\theta$ crossing the Stokes line at angle $\theta = \arg(\omega_+)$ is upper-triangular in the instanton-number filtration, and its off-diagonal entries are the Stokes constants.
  \item \emph{Resurgence--wall-crossing correspondence} \textup{(}at $d = 3$ via the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution, Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}: the Stokes automorphism of the shadow trans-series is the Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing automorphism, with instanton actions $|\omega_\pm|$ identified with BPS central charges $|Z(\gamma)|$ and Stokes constants identified with BPS indices $\Omega(\gamma)$.
\end{enumerate}
At $d = 2$ \textup{(}CY-A$_2$ proved\textup{)}: the shadow tower for generic K3 is finite \textup{(}class~G\textup{)}, and resurgence is trivial.  At enhanced symmetry points where the shadow class jumps to~M, parts~\textup{(i)--(iii)} are unconditional.  Part~\textup{(iv)} for $K3 \times E$ proceeds via the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}.

For the Virasoro at $c = 1$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1/2$, $\alpha = 2$, $S_4 = 10/27$, giving instanton actions at $\omega_\pm = -81/98 \pm i\delta$ with $\delta = \sqrt{320/27} \cdot 27/392$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $73$~tests in \texttt{shadow\_resurgence\_nonpert.py}, covering the Borel plane singularity analysis for $5$ standard examples, alien derivative computation and Stokes constant verification, trans-series construction through instanton level $2$, Stokes automorphism upper-triangularity and discontinuity formula, and wall-crossing correspondence at the level of instanton actions.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{theorem}[Stokes automorphism = KS wall-crossing: conifold case]
\label{thm:stokes-ks-conifold}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For the resolved conifold $X = \cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1) \to \bP^1$ with charge lattice $\Gamma = \bZ^2$ and antisymmetric Euler pairing $\langle \gamma_1, \gamma_2 \rangle = 1$, the Stokes automorphism of the resurgent DT partition function IS the Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing automorphism.  Specifically:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{Stokes line = wall}.  The single Stokes line at $\theta = \arg(\omega)$ in the Borel plane is the single wall of marginal stability $\cW(\gamma_1, \gamma_2)$ where $\phi_\sigma(\gamma_1) = \phi_\sigma(\gamma_2)$.
  \item \emph{Trans-series sectors = BPS charge sectors}.  The instanton action $|\omega| = |Z(\gamma_1 + \gamma_2)|$ is the BPS mass of the bound state; the trans-series sectors $(n_+, n_-)$ are the charge sectors $(n_1, n_2)$.
  \item \emph{Stokes matrix = KS symplectomorphism}.  The Stokes matrix $\underline{\mathfrak{S}}_\theta$ and the KS symplectomorphism $\cA_\cW$ are both upper-triangular in the charge filtration, with matching off-diagonal entry $\cS_\omega = \Omega(\gamma_1 + \gamma_2) = -1$.
  \item \emph{Pentagon identity = Stokes factorization}.  The Faddeev--Kashaev pentagon identity $E(X)E(Y) = E(Y)E(XY)E(X)$ in the quantum torus is simultaneously the KS wall-crossing formula \textup{(}2-particle chamber $\leftrightarrow$ 3-particle chamber\textup{)} and the Stokes factorization identity \textup{(}Borel sum before the Stokes line $=$ Borel sum after\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The conifold has finitely many BPS states: $\Omega(\gamma_1) = \Omega(\gamma_2) = -1$ in Chamber~I, plus $\Omega(\gamma_1 + \gamma_2) = -1$ in Chamber~II.  The pentagon identity (verified exactly in the quantum torus algebra at each charge sector and each $q$-power; \texttt{conifold\_wall\_crossing.py}) equates the two chamber orderings.  By Bridgeland~\cite{Bridgeland2019RH} (Theorem~1.1), the Stokes data of the Riemann--Hilbert problem associated to a BPS structure IS the collection of DT invariants $\Omega(\gamma)$.  For the conifold, the RH problem has a single Stokes ray, the BPS structure has a single wall, and the pentagon identity is exact.  The five structural properties (ray indexing, factorization, upper-triangularity, integer data, gauge invariance) match by direct verification.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Stokes automorphism = KS wall-crossing: $K3 \times E$]
\label{conj:stokes-ks-k3e}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
For $K3 \times E$ with the BKM root system of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$, the Stokes automorphism of the shadow trans-series \textup{(}Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:shadow-resurgence})} is the KS wall-crossing automorphism.  The identification is stratified by discriminant:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccl@{}}
\textbf{Discriminant} & \textbf{Wall type} & \textbf{Stokes type} & \textbf{Identification status} \\ \hline
$D = -1$ & Real root (flop) & Real axis & THEOREM \textup{(}local conifold\textup{)} \\
$D = 0$ & Lightlike & Imaginary axis & Conjectural \textup{(}CY-A$_3$\textup{)} \\
$D > 0$ & Imaginary (bound state) & Conjugate pair & Conjectural \textup{(}CY-A$_3$\textup{)}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
At each real root wall ($D = -1$), the local model is the conifold \textup{(}$\langle \gamma_1, \gamma_2 \rangle = 1$, pentagon identity\textup{)}, so the identification is a theorem locally.  The BKM root multiplicities $c(D)$ from $\phi_{0,1}$ simultaneously count BPS states, walls, and Stokes lines at each discriminant.  The global stitching of infinitely many local identifications proceeds via the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}.

The Bridgeland Riemann--Hilbert bridge \textup{(}\cite{Bridgeland2019RH}\textup{)} provides the chain:
\[
  \text{Shadow tower} \xrightarrow{\text{Borel}} \text{Borel plane} \xrightarrow{\text{singularities}} \text{Stokes rays} \xrightarrow{\text{Bridgeland RH}} \text{DT invariants} \xrightarrow{\text{KS}} \text{Wall-crossing.}
\]
\end{conjecture}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $84$~tests in \texttt{stokes\_wallcrossing\_identification.py}, covering the five-point structural check (ray indexing, factorization, upper-triangularity, integer data, gauge invariance), conifold pentagon $=$ Stokes factorization, conifold Stokes matrix $=$ KS matrix, $K3 \times E$ discriminant-stratified identification, real root $=$ mini-conifold, imaginary root Stokes structure, Bridgeland RH bridge, and cross-checks with \texttt{shadow\_resurgence\_nonpert.py}, \texttt{k3e\_wall\_crossing\_shadow.py}, and \texttt{conifold\_wall\_crossing.py}.


\subsection{Hopf fibration decomposition of the chain-level obstruction}
\label{subsec:hopf-fibration-decomposition}

The topological vanishing (Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}) and the \v{C}ech contracting homotopy (Theorem~\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}) leave open one precise question: the $\Ainf$-compatibility of the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing trivialization. The Hopf fibration $S^1 \to S^3 \to S^2$ decomposes this question into three independent components, two of which are resolved.

\begin{proposition}[Hopf fibration decomposition of the $\bS^3$-framing obstruction]
\label{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $\cC$ be a smooth proper CY$_3$ category with cyclic $\Ainf$-structure $(A, \{\mu_n\}, \langle -,- \rangle)$. The Connes hierarchy $B^{(0)}, B^{(1)}, B^{(2)}, B^{(3)}$ on the cyclic bar complex $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)$ \textup{(}where $B^{(0)} = B$ is the Connes operator and $B^{(3)} = F$ is the $\bS^3$-framing map\textup{)} satisfies the total nilpotence relation
\[
 \sum_{i+j=3} B^{(i)} \circ B^{(j)} = 0,
\]
which rearranges to
\[
 [B, F] \;=\; -[B^{(1)}, B^{(2)}].
\]
The commutator $[B^{(1)}, B^{(2)}]$ encodes the nontriviality of the Hopf fibration: $B^{(1)}$ is the $S^1$-fiber contribution \textup{(}the intermediate Connes-hierarchy map from $\Omega^1$ of the holomorphic volume form\textup{)}, $B^{(2)}$ is the $S^2$-base contribution \textup{(}the CY$_2$ part of the framing, proved by CY-A$_2$\textup{)}, and their commutator is the Euler class twist.

The chain-level $\bS^3$-framing obstruction decomposes as
\[
 \mathrm{Obs}(\cC) \;=\; \mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}}(\cC) + \mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf}(\cC) + \mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}(\cC),
\]
where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$ universally \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}}: $\pi_3(B\Sp) = \pi_3(BU) = 0$\textup{)}.
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ universally \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat}} below: Costello's open-closed TCFT framework gives $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ where $b = \sum_k b_k$ is the total $\Ainf$-Hochschild differential\textup{)}.
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ vanishes for toric CY$_3$ \textup{(}$T^3$-equivariant hCS trivialization\textup{)} and perturbatively for compact CY$_3$ with \v{C}ech homotopy \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}}\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The nilpotence relation $\sum_{i+j=3} B^{(i)} B^{(j)} = 0$ is the defining property of the Connes hierarchy for CY$_3$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:cyclic-ainf}; Costello~\cite{Costello2005TCFT}, Kontsevich--Soibelman~\cite{KontsevichSoibelman2009}). The four terms are $B^{(0)}B^{(3)} + B^{(1)}B^{(2)} + B^{(2)}B^{(1)} + B^{(3)}B^{(0)} = 0$, which is $[B,F] + [B^{(1)},B^{(2)}] = 0$.

For~(a): Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}.

For~(b): Proposition~\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat} below.

For~(c): the BV trivialization via holomorphic Chern--Simons provides $\delta_{\BV}(\mathrm{CS}) = \int \Omega \wedge F_A$. For toric CY$_3$, the $T^3$-action makes CS $\Eone$-equivariant. For compact CY$_3$, the \v{C}ech contracting homotopy (Theorem~\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}) provides a formal-power-series trivialization; the perturbative convergence is guaranteed by the algebraic structure, and the non-perturbative convergence is the single remaining open question.
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[Cyclic $\Ainf$-framing compatibility via Costello's TCFT]
\label{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $(A, \{\mu_n\}, \langle -,- \rangle)$ be a cyclic $\Ainf$-algebra of CY dimension~$d$, let $b = \sum_{k \geq 1} b_k$ be the $\Ainf$-Hochschild differential on $C_\bullet(A)$, and let $B^{(2)} \colon C_n(A) \to C_{n-2}(A)$ be the contraction operator determined by the CY pairing.  Then
\[
 \{b, B^{(2)}\} \;=\; b \circ B^{(2)} + B^{(2)} \circ b \;=\; 0.
\]
\textbf{Corrected claim} \textup{(}see Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:adversarial-audit-cyclic-ainf}}\textup{)}: the vanishing is for the \emph{total} differential $b = \sum_k b_k$.  Individual $\{b_k, B^{(2)}\}$ need NOT vanish for $k \geq 3$; only their sum does.  For formal algebras ($\mu_k = 0$ for $k \geq 3$): $b = b_2$ and $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ by the Frobenius condition.  For non-formal algebras ($\mu_3 \neq 0$): $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$ on $\mathrm{CC}_4$ \textup{(}\texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $54$~tests: $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}([a|a|a|a|b]) = 2\alpha \cdot [b]$ for $\mu_3(a,a,a) = \alpha b$\textup{)}, but the cross-degree cancellation $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\} + \{b_3, B^{(2)}\} + \cdots = 0$ is enforced by the $\Ainf$-Stasheff relations, packaged operadically as $\partial^2 = 0$ in Costello's TCFT.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Costello~\cite{Costello2005TCFT} (Theorem~A; arXiv:math/0412149), a cyclic $\Ainf$-algebra of CY dimension~$d$ is equivalent to an open TCFT.  The cyclic pairing extends this to an open-closed TCFT (ibid., Section~5; Costello~\cite{Costello2007Ainfty}; arXiv:0706.1959) whose closed sector is the Hochschild chain complex $C_\bullet(A)$.  Under this equivalence:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item the Hochschild differential $b = \sum_k b_k$ corresponds to codimension-$1$ boundary strata of the moduli of bordered surfaces (strip-like degenerations);
 \item the contraction $B^{(2)}$ corresponds to the genus-change operation (identifying two boundary marked points via the CY pairing to create a node).
\end{enumerate}
Both $b$ and $B^{(2)}$ are images of the boundary operator $\partial$ in the chain complex $C_\bullet(\cM)$ of the moduli operad~$\cM$.  The key point is that $b$ and $B^{(2)}$ correspond to \emph{geometrically distinct} boundary strata (strip-like degenerations and genus-change nodes respectively) that parametrise a specific compact $1$-dimensional moduli space $\overline{\cM}_{b, B^{(2)}}$ whose \emph{only} boundary components are the two compositions $b \circ B^{(2)}$ and $B^{(2)} \circ b$.  No other Connes-hierarchy operations ($B^{(0)}$, $B^{(1)}$, $B^{(3)}$) appear as boundary types of this moduli space, because the surface types producing $B^{(i)}$ for $i \neq 2$ involve different topological operations (rotation, interior contraction, cap product) that cannot arise as degenerations of the strip-plus-node family.  The signed boundary count of the compact $1$-manifold $\overline{\cM}_{b, B^{(2)}}$ is zero, giving $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Distinction from the mixed complex axiom.}
The mixed complex axiom $[b, B_{\mathrm{total}}] = 0$ where $B_{\mathrm{total}} = \sum_{i=0}^{d} B^{(i)}$ is a \emph{weaker} statement: it implies $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = -\{b, B^{(0)}\} - \{b, B^{(1)}\} - \{b, B^{(3)}\}$ but does NOT by itself imply $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$.  The vanishing $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ is a \emph{stronger} consequence of the operadic structure, using the specific moduli-space factorisation above.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Non-adjacent contractions resolved.}
The moduli space treats all boundary marked points democratically.  When $B^{(2)}$ contracts points $p_i$ and $p_j$, their positions relative to the $b_k$-insertion are geometric data encoded by the full moduli, not merely by the cyclic ordering.  The $\partial^2 = 0$ argument applies uniformly to all pairs $(i,j)$, whether or not they straddle the $b_k$-block.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Why individual $\{b_k, B^{(2)}\}$ can be nonzero.}
The explicit computation (\texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $54$~tests) confirms $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}([a|a|a|a|b]) = 2\alpha \cdot [b] \neq 0$ for the minimal non-formal model.  This is not a counterexample: the $b_2$-contribution to $\{b, B^{(2)}\}$ on the same element provides the cancelling term, as guaranteed by Costello's theorem.  The mechanism: the Stasheff relations at degree $n = 4$ couple $\mu_2$ and $\mu_3$, forcing the residue of $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}$ to equal $-\{b_2, B^{(2)}\}$ at every chain element.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $43$~tests in \texttt{operadic\_tcft\_mk\_b2\_engine.py} (operadic proof structure, algebra data, gap closure); $54$~tests in \texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py} (chain-level $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$, confirming the individual non-vanishing that the total cancels); $40$~tests in \texttt{stasheff\_cancellation\_obs\_ainf.py} (bidegree decomposition, weight-$s$ identities, cross-hierarchy cancellation partners, formal algebra axiom verification).
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[Resolution via Costello's TCFT]
\label{rem:adversarial-audit-cyclic-ainf}
\textsc{[Status: RESOLVED.  $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ universally by Costello's operadic TCFT.]}

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{History of the claim.}  Four successive arguments for $\Ainf$-framing compatibility:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(P\arabic*)}]
 \item \emph{Cyclic invariance argument} (original, retracted).  Claimed $[m_k, B^{(2)}] = 0$ for each $k$ from cyclic invariance alone.  \textbf{Gap}: cyclic invariance controls adjacent contractions but not ``non-adjacent'' terms.
 \item \emph{Bidegree decomposition} (retracted).  Claimed $[b, B^{\mathrm{full}}] = 0$ decomposes by degree into $[m_k, B^{(j)}] = 0$ for each $(k,j)$.  \textbf{Flaw}: the decomposition by degree does not preserve the vanishing; individual $\{b_k, B^{(2)}\}$ can be nonzero.
 \item \emph{Tsygan formality argument} (retracted).  \textbf{Flaw}: Tsygan formality applies to $(b, B)$ but not to the Connes hierarchy operators $B^{(j)}$ for $j \geq 1$.
 \item \textbf{Costello operadic TCFT} (this proof, \textbf{correct}).  The claim is $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ for the \emph{total} differential $b = \sum_k b_k$, not for individual $b_k$.  This follows from $\partial^2 = 0$ in the chain complex of Costello's open-closed TCFT moduli operad.
\end{enumerate}

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{The key correction.}
The original claim ``$[m_k, B^{(2)}] = 0$ for all $k \geq 3$'' is \textbf{false} for non-formal algebras.  The explicit computation (\texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $54$~tests) confirms:
\[
  \{b_3, B^{(2)}\}([a \mid a \mid a \mid a \mid b]) \;=\; 2\alpha \cdot [b] \;\neq\; 0
\]
on the minimal non-formal model with $\mu_3(a,a,a) = \alpha b$.  However, this does NOT imply $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} \neq 0$.  The correct claim is $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = \sum_k \{b_k, B^{(2)}\} = 0$: the nonzero $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}$ is \emph{exactly cancelled} by $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\}$ on the same element.  The cancellation is enforced by the Stasheff $\Ainf$-relations at degree $n = 4$, which couple $\mu_2$ and $\mu_3$.  Costello's TCFT packages this cross-degree cancellation as the single identity $\partial^2 = 0$.

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{Impact on the programme.}
$\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ \emph{universally}, for all cyclic $\Ainf$-algebras (formal or not).  CY-A$_3$ reverts to a \emph{single} remaining open obstruction: $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ (non-perturbative convergence).  The landscape is restored to the structure of Proposition~\ref{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition}.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $43$~tests in \texttt{operadic\_tcft\_mk\_b2\_engine.py} (operadic proof structure, local~$\bP^2$ algebra, gap closure); $54$~tests in \texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py} (chain-level individual $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$); $67$~tests in \texttt{hopf\_fibration\_s3\_framing.py}; $40$~tests in \texttt{stasheff\_cancellation\_obs\_ainf.py} (independent verification: bidegree decomposition of $[b,B]=0$ by total weight $s = k+i$, weight-$s$ identities for CY$_3$, cross-hierarchy cancellation partners, formal algebra axiom checks).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[CY-A$_3$ obstruction landscape]
\label{rem:hopf-reduction}
The Hopf fibration decomposition (Proposition~\ref{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition}) reduces CY-A$_3$ from three independent obstructions to one.  $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$ universally ($\pi_3(B\Sp) = 0$).  $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ universally by Costello's TCFT (Proposition~\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat}: $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ for the total $\Ainf$-Hochschild differential; individual $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$ for non-formal algebras, cancelled cross-degree by $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\}$).  Only $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ remains:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY$_3$} & $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}}$ & $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf}$ & $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ & \textbf{Status} \\
 \midrule
 $\C^3$ & $0$ & $0$ (formal) & $0$ (toric) & Fully resolved \\
 Conifold & $0$ & $0$ (formal) & $0$ (toric) & Fully resolved \\
 Local $\bP^2$ & $0$ & $0$ (TCFT) & $0$ (toric) & Fully resolved \\
 Quintic & $0$ & $0$ (TCFT) & $0$ (pert.) & Perturbatively resolved \\
 $K3 \times E$ & $0$ & $0$ (formal) & cond.\ on Step~5 & Kummer route \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ entry for local~$\bP^2$ is the new content: despite $\mu_3 \neq 0$ (non-formality) and despite $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$ at the chain level, the \emph{total} commutator $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ by Costello's operadic TCFT.
