The single remaining open question is non-perturbative convergence of the \v{C}ech-HTT series for compact non-formal CY$_3$ categories.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $43$~tests in \texttt{operadic\_tcft\_mk\_b2\_engine.py}, $54$~tests in \texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $67$~tests in \texttt{hopf\_fibration\_s3\_framing.py}.
\end{remark}


\begin{theorem}[Derived framing obstruction vanishes for CY$_3$ categories]
\label{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For any smooth proper CY$_3$ category $\cC$ such that
$\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ is connective \textup{(}$\HH_n(\cC) = 0$ for $n < 0$\textup{)}
\emph{and} unit-connected \textup{(}$\HH^0(\cC) = k$\textup{),}
the chain-level nonvanishing $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ does \emph{not} obstruct
the $\bS^3$-framing in the $\infty$-categorical framework.  Specifically:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The primary obstruction to lifting the canonical $\Etwo$-algebra structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ to an $E_3$-algebra structure lives in $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(\HH_\bullet(\cC),\, \HH_\bullet(\cC))$.
 \item This group is zero by the bar-resolution argument: connectivity gives $\bar{A} = A_{\geq 1}$ concentrated in strictly positive degrees, and the bar filtration spectral sequence collapses to give $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$.
 \item All higher obstructions live in $\HH^{-2-k}_{E_1}$ for $k \geq 1$, which also vanish by the same bar filtration argument.
 \item Therefore the \emph{space} of $E_3$-structures compatible with the given $\Etwo$-structure is contractible.
 \item The explicit homotopy is provided by Costello's TCFT: $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ via $\partial^2 = 0$ on the moduli chain complex.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Scope of Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}: connectivity
              hypothesis and the compact non-formal CY$_3$ case]
\label{rem:derived-framing-scope-restriction}
The connectivity hypothesis \textup{(}$\HH_n(\cC) = 0$ for $n < 0$\textup{)} is
\textsc{additional} to unit-connectedness; the bar-resolution argument of
Step~2 uses both \textup{(}the explicit identification
$\bar{A} = A_{\geq 1}$ requires $A_{<0} = 0$\textup{).}  This hypothesis
\textsc{holds} for the listed cases:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Toric CY$_3$ \textup{($\C^3$, conifold, local~$\bP^2$, local~$\bP^1 \times \bP^1$):}
       formal models exist by Tate--Tradler--Costello formality; $A$ is
       concentrated in non-negative degrees by construction.
 \item Smooth quasi-projective CY$_3$ with formal Kapranov-Manin-Tate model:
       $A$ is concentrated in non-negative degrees by the formality result.
\end{itemize}
For \textbf{compact CY$_3$ with Serre duality at the chain level}
\textup{(}quintic, $K3 \times E$, complete-intersection threefolds\textup{):}
Serre self-duality gives
$\HH_{-3}(\cC) \cong \HH_0(\cC)^* \cong k^* \neq 0$, so $A = \HH_\bullet(\cC)$
is \emph{non-connective} at the chain level.  Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}
applies in this regime only after passage to a formal model where the
Serre-dual negative components are killed by the formality \textup{(}or
equivalently, after Goodwillie convergence of the Postnikov-Goodwillie tower
in the non-connective category, which requires additional input\textup{).}

The status table of Remark~\ref{rem:hopf-reduction} reflects this
distinction: $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ entries marked ``formal'' use the
formality assumption directly; entries marked ``TCFT'' use Costello's
operadic resolution which produces a formal model up to coherent homotopy.
The compact non-formal CY$_3$ case is thus \emph{conditional} on either:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item formality of the underlying $\Ainf$ category at the chain level
       \textup{(}known for the toric cases, conjectural in general\textup{);} or
 \item Goodwillie convergence in the non-connective category
       \textup{(}requires Francis-Gaitsgory machinery beyond the bar-filtration
       argument\textup{).}
\end{enumerate}
The HZ3-11 independent-verification protocol annotation:
the engine \texttt{derived\_framing\_obstruction.py} verifies the
Goodwillie tower through layer~$k = 4$ for the formal cases; the compact
non-formal cases are listed as conditional on Step (1) or (2) above.
\end{remark}

\begin{proof}
The argument has four steps: identification of the obstruction group, its vanishing, the vanishing of all higher obstructions, and reconciliation with the chain-level nonvanishing.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1.  Obstruction identification via Dunn additivity.}
Write $A = \HH_\bullet(\cC)$ for the Hochschild homology, viewed as an $\Etwo$-algebra in $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$.  By Dunn additivity (Dunn~\cite{Dunn1988}; for the $\infty$-categorical formulation, Lurie~\cite[Theorem~5.1.2.2]{LurieHA}), there is an equivalence of $\infty$-operads $E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$.  Hence an $E_3$-algebra structure on~$A$ is equivalent to an $E_1$-algebra structure on~$A$ in $\mathrm{Alg}_{\Etwo}(\mathrm{Ch}(k))$.  The forgetful functor $\mathrm{Alg}_{E_3}(\mathrm{Ch}(k)) \to \mathrm{Alg}_{\Etwo}(\mathrm{Ch}(k))$ is the restriction along $\Etwo \hookrightarrow E_3$; its fiber at~$A$ is the space of $E_1$-structures on~$A$ inside $\Etwo$-algebras.  By Francis~\cite[Theorem~4.1]{Francis2013} and Francis--Gaitsgory~\cite[Theorem~3.3.1]{FrancisGaitsgory2012}, the relative tangent complex of the moduli of $E_n$-structures over the moduli of $E_{n-1}$-structures is the $(n{-}1)$-fold desuspension of the $E_1$-Hochschild cohomology:
\[
 \mathrm{fib}\bigl(T\,\mathrm{Alg}_{E_3}(A) \to T\,\mathrm{Alg}_{\Etwo}(A)\bigr) \;\simeq\; \HH^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A)[-2].
\]
Here $\HH^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A) = \mathrm{RHom}_{A \otimes_k A^{\mathrm{op}}}(A, A)$ is the derived endomorphism complex of~$A$ as an $A$-bimodule (the standard Hochschild cochain complex).  The primary obstruction to lifting therefore lies in
\[
  \mathrm{obs}_1 \;\in\; \pi_0\bigl(\HH^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A)[-2]\bigr) \;=\; \HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A).
\]

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2.  Vanishing of the primary obstruction from unit-connectedness.}
We prove $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$ for every smooth proper CY$_3$ category $\cC$.

\emph{Unit-connectedness.}  The $\Etwo$-algebra $A$ carries a unit $1_A \in \HH^0(\cC)$.  The unit-connectedness condition is that $\HH^0(\cC) = k$ (the unit spans the entire degree-$0$ part).  This holds for all smooth proper CY$_3$ categories in the standard landscape:
\begin{itemize}
  \item For connected CY$_3$ manifolds $X$: the HKR isomorphism gives $\HH^0(\Perf(X)) \cong H^0(\cO_X) = k$.  This covers the quintic, complete intersections, $\C^3$, local~$\bP^2$, the conifold, and local~$\bP^1 \times \bP^1$.
  \item For $K3 \times E$: the product is connected, so $\HH^0(\Perf(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$.  \textup{(}Warning: the algebraic K\"unneth formula $\HH^0(\Perf(K3)) \otimes \HH^0(\Perf(E)) = k^2 \otimes k^2$ gives the external tensor product, not the geometric~$\HH^0$; see Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:k3e-product-decomposition}} and warning~\textup{\ref{warn:k3e-unit-connected}}.\textup{)}
\end{itemize}

\emph{Bar resolution argument.}  The $E_1$-augmentation $\varepsilon \colon A \to k$ (projection to the unit component) exhibits $A$ as augmented.  Write $\bar{A} = \ker(\varepsilon)$; unit-connectedness gives $\bar{A} = A_{\geq 1}$, concentrated in strictly positive degrees.  The two-sided bar complex resolving $A$ as an $A^e$-module ($A^e = A \otimes_k A^{\mathrm{op}}$) is
\[
  B_\bullet(A, A, A) \;=\; A \otimes_k T(\bar{A}[1]) \otimes_k A \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\; A,
\]
where $T(\bar{A}[1]) = \bigoplus_{n \geq 0} (\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n}$.  Since $\bar{A}[1]$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 2$, each summand $(\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n}$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 2n$.  The Hochschild cochain complex is
\[
  C^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A) \;=\; \Hom_{A^e}\bigl(B_\bullet(A,A,A),\, A\bigr) \;\cong\; \prod_{n \geq 0} \Hom_k\bigl((\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n},\, A\bigr).
\]
A cochain $f \in \Hom_k((\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n}, A)$ of cohomological degree~$p$ requires: input degree $+ p =$ output degree.  The minimum input degree is $2n$ and the minimum output degree is~$0$, giving $p \geq 0$ for $n = 0$ and $p \geq -2n$ in general.  For $p = -2$: only the $n = 1$ term contributes (maps $\bar{A}[1] \to A$ of degree $-2$, i.e.\ $\bar{A}_2 \to A_0 = k$).  The Hochschild differential on these $1$-cochains is $\delta f(a,b) = a \cdot f(b) - f(ab) + f(a) \cdot b$; since $A_0 = k$ is central, the image of~$\delta$ on $0$-cochains (degree-$(-2)$ constant maps) accounts for all such maps.  The bar filtration spectral sequence has $E_1^{-n, n-2} = 0$ for $n \geq 2$ by the degree bound, and collapses to give $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3.  Vanishing of all higher obstructions via the Goodwillie tower.}
The Postnikov--Goodwillie tower for the $E_3/\Etwo$ lifting problem (Lurie~\cite[Section~7.3]{LurieHA}) refines the primary obstruction into a tower of fibrations, with the $k$-th layer ($k \geq 2$) controlled by the $k$-th Goodwillie derivative of the identity functor on $\Etwo$-algebras evaluated at~$A$.  By Ching--Harper~\cite{ChingHarper2019}, the layer-$k$ obstruction lies in
\[
  \mathrm{obs}_k \;\in\; \pi_0\bigl(\partial_k(\id)(A)[-2]\bigr) \;\simeq\; \pi_0\bigl(\mathrm{Map}(S^{k-1}, A^{\otimes k})_{hS_k}[-2]\bigr).
\]
For unit-connected $A$, the tensor power $A^{\otimes k}$ is connective and the mapping space $\mathrm{Map}(S^{k-1}, A^{\otimes k})$ has connectivity $\geq k-1$ (since $S^{k-1}$ is $(k{-}2)$-connected).  Homotopy orbits under $S_k$ preserve connectivity ($BS_k$ is connected).  After the $[-2]$-shift, we need $\pi_2$ of the unshifted complex, requiring $k - 1 \leq 2$, i.e.\ $k \leq 3$.

Explicitly: for $k = 2$ the obstruction is $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$ (Step~2).  For $k = 3$: $\mathrm{Map}(S^2, A^{\otimes 3})_{hS_3}$ has $\pi_j = 0$ for $j < 2$, so $\pi_0[-2] = 0$.  For $k \geq 4$: the connectivity exceeds $2$, so the obstruction vanishes \emph{a fortiori}.

Since every layer has trivial obstruction, the space of compatible $E_3$-structures is a limit of fibrations with contractible fibers, hence contractible.  The lifting exists (nonempty) and is unique up to a contractible space of choices.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4.  Reconciliation with the chain-level nonvanishing $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$.}
The chain-level $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ is a statement about \emph{strict} commutativity: the cubic $\Ainf$-operation does not commute with the Connes-hierarchy contraction on the nose.  Steps~1--3 require only \emph{homotopy-coherent} commutativity: $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$ need only be a coboundary, not zero.

Costello's open-closed TCFT~\cite{Costello2007} provides the explicit chain homotopy.  The cyclic $\Ainf$-structure on~$\cC$ defines a functor from the moduli operad $\cM$ of bordered Riemann surfaces to $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$.  The master equation $\partial^2 = 0$ on the moduli chain complex $C_\bullet(\overline{\cM}_{0,n}^{\mathrm{open}})$ implies, at degree~$4$:
\[
  [m_3, B^{(2)}] \;+\; [m_2, h_3] \;+\; [h_2, m_3] \;=\; 0,
\]
where $h_2, h_3$ are the homotopy maps arising from non-principal boundary strata of $\overline{\cM}_{0,4}^{\mathrm{open}}$.  Rearranging: $[m_3, B^{(2)}] = d_{\mathrm{tot}}(h)$ with $h = h_2 + h_3$ and $d_{\mathrm{tot}} = b + B + \sum_{k \geq 3} \widetilde{m}_k$.  Tradler's strictification (Tradler, arXiv:math/0108027) ensures compatibility of~$h$ with the cyclic structure.  Thus the chain-level nonvanishing records the nontriviality of the coherent homotopy witnessing the $E_3$-structure: an exact cocycle in the total complex, not an obstruction.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Supplementary verification}: $51$~tests in \texttt{derived\_framing\_obstruction.py} confirm the obstruction groups for all $7$~CY$_3$ geometries, Goodwillie layers $k = 2, 3, 4$, the Francis--Gaitsgory tangent complex, the explicit homotopy construction, and cross-checks with Proposition~\ref{prop:aq-e3-lifting-verification}.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[What actually obstructs CY-A$_3$]
\label{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}
The chain-level $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ is a red herring.  The actual obstacles to CY-A$_3$ are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{BV compatibility}: the holomorphic Chern--Simons contracting homotopy must be compatible with the full $E_3$-structure.  For toric CY$_3$: automatic ($T^3$-equivariance).  For compact CY$_3$: requires non-perturbative convergence of the \v{C}ech-HTT series.
 \item \emph{Quantization independence}: for compact CY$_3$ with $h^{2,1} > 1$, the chiral algebra should not depend on the choice of complex structure deformation within the family.
 \item \emph{Non-perturbative convergence}: the formal chiral operations from HTT must converge to holomorphic functions.
\end{enumerate}
None of these involve the chain-level commutator $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$.  The $\Ainf$-compatibility is settled at Level~2 (Costello TCFT) and Level~3 (this theorem); the remaining obstacles are analytic.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $51$~tests in \texttt{derived\_framing\_obstruction.py}.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[Adversarial stress test of the $E_3$ obstruction proofs]
\label{rem:e3-obstruction-stress-test}
An adversarial stress test (\texttt{cya3\_stress\_test.py}, $45$~tests) attacks Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:aq-e3-lifting-verification}, and Proposition~\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat} via five independent vectors:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Dunn additivity domain}: $E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$ holds for $E_n$-algebras in a symmetric monoidal $\infty$-category. The ambient $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$ qualifies; however, $E_3$ in $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$ does not automatically yield an $E_3$-chiral (= $E_3$-factorization) algebra; the factorization descent is part of the CY-A$_3$ programme. \textbf{Severity}: moderate; mitigated by the correct scoping in Remark~\ref{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}.
 \item \emph{Unit-connectedness scope}: the generic argument ($\HH^0(\cC) = k$ implies unit-connected) applies to all connected CY$_3$ manifolds.  For $K3 \times E$: the geometric Hochschild cohomology $\HH^0(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$ by connectedness, so the generic argument applies directly.  \textup{(}An earlier version of this remark incorrectly claimed $\HH^0 = k^2$ by conflating the algebraic K\"unneth formula for the external tensor product with the geometric $\HH^0$; the HKR decomposition $H^0(\cO_{K3}) \oplus H^2(T_{K3}) = k^2$ applies to K3 alone, not to $K3 \times E$.  See working-notes correction~\textup{\ref{warn:k3e-unit-connected}}.\textup{)}  The product decomposition of Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-product-decomposition} provides an independent confirmation. \textbf{Severity}: low (resolved by the geometric $\HH^0$ computation).
 \item \emph{Andr\'e--Quillen cotangent complex}: reduces to vectors~(i) and~(ii); no independent gap.
 \item \emph{TCFT $B^{(2)}$ identification}: Costello's open-closed TCFT equivalence identifies $B^{(2)}$ with the Connes-hierarchy contraction; this requires a strictly cyclic $\Ainf$ model. Tradler's strictification theorem (arXiv:math/0108027) guarantees existence. \textbf{Severity}: low.
 \item \emph{Cohomological vs chain-level}: all three proofs give cohomological vanishing; the chain-level $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ is a fact. The proofs correctly show the obstruction \emph{vanishes}; they do not claim to \emph{construct} the $E_3$-chiral algebra. \textbf{Severity}: non-issue (correctly scoped).
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{Verdict}: no fatal weaknesses. The proofs are sound for what they claim. The one moderate gap (factorization descent from $E_3$ in $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$ to $E_3$-factorization algebra) is mitigated by correct scoping in Remark~\ref{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}. The $K3 \times E$ unit-connectedness concern is resolved: $\HH^0(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)) = k$ by connectedness.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $45$~tests in \texttt{cya3\_stress\_test.py} (attack vectors, $\HH^0$ landscape, $K3 \times E$ product decomposition, mechanism classification, manuscript recommendations).
\end{remark}

\begin{warning}[$K3 \times E$ unit-connectedness]
\label{warn:k3e-unit-connected}
The algebraic K\"unneth formula $\HH^0(\Perf(K3)) \otimes \HH^0(\Perf(E))
= k^2 \otimes k^2$ gives the external tensor product of Hochschild
cohomologies, not the geometric $\HH^0$ of the product.  For the product
variety: $\HH^0(\Perf(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$ by
connectedness.  The HKR decomposition $H^0(\cO_{K3}) \oplus H^2(T_{K3})
= k^2$ applies to K3 alone and does not propagate to the product.
\end{warning}


\begin{proposition}[Independent AQ verification of $E_3$ lifting for CY$_3$]
\label{prop:aq-e3-lifting-verification}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For any smooth proper CY$_3$ category $\cC$ with chiral algebra $A = \HH_\bullet(\cC)$, the $\Etwo$-Andr\'e--Quillen cohomology satisfies $D^4_{E_2}(A, A) = 0$.  Consequently, the $\Etwo \to E_3$ lifting obstruction vanishes.  Three independent computations confirm this:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The $\Etwo$-cotangent complex $L_{E_2}(A)$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 1$ \textup{(}unit-connectedness\textup{)}, so the relative cotangent complex $L_{E_3/E_2}(A) = \Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A)$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 3$, giving $D^4 = \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}(L_{E_3/E_2}(A),\, A) = 0$.
 \item The $3$rd Goodwillie derivative of the identity functor on $\Etwo$-algebras, $\partial_3(\mathrm{Id})(A) = \mathrm{Map}(S^2, A^{\otimes 3})_{hS_3}$, has $\pi_0 = 0$ for unit-connected input, confirming the $E_3/\Etwo$ layer is trivial.
 \item The identification $D^4_{E_2}(A, A) = \HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A)$ via Dunn additivity \textup{(}$E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$\textup{)} recovers the vanishing proved in Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}.
\end{enumerate}
This holds for all shadow classes \textup{(}$\mathbf{G}$, $\mathbf{L}$, $\mathbf{C}$, $\mathbf{M}$\textup{)} and is independent of formality.  For the non-formal local~$\bP^2$ \textup{(}class~$\mathbf{M}$, $m_3 \neq 0$\textup{)}: $D^4 = 0$ despite $D^2_{E_2}(A, A) \neq 0$ \textup{(}mock modular deformations\textup{)}.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $61$~tests in \texttt{lurie\_e3\_obstruction.py} (AQ cohomology, Goodwillie derivatives, Francis--Gaitsgory deformation complex, Dunn equivalence cross-check, CY$_3$ landscape, explicit $K3 \times E$ and local~$\bP^2$ computations).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The argument proceeds via the Andr\'e--Quillen obstruction theory of Lurie~(HA~7.4) and Francis~\cite{Francis2013}.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 1: Identifying the obstruction group.}
By Dunn additivity, $E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$, so the relative cotangent complex for the $\Etwo \to E_3$ lifting is $L_{E_3/E_2}(A) \simeq \Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A)$.  The primary obstruction lives in
\[
 D^4_{E_2}(A, A) \;=\; \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Mod}_A^{E_2}}(\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A),\, A).
\]

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 2: Cotangent complex connectivity.}
For unit-connected~$A$ ($\HH^0(\cC) = k$: quintic, local~$\bP^2$, $\C^3$, conifold, $K3 \times E$), $L_{E_2}(A)$ is concentrated in degrees~$\geq 1$ (the degree-0 indecomposables quotient is trivial).  The relative complex $\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A)$ is therefore concentrated in degrees~$\geq 3$.  The mapping space $\mathrm{Map}(\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A), A)$ has $\pi_0 = 0$ because the source has no degree-0 generators that could map nontrivially to~$A$.  \textup{(}Note: $K3 \times E$ is unit-connected because $\HH^0(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$ by connectedness; the product decomposition of Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-product-decomposition} gives an independent confirmation.\textup{)}

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 3: Goodwillie calculus confirmation.}
The $3$rd derivative of the identity functor on $\Etwo$-algebras gives $\partial_3(\mathrm{Id})(A) = \mathrm{Map}(S^2, A^{\otimes 3})_{hS_3}$.  For unit-connected~$A$, the tensor power $A^{\otimes 3}$ is $0$-connected, and the $S^2$-mapping shifts connectivity, giving $\pi_0 = 0$.  The $S_3$-homotopy orbits preserve this vanishing.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 4: Equivalence with Dunn approach.}
The chain of identifications
\[
 D^4_{E_2}(A, A) \;=\; \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}(\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A), A) \;=\; \HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) \;=\; 0
\]
recovers the vanishing from Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}, providing an independent cross-check.  The two engines agree for all $7$ standard CY$_3$ geometries.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[Product decomposition for $K3 \times E$]
\label{rem:k3e-product-decomposition}
For the product $K3 \times E = \CY_2 \times \CY_1$, the $\bS^3$-framing decomposes completely. The CY$_3$ trace $\Tr_3 \colon \HH_3(K3 \times E) \to \C$ factors as $\Tr_3 = \Tr_2^{K3} \otimes \Tr_1^E$, where $\Tr_2^{K3} \colon \HH_2(K3) \to \C$ is the CY$_2$ trace (proved by CY-A$_2$) and $\Tr_1^E \colon \HH_1(E) \to \C$ is the CY$_1$ trace (the Connes operator, classical). The $\bS^3$-framing map on $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(K3 \times E) \simeq \mathrm{CC}_\bullet(K3) \otimes \mathrm{CC}_\bullet(E)$ is
\[
 F^{(3)}_{K3 \times E} \;=\; F^{(2)}_{K3} \otimes B^{(1)}_E,
\]
where $F^{(2)}_{K3}$ contracts two factors using the K3 trace and $B^{(1)}_E$ contracts one factor using the elliptic curve trace. Both ingredients are proved: $F^{(2)}_{K3}$ by CY-A$_2$, and $B^{(1)}_E$ is the classical Connes-hierarchy map. The Euler class correction from the Hopf fibration vanishes for products (the framing data is $S^2 \times S^1$, not $S^3$, so no Hopf twist occurs). The remaining content of Kummer Step~5 is the BV compatibility of this tensor product decomposition with the orbifold resolution $T^4/\bZ_2 \times E$.
\end{remark}


The following two computations illustrate the chain-level chiral
structure for concrete CY$_3$ geometries, distinguishing formal
from non-formal behaviour.

\begin{computation}[Chain-level chiral structure for the conifold]
\label{comp:conifold-chain-level}
The resolved conifold
$X = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1) \to \bP^1)$
has $\Ext^\bullet(\cE, \cE)$ with $12$ generators distributed
as $2 + 4 + 4 + 2$ by Ext degree. The Euler characteristic is
$\chi = 2 - 4 + 4 - 2 = 0$ (CY$_3$ check: $\sum (-1)^i \dim
\Ext^i = 0$). The $\Ainf$-algebra is \emph{formal}: the
transferred operations satisfy $m_k = 0$ for $k \geq 3$
(Kaledin's theorem: the derived category of the resolved conifold
is intrinsically formal as a CY$_3$ category, since it is derived
equivalent to $\C Q / (\partial W)$ for the Klebanov--Witten
quiver with quadratic potential). The chiral Jacobi identity
$\sum_{\sigma \in S_3} (-1)^{|\sigma|} \mu_2^{\mathrm{ch}}
(\mu_2^{\mathrm{ch}}(a_{\sigma(1)}, a_{\sigma(2)}; z_{12});
a_{\sigma(3)}; z_{13}) = 0$ is verified through total degree~$6$
in the generators. The shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{G}$
($r_{\max} = 2$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$), consistent with the formality.
\end{computation}

\begin{computation}[Non-formality for local $\bP^2$]
\label{comp:local-p2-nonformality}
The local $\bP^2$ geometry
$X = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-3) \to \bP^2)$ has $24$ generators
from the McKay quiver of $\bZ_3$: three vertices with $8$
generators each (the McKay correspondence assigns the regular
representation $\C[\bZ_3] = \rho_0 \oplus \rho_1 \oplus \rho_2$
to the vertices, and the tensor product with the defining
$\SL(3)$-representation determines the arrows).
The algebra is \emph{not} formal: the transferred ternary
operation is
\[
 m_3(x_{01}, y_{12}, z_{20}) \;=\; e_0,
\]
where $x_{01} \in \Ext^1(\cE_0, \cE_1)$,
$y_{12} \in \Ext^1(\cE_1, \cE_2)$,
$z_{20} \in \Ext^1(\cE_2, \cE_0)$ are the three generating
arrows around the quiver triangle, and
$e_0 \in \Ext^0(\cE_0, \cE_0)$ is the identity endomorphism.
This $m_3$ is the cubic term of the Ginzburg potential
$W = x_{01} y_{12} z_{20} - x_{02} y_{21} z_{10}$.
The chiral lift has double-pole structure:
\[
 \mu_3^{\mathrm{ch}}(x_{01}(z_1), y_{12}(z_2), z_{20}(z_3))
 \;=\; \frac{e_0}{(z_1 - z_2)(z_2 - z_3)}.
\]
The shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{M}$ ($r_{\max} = \infty$,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 3/2$): the non-formality forces an infinite tower of
shadow obstructions.
\end{computation}


\begin{remark}[The formality hierarchy for CY categories]
\label{rem:formality-hierarchy}
Three levels of formality must be distinguished:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \emph{de~Rham formality} (DGMS 1975): for compact K\"ahler~$X$, the de~Rham cdga $(H^*(X, \bC), 0, \cup)$ is formal. All Massey products on $H^*(X, \bC)$ vanish. This applies to every smooth projective CY manifold.
 \item \emph{Hodge-to-de~Rham degeneration} (Kaledin 2008): for any smooth proper dg category $\cC$ over characteristic zero, the Hochschild-to-cyclic spectral sequence degenerates at $E_2$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:hodge-dR-degeneration}}). This is universal and does not distinguish geometries.
 \item \emph{$\Ainf$-formality}: the minimal $\Ainf$-model on $\Ext^\bullet(\cE, \cE)$ has $m_k = 0$ for all $k \geq 3$. This is the strongest condition and fails for most CY$_3$ categories.
\end{enumerate}
Level~(i) does \emph{not} imply level~(iii): the elliptic curve is compact K\"ahler (hence de~Rham formal) but its derived category has $m_3 \neq 0$ (Polishchuk, arXiv:math/0205149).

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Compact CY$_3$ manifolds.}\enspace
For the quintic $Q \subset \bP^4$, level~(i) holds (DGMS) and level~(ii) holds (Kaledin), but level~(iii) \emph{fails}: the full $\Ainf$-category $D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(Q))$ has $m_3 \neq 0$ from the Yukawa coupling (the genus-$0$ three-point function, classical intersection $H^3 = 5$ plus Gromov--Witten corrections).
Kaledin's tilting formality theorem (arXiv:math/0308135) does not apply because compact CY$_3$ manifolds have no tilting generator ($\Ext^3(\cE, \cE) \neq 0$ by Serre duality).

\emph{Critical distinction}: $\Ext^*(\cO_Q, \cO_Q) = H^*(\cO_Q) = \bC \oplus 0 \oplus 0 \oplus \bC$ is concentrated in degrees~$0$ and~$3$, hence trivially formal. The non-formality lives in the multi-object $\Ainf$-structure involving $\Ext^*(\cO(a), \cO(b))$ for different twists $a, b$.

For the programme: since the quintic is \emph{not} $\Ainf$-formal, the TCFT resolution (Tsygan--Costello; see \texttt{tsygan\_formality\_obs\_ainf.py}) is necessary to establish $[\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf}] = 0$.
The shadow tower has positive depth (class~$\mathbf{M}$), encoding the GW corrections. The $\bS^3$-framing programme requires compatibility with the \emph{full} $\Ainf$-structure. The $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$ commutator is exact (not zero), which suffices: cohomological vanishing is the correct condition for the derived/homotopical setting.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $40$~tests in \texttt{formality\_ainf\_dcoh.py}: Hodge data for all standard CY manifolds, the three-level hierarchy, Kaledin K1/K2 analysis, quintic Ext algebra distinction (single-object vs full category), product formality for K3~$\times$~$E$, and programme implications.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[The $\En$ landscape for Calabi--Yau categories]
\label{rem:en-landscape}
The CY dimension $d$ determines the native algebraic structure. For $d \leq 3$:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY dim} & \textbf{Native $\En$} & \textbf{Enhancement} & \textbf{Mechanism} \\
 \midrule
 $d = 1$ (elliptic curve) & $\Einf$ & --- & Braiding symmetric \\
 $d = 2$ (K3, Higgs) & $\Etwo$ & native & $\bS^2$-framing automatic \\
 $d = 3$ (CY threefold) & $\Eone$ & $\Eone \to \Etwo$ & Drinfeld center / $\bS^3$-framing \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
By Dunn additivity, $\Etwo \simeq \Eone \otimes_{E_0} \Eone$. The $\Omega$-background freezes one $\Eone$-factor (it introduces a preferred direction in the plane), reducing $\Etwo$ to $\Eone$. The Drinfeld center passage $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(\cdot))$ restores the $\Etwo$ structure by recovering the frozen factor.

%: The slogan "S^3-framing gives E_3 gives E_2" is correct at the
% sophisticated level (via Drinfeld center) but FALSE at the naive level
% (direct restriction gives symmetric braiding). State the level explicitly.
The $E_3$-to-$\Etwo$ restriction via Dunn additivity gives a \emph{symmetric} braiding, since $\pi_1(\mathrm{Conf}_2(\R^3))$ is trivial. The genuinely nonsymmetric braiding arises through the Drinfeld center, not through this restriction.

For $d \geq 4$, the $\Eone$ stabilization theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}, Chapter~\ref{ch:en-factorization}) shows that the chiral algebra is always $\Eone$, with additional shifted structure classified by $\pi_d(BU)$ (Bott periodicity). The framing obstruction is $\Z$-valued at all even $d$, trivial at $d \equiv 1, 3, 7 \pmod{8}$, and $\Z_2$-valued from the $\Sp$-refinement at $d \equiv 5 \pmod{8}$.
\end{remark}

The topological obstruction to the $\bS^3$-framing vanishes universally, and the chain-level trivialization is supplied by holomorphic Chern--Simons for the standard compact and toric examples. The question that remains is where the nonsymmetric braiding comes from: as the $E_n$-landscape table makes clear, direct Dunn restriction from $E_3$ to $\Etwo$ yields only symmetric braiding for $d = 3$. The answer is the Drinfeld center.

% ============================================================
\section{The Drinfeld center and $\Etwo$ enhancement}
\label{sec:d3-drinfeld-center}
% ============================================================

The key structural innovation for $d = 3$ is that the quantum group braiding does \emph{not} come from restricting the $E_3$-structure to $\Etwo$, but from taking the Drinfeld center of the $\Eone$-monoidal representation category.

\subsection{The CY condition and the universal $R$-matrix}
\label{subsec:cy-rmatrix}

The structure function of the affine Yangian $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:yangian-structure-function}
 g(z) = \frac{(z - h_1)(z - h_2)(z - h_3)}{(z + h_1)(z + h_2)(z + h_3)},
\end{equation}
where $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ are the equivariant parameters. The CY$_3$ condition manifests as the identity
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cy-condition-rmatrix}
 g(z) \, g(-z) = 1.
\end{equation}
This is the $R$-matrix unitarity condition: it guarantees that the braiding $\sigma_{V,W} \colon V \otimes W \to W \otimes V$ defined by $R(z)$ is involutive up to homotopy. The identity~\eqref{eq:cy-condition-rmatrix} is an algebraic identity that holds for \emph{any} $h_1, h_2, h_3$, without the CY constraint $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$: the numerator of $g(z)g(-z)$ is $\prod_i(z-h_i)(-z-h_i) = \prod_i(h_i^2 - z^2)$, which equals the denominator $\prod_i(z+h_i)(-z+h_i) = \prod_i(h_i^2 - z^2)$. The CY constraint plays a different role: it ensures $g(z) \to 1$ as $z \to \infty$, which is needed for the $R$-matrix integral representation to converge (Pillar~(d) of Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}).

\begin{theorem}[Drinfeld center identification for $\C^3$]
\label{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
\[
 \cZ\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))\bigr) \;\simeq\; \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)) \;\simeq\; \Rep^{\Etwo}(\cW_{1+\infty}).
\]
The character of the Drinfeld center is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:drinfeld-center-character}
 \mathrm{ch}\bigl(\cZ(\Rep(Y^+))\bigr) \;=\; M(q)^2 \cdot P(q)
 \;=\; \prod_{n \geq 1} (1-q^n)^{-(2n+1)},
\end{equation}
where $M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$ is the MacMahon function (counting plane partitions) and $P(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-1}$ is the Euler partition function. This is verified to~$10$ levels by three independent computations: direct enumeration of half-braidings on Fock modules, comparison with the affine Yangian character, and product decomposition $M^2 P$.

The Yang $R$-matrix on the charge-$2$ sector is an explicit $8 \times 8$ matrix satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The Yang--Baxter equation $R_{12}(u-v)\,R_{13}(u)\,R_{23}(v) = R_{23}(v)\,R_{13}(u)\,R_{12}(u-v)$ at the matrix level (symbolic verification).
 \item Unitarity: $R(z)\,R(-z) = \mathrm{Id}$ (from the CY condition $g(z)g(-z) = 1$).
 \item $\Etwo$ nontriviality: the braiding is genuinely nonsymmetric (not $\Einf$).
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 93 tests in \texttt{drinfeld\_center\_yangian.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The identification proceeds by constructing the half-braiding data explicitly.

A half-braiding on an object $V \in \Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+)$ is a natural isomorphism $\beta_{V,-} \colon V \otimes (-) \xrightarrow{\sim} (-) \otimes V$ satisfying the hexagon axiom. For a Fock module $\cF_\lambda$ labelled by a partition $\lambda$, the half-braiding is determined by the $R$-matrix: $\beta_{\cF_\lambda, \cF_\mu} = R_{\lambda,\mu}(u)$, where
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:r-matrix-partitions}
 R_{\lambda,\mu}(u) = \prod_{s \in \lambda, \, t \in \mu} g\bigl(u + c(s) - c(t)\bigr),
\end{equation}
and $c(s) = h_1 \, a(s) + h_2 \, l(s) + h_3 \, a'(s)$ is the content function (arm, leg, co-arm weighted by equivariant parameters).

The hexagon axiom is equivalent to the Yang--Baxter equation for $R$. This is verified at the matrix level for all triples $(\lambda, \mu, \nu)$ with $|\lambda| + |\mu| + |\nu| \leq 6$.

The character computation: $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ has character $M(q)$ (the positive half of the affine Yangian counts plane partitions). The Drinfeld center doubles the $Y^+$ generators (the half-braiding data adds the ``negative'' Yangian generators) and adds a central element (the half-braiding on the identity), giving $M(q)^2 \cdot P(q)$. The additional factor $P(q)$ counts the central extensions.
\end{proof}


\begin{corollary}[$\Eone \to \Etwo$ obstruction is trivial]
\label{cor:e1-e2-trivial}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The $\Eone \to \Etwo$ enhancement obstruction is trivial for all tested CY$_3$ CoHAs:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\C^3$: zero (Drinfeld double; $g(z)g(-z) = 1$ from the CY condition).
 \item Resolved conifold: zero.
 \item $K3 \times E$: zero (inherits from the $K3$ factor).
 \item General compact CY$_3$: conditional on $\bS^3$-framing, which Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}} shows is trivializable.
\end{itemize}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 93 tests in \texttt{drinfeld\_center\_yangian.py}; 124 tests in \texttt{cy\_to\_chiral\_functor.py}.
\end{corollary}


% ============================================================
\section{The bar complex and shadow tower of $\C^3$}
\label{sec:c3-bar-shadow}
% ============================================================

Whenever the relevant CY$_3$ chiral algebra exists, the Vol~I shadow obstruction tower (Theorems~A--D) specializes to it. For $\C^3$, the shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{G}$ (Gaussian, $r_{\mathrm{max}} = 2$): all higher shadows vanish, and the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ determines the full genus expansion.


\begin{proposition}[Bar-complex Euler product for $\C^3$]
\label{prop:c3-bar-euler}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The bar-complex Euler product for $\C^3$ is
\[
 \sum_{k \geq 0} (-1)^k \, \mathrm{ch}(B^k) \;=\; \frac{1}{M(q)} \;=\; \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^n.
\]
The BPS invariants are $\Omega(n) = n$ (the multiplicity of the degree-$n$ BPS state equals~$n$). The first bar cohomology at degree~$n$ has $\dim H^1(B)_n = n$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 115 tests in \texttt{c3\_grand\_verification.py}; 59 tests in \texttt{bar\_comparison\_c3.py}.
\end{proposition}


\begin{proposition}[Crystal melting = $\Eone$ bar cohomology]
\label{prop:crystal-melting-bar-cohomology}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Three-dimensional partitions (crystal melting configurations for $\C^3$) are precisely the bar cohomology classes of $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$. Explicitly:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The MacMahon function $M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$ is the bar Euler characteristic of the positive half of the affine Yangian: $M(q) = \sum_{k \geq 0} \mathrm{ch}(H^k(B(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))))$.
 \item The BPS invariants $\Omega(n) = n$ are recovered as $\Omega(n) = \dim H^1(B(Y^+))_n$, i.e.\ the degree-$1$ bar cohomology at degree~$n$ has dimension~$n$. Three independent verification paths agree: (a)~plethystic logarithm $\mathrm{PLog}(M(q)) = \sum_{n \geq 1} n q^n$; (b)~direct computation of $H^1(B(\mathrm{Sym}(V_{\mathrm{BPS}})))$ for the commutative model; (c)~the formula $\Omega(n) = n$ from Kontsevich--Soibelman.
 \item The bar Euler characteristic inverts the MacMahon function: $\sum_{k \geq 0}(-1)^k (M(q)-1)^k = 1/M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^n$. This identity is verified by three independent paths: direct product, alternating bar sum, and power-series inversion of $M(q)$.
\end{enumerate}
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 70 tests in \texttt{test\_crystal\_bar\_identification.py}, verifying all three claims by the three-path method (\texttt{crystal\_bar\_identification.py}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Crystal melting as bar filtration]
\label{rem:crystal-melting-bar}
The crystal melting model of Okounkov--Reshetikhin--Vafa, counting three-dimensional partitions weighted by $q^{|\pi|}$, is the degree filtration of the $\Eone$ bar complex of $\cW_{1+\infty}$: the bar degree-$n$ component $\barB^n(\cW_{1+\infty})$ counts configurations of $n$ atoms in the crystal. The MacMahon function $M(q) = \prod_{n \ge 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$ is the bar Poincar\'e series (Proposition~\ref{prop:c3-bar-euler}). Concretely, the degree filtration $F^{\bullet} \barB(\cW_{1+\infty})$ has associated graded whose Hilbert series at degree~$n$ counts plane partitions of size~$n$, with the bar differential encoding the crystal growth rules (addition and removal of atoms at admissible sites). The inverse MacMahon function $1/M(q) = \prod_{n \ge 1}(1-q^n)^n$ is the bar Euler characteristic (alternating sum over bar degrees), consistent with Proposition~\ref{prop:crystal-melting-bar-cohomology}(iii).
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Topological vertex = degree-$3$ $\Eone$ bar amplitude]
\label{prop:topological-vertex-e1-amplitude}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The topological vertex $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q)$ of Aganagic--Klemm--Mari\~{n}o--Vafa is the degree-$3$ $\Eone$ bar amplitude of $A_{\C^3} = \cW_{1+\infty}$, evaluated on Fock-space states $|\lambda\rangle, |\mu\rangle, |\nu\rangle$:
\[
 C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q) \;=\; \bigl\langle \lambda,\mu,\nu \bigm| B^{\Eone}_{0,3}(A_{\C^3}) \bigr\rangle.
\]
Four independent components establish this identification.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Algebra identification.} By Schiffmann--Vasserot, $\CoHA(\C^3) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$; by Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak, $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1) = \cW_{1+\infty}$. The CY-to-chiral functor produces $A_{\C^3} = \cW_{1+\infty}$ as an $\Eone$-chiral algebra.
 \item \emph{Vertex as $3$-point amplitude.} The topological vertex $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}$ is the open topological string amplitude on the pair-of-pants ($g = 0$, $n = 3$) with boundary conditions $|\lambda\rangle, |\mu\rangle, |\nu\rangle$ on the three Lagrangians. In chiral algebra language this is the genus-$0$ degree-$3$ factorization homology amplitude $B_{0,3}(A_{\C^3})$; the $\Eone$ structure (not $\Etwo$) is used because $A_{\C^3}$ is natively $\Eone$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}).
 \item \emph{Gluing = sewing.} The toric diagram gluing rules (one vertex factor $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}$ per trivalent node, one propagator $(-q)^{|\lambda|}/z_\lambda$ per internal edge, sum over internal partitions) are the $\Eone$ sewing rules (Vol~I, MC5 analytic HS-sewing lane). The edge propagator $(-q)^{|\lambda|}/z_\lambda$ is the $\Eone$ bar complex pairing on $H^1(B^1)$.
 \item \emph{Refined vertex.} The refined vertex $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q,t)$ (Iqbal--Kozcaz--Vafa) incorporates the two independent $\Omega$-background parameters $(q,t) = (e^{-h_1}, e^{-h_2})$ of the $\Eone$ bar complex with equivariant data.
\end{enumerate}
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 162 tests in \texttt{test\_topological\_vertex\_e1\_engine.py}, testing the identification by six independent methods including direct Schur-function computation, Fock-space bar amplitude, gluing vs.\ sewing comparison, and partition function factorization (\texttt{topological\_vertex\_e1\_engine.py}).
\end{proposition}


\begin{theorem}[Modular characteristic of $\C^3$: five-path verification]
\label{thm:kappa-c3}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cW_{1+\infty}, c=1) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 1$.

%: kappa depends on the full algebra, not the Virasoro subalgebra.
This is \emph{not} $c/2 = 1/2$. At $c = 1$, the $\cW_{1+\infty}$ algebra \emph{is} the Heisenberg VOA $H_1$, whose modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_k) = k$, giving $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$. The formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = c/2$ is specific to the Virasoro algebra; for the Heisenberg VOA, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = k$ (the level), not $c/2$.

Five independent verifications:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{OPE}: $J(z)J(w) \sim 1/(z-w)^2$ gives level $k = 1$, hence $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 1$.
 \item \emph{MacMahon asymptotics}: the genus-$1$ free energy $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}/24 = 1/24$.
 \item \emph{DT partition function}: the degree-$0$ contribution to $Z_{\DT}$ gives $F_1 = 1/24$.
 \item \emph{Affine Yangian}: the structure function $g(u)$ at the self-dual point determines $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$.
 \item \emph{CY categorical trace}: $\chi^{\CY}_{\mathrm{eff}}(\C^3) = 1$.
\end{enumerate}
The shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{G}$ (Gaussian, $r_{\mathrm{max}} = 2$): $C = 0$, $Q = 0$, $\Delta = 8\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4 = 0$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 115 tests in \texttt{c3\_grand\_verification.py}; 98 tests in \texttt{c3\_shadow\_tower.py}.
\end{theorem}


\begin{remark}[Per-channel $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ and MacMahon decomposition]
\label{rem:c3-per-channel}
The $\cW_{1+\infty}$ algebra has generators at each spin $s = 1, 2, 3, \ldots$, giving per-channel modular characteristics $\kappa_s = 1/s$. The total $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \sum_{s=1}^{N} \kappa_s = H_N$ (the $N$-th harmonic number, divergent as $N \to \infty$). The effective $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ per MacMahon level is $1$ (constant): the $n$-fold multiplicity of degree-$n$ BPS states exactly compensates the $1/n$ suppression. The overall classification is class~$\mathbf{M}$ (infinite shadow depth) when the full spin tower is included; it reduces to class~$\mathbf{G}$ at the Heisenberg truncation $s = 1$.

At $N = 1$, all three constructions (envelope, shuffle algebra, crystal melting) produce Heisenberg. The CoHA character is $M(q)$ (plane partitions), exceeding the $\cW$-algebra character $P(q)$ (ordinary partitions):
\[
 \frac{M(q)}{P(q)} \;=\; \prod_{n \geq 2}(1-q^n)^{-(n-1)}.
\]
This ratio counts the higher-spin contributions.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 87 tests in \texttt{c3\_envelope\_comparison.py}; 50 tests in \texttt{macmahon\_shadow\_decomposition.py}.
\end{remark}


% ============================================================
\section{$\Eone$ universality for CY$_3$ chiral algebras}
\label{sec:e1-universality}
% ============================================================

The preceding sections have established the $d = 3$ functor chain and the Drinfeld center passage from $\Eone$ to $\Etwo$. The following theorem shows that the $\Eone$ structure is \emph{universal}: every CY$_3$ chiral algebra with $\Omega$-deformation is natively $\Eone$, not $\Etwo$. The proof has four independent pillars.


\begin{theorem}[$\Eone$ universality for CY$_3$ chiral algebras]
\label{thm:e1-universality-cy3}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For any toric CY$_3$ category $\cC$ with $T^3$-equivariant $\Omega$-deformation ($\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3 \neq 0$, subject to $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$), the toric CoHA / chart-gluing route supplies a canonical ordered $\Eone$-side package. Any toric CY$_3$ chiral algebra $A_\cC$ obtained from that package is natively $\Eone$, not $\Etwo$. This theorem does \emph{not} identify $A_\cC$ with the general functor $\Phi_3(\cC)$; it uses only the toric CoHA route. The general functor $\Phi_3$ is now proved (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}), providing the identification for all smooth proper CY$_3$ categories including compact non-toric ones.

Concretely: the $\bS^3$-framing obstruction vanishes topologically ($\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = 0$), but the chain-level BV trivialization via holomorphic CS is NOT $\Etwo$-equivariant. The $\Etwo$-braided structure is recovered \emph{only} through the Drinfeld center of the $\Eone$-monoidal representation category (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix a toric realization $A_\cC$ coming from the CoHA / chart-gluing package. Four independent pillars, each sufficient to establish the $\Eone$ nature.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (a): Abelianity of the classical bracket.}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}, the $\GL(3)$-invariant Schouten--Nijenhuis brackets on $\PV^*(\C^3)$ all vanish. The classical Lie conformal algebra is therefore abelian: it carries no Lie bracket, and hence no classical braiding. The entire noncommutative structure of $A_\cC$ arises from quantization in the factorization envelope, not from a pre-existing bracket. This quantization introduces associativity ($\Eone$) through the extension correspondence (the CoHA multiplication), which is ordered: short exact sequences $0 \to V' \to V \to V'' \to 0$ have a preferred direction (sub before quotient). There is no natural isomorphism between the ``$V'$ sub, $V''$ quot'' and ``$V''$ sub, $V'$ quot'' correspondences; such an isomorphism would be the $R$-matrix, which requires the Drinfeld double.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (b): One-dimensional deformation space.}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-hochschild}, $\HH^2(\PV^*(\C^3)) = 1$: the deformation space is one-dimensional, spanned by $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$. An $\Eone$-algebra (associative) has a one-parameter deformation theory (the associator is a single scalar). An $\Etwo$-algebra would have a \emph{two}-dimensional deformation space, since Dunn additivity $\Etwo \simeq \Eone \otimes_{E_0} \Eone$ contributes one parameter per $\Eone$-factor. The fact that $\dim \HH^2 = 1$ is therefore diagnostic of $\Eone$, not $\Etwo$.

This argument applies universally: for any toric CY$_3$ with $T^3$-equivariant $\Omega$-deformation, the equivariant deformation space is one-dimensional (spanned by $\sigma_3$), and the conclusion is $\Eone$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (c): BV trivialization breaks $\Etwo$ to $\Eone$.}
The topological $\bS^3$-framing obstruction vanishes: $\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = \pi_2(\Sp(2m)) = 0$ for all $m \geq 1$ (the CY$_3$ pairing reduces the structure group to $\Sp(2m)$, and all compact simply-connected simple Lie groups have vanishing $\pi_2$; independently, $\pi_3(BU) = 0$ by Bott periodicity, since $3$ is odd).

However, the chain-level BV trivialization via holomorphic Chern--Simons is \emph{not} $\Etwo$-compatible. The holomorphic CS functional
\[
 \operatorname{CS}(\bar\partial + A) \;=\; \int_X \Omega \wedge \operatorname{tr}\!\bigl(A \wedge \bar\partial A + \tfrac{2}{3} A \wedge A \wedge A\bigr)
\]
depends on the choice of holomorphic volume form $\Omega \in \Gamma(X, \Omega^3_X)$. The $\Etwo$-operad structure requires invariance under the $U(1)$-rotation of the plane perpendicular to the $\R$-direction (the rotation that exchanges the two $\Eone$-factors in Dunn additivity). Under this rotation, $\Omega$ transforms with weight~$1$: $\Omega \mapsto e^{i\theta}\Omega$. The BV trivialization $\delta_{\BV}(\operatorname{CS}) = \int \Omega \wedge F_A$ is therefore \emph{not} $U(1)$-equivariant. This is the chain-level obstruction that breaks $\Etwo$ to $\Eone$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (d): $R$-matrix unitarity from the CY condition.}
The structure function $g(z) = \prod_i (z-h_i)/(z+h_i)$ satisfies $g(z)\,g(-z) = 1$ as an algebraic identity (the numerator and denominator of the product are identical). The CY condition $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ plays a different role: it ensures $g(z) \to 1$ as $z \to \infty$, which is needed for the $R$-matrix integral representation to converge and for the braiding to be well-defined on the representation category. The $R$-matrix lives in $Y^+(\widehat\fgl_1) \,\widehat\otimes\, Y^-(\widehat\fgl_1)$ (the tensor product of the \emph{two halves} of the Drinfeld double), confirming that the braiding is not intrinsic to the CoHA $Y^+$ alone. The CoHA sees only one half and is therefore $\Eone$.

\medskip
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 89 tests in \texttt{e1\_universality\_cy3.py} testing all four pillars for $\C^3$, resolved conifold, local $\bP^2$, and the quintic.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[$\Eone$ universality vs $\Etwo$ recovery]
\label{rem:e1-vs-e2}
The theorem does NOT say that CY$_3$ chiral algebras lack $\Etwo$ structure. It says that the $\Etwo$ structure is \emph{not native}: it arises through the Drinfeld center passage $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat\frakg_\cC))$, which adds new data (the half-braiding / $R$-matrix). The distinction is operadic: the CoHA $\cH(Q, W)$ is an algebra over the $\Eone$-operad (little intervals), not over the $\Etwo$-operad (little disks), even though its representation category acquires $\Etwo$-braiding after taking the Drinfeld center.

At the self-dual point $\sigma_3 = 0$, the $\Omega$-deformation is trivial and the chiral algebra degenerates to the free Heisenberg $H_1$. The representation category is then symmetric monoidal ($\Einf$): the Drinfeld center adds no new braiding. The $\Eone$ universality theorem applies only when $\sigma_3 \neq 0$.
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Categorical $\Etwo$-obstruction for CY$_3$]
\label{prop:e1-obstruction-categorical}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $\cC$ be a smooth proper CY$_3$ category over~$\C$ \textup{(}hypotheses \textup{\ref{hyp:smooth}--\ref{hyp:cy3}} of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}. No torus action is assumed. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Topological framing is unobstructed.}
 CY$_3$ Serre duality $\Ext^k(E, F) \cong \Ext^{3-k}(F, E)^*$ gives an antisymmetric pairing on $\Ext^1(E, E)$, reducing the structure group from $\GL(2m)$ to $\Sp(2m)$. Since $\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = 0$ for all $m \geq 1$, the topological $\bS^3$-framing obstruction vanishes universally. This is the Serre duality content of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}(i)}.
 \item \emph{The structural $\Etwo$-obstruction is nonzero.}
 The Euler form $\chi(E, F) = \sum_k (-1)^k \dim \Ext^k(E, F)$ satisfies $\chi(E, F) = -\chi(F, E)$ by the CY$_3$ Serre functor. If $\rk(\chi) \geq 2$ \textup{(}i.e., the charge lattice $K_0(\cC)$ has rank $\geq 2$ and the Euler form is not identically zero\textup{)}, then the structural component $\cO_2^{\mathrm{str}} \neq 0$ \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:three-component-obstruction}(ii)}\textup{)}. The CoHA multiplication, defined by the correspondence of short exact sequences $0 \to V' \to V \to V'' \to 0$, is inherently ordered: sub before quotient. No natural isomorphism between the two orderings exists without passing to the Drinfeld double.
 \item \emph{The $\Etwo$-promotion is categorically obstructed.}
 Combining~(i) and~(ii): for any CY$_3$ category $\cC$ with $\rk(\chi) \geq 2$, the topological obstruction to the $\bS^3$-framing vanishes but the structural obstruction to $\Etwo$-enhancement does not. If a chiral algebra $A_\cC$ exists \textup{(}conditionally, via Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}, it is at most $\Eone$: the $\Etwo$-braiding arises only through the Drinfeld center of $\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Part~(i) is the symplectic path of Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}, whose proof uses only the CY$_3$ Serre pairing and the homotopy group computation $\pi_2(\Sp(2m)) = 0$. No torus action enters.

Part~(ii) follows from the antisymmetry of the Euler form. CY$_3$ Serre duality gives $\chi(E, F) = (-1)^3 \chi(F, E) = -\chi(F, E)$. An antisymmetric bilinear form on a lattice of rank $\geq 2$ has $\rk(\chi) \geq 2$ whenever any arrow in the Ext-quiver has multiplicity $\dim \Ext^1(S_i, S_j) \neq \dim \Ext^1(S_j, S_i)$, which by Serre duality $\Ext^1(S_i, S_j) \cong \Ext^2(S_j, S_i)^*$ is generically the case. The structural obstruction $\cO_2^{\mathrm{str}} = \rk(B^{\mathrm{anti}})$ where $B^{\mathrm{anti}}_{ij} = \dim \Ext^1(S_i, S_j) - \dim \Ext^1(S_j, S_i)$ is proved in Proposition~\ref{prop:three-component-obstruction}(ii) without any toric hypothesis.

Part~(iii) is the logical conjunction. The topological vanishing removes the possibility that the $\Etwo$-obstruction is of homotopy-theoretic origin (as it would be for CY$_4$, where $\pi_4(BU) = \pi_3(U) = \Z$ provides a $\Z$-valued obstruction to the $\bS^4$-framing; when this obstruction is nonzero, $\Etwo$-enhancement is obstructed at the topological level). The structural nonvanishing shows that for CY$_3$ the obstruction is purely algebraic: it lives in the antisymmetry of the Euler form, which is a categorical invariant of the CY$_3$ structure.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the categorical argument]
\label{rem:categorical-e1-scope}
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:e1-obstruction-categorical}} establishes that the $\Etwo$-promotion is \emph{obstructed} for any CY$_3$ category with $\rk(\chi) \geq 2$. It does not, by itself, \emph{construct} the $\Eone$ structure. In the toric case, the CoHA / chart-gluing machinery supplies the ordered $\Eone$-side object treated in Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}}; this should not be conflated with the conjectural general functor $\Phi_3$. For non-toric CY$_3$, constructing an $\Eone$-chiral algebra $A_\cC$ still requires Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}} and its chain-level $\bS^3$-framing hypothesis~\textup{\ref{hyp:framing}}.

The four pillars of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}} decompose as follows with respect to the toric hypothesis:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Pillar~(a) \textup{(}abelian classical bracket\textup{)}: specific to $\C^3$. For compact CY$_3$, the global sections of the polyvector sheaf carry nontrivial brackets from the complex structure moduli. Does not categorify.
 \item Pillar~(b) \textup{(}one-dimensional deformation space\textup{)}: specific to toric CY$_3$. Compact CY$_3$ have $\dim \HH^2 = h^{2,1}$, which is multi-dimensional for generic threefolds ($h^{2,1} = 101$ for the quintic). Does not categorify.
 \item Pillar~(c) \textup{(}BV-to-$\Eone$ breaking\textup{)}: partially categorical. The topological vanishing $\pi_3(B\Sp) = 0$ is fully categorical \textup{(}this is Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:e1-obstruction-categorical}(i)} above\textup{)}. The chain-level BV breaking via the holomorphic volume form $\Omega$ requires geometric input beyond the categorical CY$_3$ structure.
 \item Pillar~(d) \textup{(}$R$-matrix unitarity\textup{)}: specific to toric CY$_3$ with explicit equivariant parameters. The structure function $g(z)$ requires the torus action. Does not categorify in its current form.
\end{itemize}
The categorical content of the four pillars is precisely the $\Etwo$-obstruction of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:e1-obstruction-categorical}}: Serre duality forces antisymmetry of the Euler form, which obstructs the promotion. The \emph{construction} of the $\Eone$ structure for non-toric CY$_3$ remains the content of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[K-theoretic vs cohomological Hall: a refinement of $\Phi_3$]
\label{rem:kha-vs-coha-bridge}
The $d = 3$ functor chain of Section~\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain} lands on the \emph{cohomological} side: $\CoHA(\cC) := H^\bullet_{G \times T}(\cM^W_\cC, \varphi_W \Q)$ with Kontsevich--Soibelman convolution through the Hecke stack of short exact sequences~\cite{KS2008}. For $\cC = \mathrm{MF}(\C^3, 0)$ the Schiffmann--Vasserot identification gives $\CoHA(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$~\cite{SV2013}, which the envelope step upgrades to $\cW_{1 + \infty}$ at $c = 1$ on the chiral side.

A parallel construction replaces cohomology by equivariant $K$-theory: $\KHA(\cC) := K_{G \times T}(\cM^W_\cC)$ with $\mathrm{Tor}$-convolution through the same Hecke stack, in the sense of Kapranov--Vasserot~\cite{KV2011} and Negu\c{t}~\cite{Negut2022}. Three structural differences from $\CoHA$ are load-bearing for the programme.

First, $\KHA(\C^3) \simeq U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}_1})$, the \emph{quantum toroidal} algebra, rather than the affine Yangian. The transition is exponential in spectral parameter: where $\CoHA$ sees the rational structure function $g(z) = \prod_i (z - h_i)/(z + h_i)$, $\KHA$ sees the trigonometric function $g^q(z) = \prod_i (1 - q^{h_i} z^{-1})/(1 - q^{-h_i} z^{-1})$. The programme's engine \texttt{compute/lib/e3\_ce\_quantum\_toroidal.py} implements the trigonometric structure function directly; the Koszul-dual identification $\Phi_3^K(\C^3) \simeq U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}_1})$ at degenerate parameters is the target of \texttt{conj:k3-quantum-toroidal}.

Second, the relationship is controlled by the Chern character $\ch \colon \KHA \to \CoHA \otimes \Q$. This is a ring homomorphism (convolution commutes with $\ch$ by Grothendieck--Riemann--Roch on the Hecke correspondence), and it is surjective after inverting integer denominators, but it has a torsion kernel given by the $\mathrm{Tor}$-corrections invisible to rational cohomology. The $K$-theoretic refinement is therefore strictly finer: $\KHA$ detects torsion BPS invariants and quantum-group structure constants at roots of unity where $\CoHA$ collapses. Concretely, the Nekrasov partition function $Z^{\mathrm{inst}}(q, t; \epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ lives natively on the $\KHA$ side (equivariant $K$-theoretic DT counts); its $\CoHA$ shadow is the cohomological partition function $Z^{\mathrm{coh}}(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2) = \lim_{q \to 1} Z^{\mathrm{inst}}$ under the standard specialization $q = e^{\hbar \epsilon}$, $t = e^{\hbar \beta}$, $\hbar \to 0$.

Third, the programme's CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_3$ as constructed in Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} lands on the $\CoHA$ side: the factorization envelope of a $\mathrm{PV}^\bullet$-valued Lie conformal algebra produces rational (Laurent-series) OPE kernels, matching $\CoHA$'s rational structure function. A $K$-theoretic functor $\Phi_3^K$ would take values in chiral algebras over $\Z[q^{\pm 1}, t^{\pm 1}]$ with trigonometric OPE kernels (quantum vertex algebras in the Etingof--Kazhdan sense); its existence, parallelism with $\Phi_3$, and compatibility with the Chern character through the limit $q \to 1$ are conjectural (\texttt{conj:phi3-K-theoretic} in the FRONTIER ledger). The toric verification locus already tested on the cohomological side ($\C^3$, conifold, local~$\bP^2$, local~$\bP^1 \times \bP^1$, $K3 \times E$) has a parallel $\KHA$ calculation where both sides are independently known for $\C^3$, the conifold, and local $\bP^2$; gluing across charts in the $K$-theoretic setting requires a $q$-deformed hocolim whose definition is open.

The distinction cures a drift latent in the programme narration. Where the prose uses ``CoHA'' loosely to mean ``Hall-algebra output of the CY-A$_3$ functor'', the intended object is the \emph{cohomological} Hall algebra, and the \emph{K-theoretic} refinement is a strictly finer parallel with its own target (quantum toroidal) distinct from the affine Yangian. The programme's Vol III $\C^3$ verification is a CoHA statement; the quantum toroidal appears in Koszul-dual position on the $\Etwo$ category ($\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$), not as the direct output of $\Phi_3$. A genuinely K-theoretic CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_3^K$ remains to be constructed; when it is, its output on $\C^3$ is expected to be the quantum toroidal chiral algebra whose classical ($q \to 1$) limit is $\cW_{1 + \infty}$.
\end{remark}


% ============================================================
\section{The quiver-chart gluing construction}
\label{sec:quiver-chart-gluing}
% ============================================================

The preceding sections construct the CY$_3$ chiral algebra for $\C^3$ via the five-step chain (Section~\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain}) and establish $\Eone$ universality (Section~\ref{sec:e1-universality}). For a general CY$_3$ $X$, the geometry is not globally toric. The strategy is to cover $X$ by quiver charts (local presentations of the derived category as modules over a quiver with potential) and to glue the local $\Eone$-chiral algebras into a global object via homotopy colimits. Wall-crossing mutations provide the transition data, and the bar-hocolim commutation theorem guarantees that the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is an invariant of the global algebra, independent of the atlas.

\subsection{Quiver chart atlases}
\label{subsec:quiver-chart-atlas}

\begin{definition}[Quiver chart]
\label{def:quiver-chart}
A \emph{quiver chart} on a CY$_3$ category $\cC$ is a triple $(Q, W, \Psi)$ consisting of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item a finite quiver with potential $(Q, W)$, where $Q = (Q_0, Q_1)$ is a quiver and $W \in \C Q / [\C Q, \C Q]$ is a potential (a formal linear combination of oriented cycles modulo cyclic equivalence);
 \item a fully faithful exact functor $\Psi \colon D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}\C Q / (\partial W)) \hookrightarrow \cC$ whose essential image generates a thick subcategory of $\cC$.
\end{enumerate}
The chart is \emph{full} if $\Psi$ is an equivalence. The chart is \emph{CY-compatible} if the CY$_3$ structure on $\cC$ restricts to the Ginzburg CY$_3$ structure on $D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}\C Q / (\partial W))$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Quiver-chart atlas]
\label{def:quiver-chart-atlas}
A \emph{quiver-chart atlas} for a CY$_3$ category $\cC$ is a finite diagram
\[
 \cA = \bigl\{(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha, \Psi_\alpha)\bigr\}_{\alpha \in I}
\]
of CY-compatible quiver charts indexed by a finite poset $I$, together with:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \textbf{Cover.} The thick subcategories $\langle \mathrm{Im}(\Psi_\alpha) \rangle_{\alpha \in I}$ jointly generate $\cC$ (every object of $\cC$ is a finite iterated cone on objects in the union of the images).
 \item \textbf{Transition data.} For each pair $\alpha \leq \beta$ in $I$, a specified sequence of quiver mutations $\mu_{\alpha\beta} \colon (Q_\alpha, W_\alpha) \dashrightarrow (Q_\beta, W_\beta)$ and a natural equivalence $\Psi_\beta \circ \mu_{\alpha\beta}^* \simeq \Psi_\alpha$ on the overlap.
 \item \textbf{Cocycle.} The mutation sequences satisfy the cocycle condition: for $\alpha \leq \beta \leq \gamma$, the composite $\mu_{\beta\gamma} \circ \mu_{\alpha\beta}$ is homotopic to $\mu_{\alpha\gamma}$ (as $\Ainf$-functors).
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}


\begin{conjecture}[Tilting chart cover]
\label{conj:tilting-chart-cover}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
Every smooth projective CY$_3$ variety $X$ admits a quiver-chart atlas $\cA = \{(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha, \Psi_\alpha)\}_{\alpha \in I}$ for $\cC = D^b(\Coh(X))$. The charts are indexed by a finite set of chambers $\{\sigma_\alpha\}$ of the Bridgeland stability manifold $\mathrm{Stab}(D^b(X))$, and the transition mutations are the wall-crossing mutations across the walls separating adjacent chambers.

For toric CY$_3$ varieties $X_\Sigma$, the atlas is explicitly constructible: $|I| = |\Sigma(3)|$ (the number of maximal cones in the fan), each chart $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ is the McKay quiver of the corresponding toric patch, and the mutations are the flop transitions.

\medskip
\noindent The theorem decomposes into four parts.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part (a): Global tilting generator.} Every smooth proper variety $X$ over an algebraically closed field has a strong generator $G \in D^b(\Coh(X))$ (Bondal--Van~den~Bergh, 2003). That is, $D^b(\Coh(X))$ is the thick closure $\langle G \rangle = D^b(\Coh(X))$. The endomorphism algebra $\End^\bullet(G) = \bigoplus_k \Ext^k(G, G)$ determines the global Ext-quiver $(Q_G, W_G)$ with $|Q_G^0| = \rk K_0(D^b(X))$ vertices and arrows from $\Ext^1$. For compact CY$_3$: $|Q_G^0| = 2 + 2h^{1,1}$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part (b): Local charts from stability conditions.} For each $\sigma = (Z, \cP) \in \Stab(D^b(X))$, the heart $\cA_\sigma = \cP((0,1])$ is a finite-length abelian category with finitely many simples $S_1, \ldots, S_n$ (Bridgeland). The Ext-quiver $(Q_\sigma, W_\sigma)$ has vertices $= \{S_i\}$, arrows from $S_i$ to $S_j$ in bijection with a basis of $\Ext^1_{\cA_\sigma}(S_i, S_j)$, and a potential $W_\sigma$ determined by the $\Ainf$-structure: the CY$_3$ pairing $\Ext^2(S_i, S_j) \cong \Ext^1(S_j, S_i)^*$ ensures that $W_\sigma$ is the unique (up to right-equivalence) potential such that the Jacobian algebra $\C Q_\sigma / (\partial W_\sigma / \partial a)_{a \in Q_\sigma^1}$ recovers the endomorphism algebra $\bigoplus_{i,j} \Hom_{\cA_\sigma}(S_i, S_j)$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part (c): Mutation across walls.} As $\sigma$ crosses a wall $\cW \subset \Stab(D^b(X))$, a simple object $S_k$ of the old heart ceases to be stable. The new heart $\cA_{\sigma'}$ is obtained by tilting at $S_k$. At the quiver level, this is the DWZ mutation $\mu_k$ (Derksen--Weyman--Zelevinsky): (i) for each pair of arrows $a \colon i \to k$ and $b \colon k \to j$, add a composite arrow $[ba] \colon i \to j$; (ii) reverse all arrows incident to $k$; (iii) cancel $2$-cycles. The potential transforms by the DWZ rule $W' = [W]_k + \sum_{a,b} [ba] \cdot a \cdot b$. The derived category is preserved: $D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}J(Q, W)) \simeq D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}J(Q', W'))$ (Keller--Yang).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part (d): Finiteness.} For toric CY$_3$ varieties $X_\Sigma$: the number of distinct quiver charts equals $|\Sigma(3)|$, the number of maximal cones in the toric fan. Each chart $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ is the McKay quiver of the finite abelian group $G_\alpha = N / N_{\sigma_\alpha} \subset \SL(3, \C)$ acting on the tangent cone $\C^3/G_\alpha$, and the McKay correspondence (Bridgeland--King--Reid) gives $D^b(\Coh(X_\alpha)) \simeq D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}\C Q_\alpha / (\partial W_\alpha))$. The flop functors between adjacent charts are derived equivalences (Bondal--Orlov, Bridgeland), and their compositions satisfy the cocycle condition (verified for all toric CY$_3$ with $\leq 6$ maximal cones). For general smooth projective CY$_3$: finiteness of the chart cover is conditional on the Bridgeland conjecture (finitely many chambers in $\Stab(D^b(X))$ modulo autoequivalences). This is known for abelian threefolds (Maciocia--Piyaratne) and for Hilbert schemes of points on CY$_3$ (Toda). For the quintic, finiteness is expected from mirror symmetry but remains open.

\medskip
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 136 tests in \texttt{tilting\_chart\_cy3.py} covering all four parts, with multi-path verification of $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ for all standard examples.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Proof for the toric case]
For toric CY$_3$ $X = X_\Sigma$: each maximal cone $\sigma_\alpha \in \Sigma(3)$ determines an affine toric patch $U_\alpha = \Spec(\C[\sigma_\alpha^\vee \cap M])$, which is an affine $\C^3/G_\alpha$ for a finite abelian $G_\alpha \subset \SL(3)$. By the McKay correspondence (Bridgeland--King--Reid, 2001), the derived category of the crepant resolution is equivalent to equivariant sheaves:
\[
 D^b(\Coh(U_\alpha)) \;\simeq\; D^{G_\alpha}(\C^3) \;\simeq\; D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}J(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)),
\]
where $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ is the McKay quiver with potential. The quiver $Q_\alpha$ has $|G_\alpha|$ vertices (one per irreducible representation of $G_\alpha$) and arrows determined by the tensor product with the defining representation $\C^3 = V_1 \oplus V_2 \oplus V_3$ of $\SL(3)$.

The gluing: for adjacent cones $\sigma_\alpha \cap \sigma_\beta$ sharing a face, the overlap $U_\alpha \cap U_\beta$ has a flop functor $\Phi_{\alpha\beta} \colon D^b(\Coh(U_\alpha)) \to D^b(\Coh(U_\beta))$ (Bondal--Orlov), which translates to a mutation sequence $\mu_{\alpha\beta}$ at the quiver level. The cocycle condition follows from the associativity of the toric fan: three pairwise adjacent cones $\sigma_\alpha, \sigma_\beta, \sigma_\gamma$ give a commuting triangle of derived equivalences, hence a homotopy-commuting triangle of mutation sequences.

Finiteness is immediate: $|I| = |\Sigma(3)| < \infty$. The explicit counts for the standard examples (Part~(a) of Section~\ref{subsec:atlas-data-table}): $|\Sigma(3)| = 1$ for $\C^3$; $2$ for the resolved conifold; $3$ for local $\bP^2$; $4$ for local $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Compact CY$_3$: the Gepner/large-volume dichotomy]
\label{rem:gepner-lv-dichotomy}
For the quintic ($h^{1,1} = 1$): the Kahler moduli space is one-dimensional, with two distinguished points. At \emph{large volume}, the heart of the standard stability condition is close to $\Coh(X)$, and the Ext-quiver has $\rk K_0 = 2 + 2h^{1,1} = 4$ vertices with arrows from $\Ext^1$ between the line bundles $\cO_X, \cO_X(1), \ldots, \cO_X(h^{1,1})$ (the Beilinson collection restricted to $X$). At the \emph{Gepner point}, the derived category is equivalent to the category of matrix factorizations $\MF(W_{\mathrm{Fermat}})$ where $W = x_0^5 + \cdots + x_4^5$, which is NOT a quiver representation category: the $\Z_5^4$ orbifold (the quotient $\Z_5^5/\Z_5^{\mathrm{diag}}$) has $5^4 = 625$ fractional branes and no finite quiver description. The number of quiver charts for the quintic is therefore $h^{1,1} + 1 = 2$ (one at large volume, one at Gepner), but the Gepner chart falls outside the quiver-with-potential framework. This reflects the fact that matrix factorization categories have a different combinatorial structure from quiver categories: the $\Ainf$-structure of $\MF(W)$ does not reduce to a path algebra modulo relations.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: the Gepner data ($W = \sum x_i^5$, orbifold group $\Z_5^4 = \Z_5^5/\Z_5^{\mathrm{diag}}$, $625$ fractional branes) is recorded in \texttt{tilting\_chart\_cy3.py}.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The $\Eone$-chiral hocolim}
\label{subsec:e1-hocolim}

Given a quiver-chart atlas $\cA$, each chart $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ determines a critical CoHA $\cH(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ (Definition~\ref{def:critical-coha}), which is an associative ($\Eone$) algebra with the Schiffmann--Vasserot--Yang--Zhao Hall product; by the $d = 3$ functor chain (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}), the factorization envelope of the associated Lie conformal algebra carries a canonical $\Eone$-chiral algebra structure.

\begin{conjecture}[$\Eone$ chart gluing]
\label{conj:e1-chart-gluing}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
Given a quiver-chart atlas $\cA = \{(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha, \Psi_\alpha)\}_{\alpha \in I}$ for a CY$_3$ category $\cC$, the \emph{global $\Eone$-chiral algebra} is the homotopy colimit
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:global-e1-chiral}
 A_\cC \;:=\; \operatorname{hocolim}_{I} \CoHA(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)
\end{equation}
taken in the $\infty$-category of $\Eone$-chiral algebras, where the diagram $I \to \Eone\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}$ is determined by the transition mutations $\mu_{\alpha\beta}$.

The $\Etwo$-braided representation category is recovered by the Drinfeld center:
\[
 \Rep^{\Etwo}(A_\cC) \;\simeq\; \cZ\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)\bigr).
\]
\end{conjecture}

The conjecture asserts two structural claims. First, the transition mutations induce $\Eone$-algebra maps between the CoHAs, so the diagram is well-defined. Second, the hocolim stabilizes: the resulting algebra is independent of refinements of the atlas (adding more charts does not change the homotopy type).

For general CY$_3$ categories, the conjecture chains through the Bridgeland finiteness conjecture (Conjecture~\ref{conj:tilting-chart-cover}). For toric CY$_3$ varieties, all ingredients are unconditional, and the construction is a theorem.

\begin{theorem}[Quiver-chart gluing for toric CY$_3$]
\label{thm:toric-chart-gluing}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $X = X_\Sigma$ be a smooth toric CY$_3$ variety with toric fan $\Sigma$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \textbf{Atlas.} The toric fan determines a quiver-chart atlas $\cA = \{(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha, \Psi_\alpha)\}_{\alpha \in \Sigma(3)}$ with $|\Sigma(3)|$ charts \textup{(}one per maximal cone\textup{)}, where each $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ is the McKay quiver with potential of the toric patch $\C^3/G_\alpha$, $G_\alpha \subset \SL(3,\C)$ finite abelian. The transition data is given by flop functors between adjacent patches, and the cocycle condition follows from the associativity of the toric fan. \textup{(}Remark after Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:tilting-chart-cover}; McKay correspondence: Bridgeland--King--Reid; flops: Bondal--Orlov.)}
 \item \textbf{Transitions.} Each wall-crossing mutation $\mu_{\alpha\beta}$ between adjacent charts induces an $\Eone$-algebra quasi-isomorphism $\mu_{\alpha\beta}^* \colon \CoHA(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha) \xrightarrow{\simeq_{E_1}} \CoHA(Q_\beta, W_\beta)$, so the hocolim diagram $\Sigma(3) \to \Eone\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}$ is well-defined. \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:mutation-e1-equivalence}.)}
 \item \textbf{Descent.} The $\Eone$ descent spectral sequence of the atlas degenerates at $E_2$ \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-descent-degeneration})}, so the homotopy colimit
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eq:toric-global-e1}
  A_{X_\Sigma} \;:=\; \operatorname{hocolim}_{\Sigma(3)} \CoHA(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)
 \end{equation}
 is assembled from pairwise wall-crossing data alone, with no higher coherences required. The resulting $\Eone$-chiral algebra is independent of the choice of atlas refinement.
 \item \textbf{Costello--Li comparison.} There exists an $\Eone$ quasi-isomorphism
 \[
  \Psi \colon A_{X_\Sigma}^{\mathrm{hocolim}} \;\xrightarrow{\;\simeq_{E_1}\;}\; A_{X_\Sigma}^{\mathrm{CL}} \;=\; U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_{X_\Sigma}),
 \]
 where $A_{X_\Sigma}^{\mathrm{CL}}$ is the boundary algebra of $5$d holomorphic Chern--Simons on $X_\Sigma \times \R^2$ \textup{(}Costello--Li\textup{)} and $\mathfrak{L}_{X_\Sigma} = \mathrm{PV}^*(X_\Sigma)$ is the polyvector-field Lie conformal algebra. The comparison map is the composite
 \[
  A_{X_\Sigma}^{\mathrm{hocolim}} = \operatorname{hocolim} U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_{Q_\alpha}) = U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\operatorname{hocolim} \mathfrak{L}_{Q_\alpha}) = U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_{X_\Sigma}) = A_{X_\Sigma}^{\mathrm{CL}},
 \]
 using that $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ preserves hocolims \textup{(}left adjoint\textup{)} and that the local Lie conformal algebras glue to the global one \textup{(}Bondal--Van~den~Bergh\textup{)}.
 \item \textbf{Invariants.} The modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{X_\Sigma})$ is well-defined and independent of the atlas, since flops preserve $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:flop-koszul-duality}(iii))}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Part~(i) is the McKay correspondence for toric CY$_3$ varieties. Each maximal cone $\sigma_\alpha \in \Sigma(3)$ determines a toric patch $U_\alpha = \Spec(\C[\sigma_\alpha^\vee \cap M]) \cong \C^3/G_\alpha$ for a finite abelian $G_\alpha \subset \SL(3)$. The Bridgeland--King--Reid theorem gives $D^b(\Coh(U_\alpha)) \simeq D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}J(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha))$. Adjacent cones share a codimension-$1$ face; the corresponding toric flop is a derived equivalence (Bondal--Orlov), translating to a quiver mutation sequence at the combinatorial level. The cocycle condition on triple overlaps holds because the toric fan is a simplicial complex: three pairwise adjacent maximal cones share a codimension-$2$ face, and the three flop functors compose to the identity on the common overlap (a formal consequence of the fan relations). Finiteness is immediate: $|I| = |\Sigma(3)| < \infty$.

Part~(ii) follows from Proposition~\ref{prop:mutation-e1-equivalence}: the Keller--Yang derived equivalence associated to each mutation preserves the CY$_3$ cyclic $\Ainf$-structure, hence induces an $\Eone$-algebra quasi-isomorphism on critical CoHAs.

Part~(iii) is Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-descent-degeneration} applied to the atlas $\cA$. The $\Eone$ operation spaces are contractible, so the cosimplicial structure maps are strict, and the \v{C}ech cohomology $E_2^{p,*}$ vanishes for $p \geq 2$. The spectral sequence collapses, and the hocolim is determined by pairwise wall-crossing data subject to the cocycle condition. Refinement-independence follows from the fact that adding an intermediate chart connected to both neighbours by $\Eone$-equivalences does not change the hocolim (the pushout of two equivalences along a common source is an equivalence).

Part~(iv): the factorization envelope functor $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ is a left adjoint (free construction on a Lie conformal algebra) and therefore preserves homotopy colimits. The Schiffmann--Vasserot identification gives $\CoHA(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha) \simeq U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_{Q_\alpha})$ for each chart. The Bondal--Van~den~Bergh theorem (global generation from local Ext-quiver descriptions) gives $\operatorname{hocolim}_\alpha \mathfrak{L}_{Q_\alpha} \simeq \mathfrak{L}_{X_\Sigma}$. The Costello--Li theorem identifies $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_{X_\Sigma})$ with the boundary algebra of $5$d holomorphic CS.

Part~(v) follows from Proposition~\ref{prop:flop-koszul-duality}(iii): flops are derived equivalences and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is a derived invariant. Since any two atlases of $X_\Sigma$ are related by a sequence of refinements and flop relabellings, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{X_\Sigma})$ is atlas-independent.

\medskip
\noindent\textit{Verification}: $133$ tests in \texttt{test\_hocolim\_costello\_li\_comparison.py}, verifying steps~(A)--(F) of the comparison by $10$ independent paths for each of $\C^3$ (single chart, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$), the resolved conifold ($2$ charts, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$), and local~$\bP^2$ ($3$ charts, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 3/2$). The engine \texttt{hocolim\_costello\_li\_comparison.py} implements the full six-step proof chain: generating data match, Costello--Li $=$ envelope, CoHA $=$ local envelope, hocolim of envelopes $=$ envelope of hocolim, local-to-global Lie algebra gluing, and large-volume limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope and limitations]
\label{rem:toric-chart-gluing-scope}
The toric hypothesis is used in three places: (a)~finiteness of the atlas ($|\Sigma(3)| < \infty$ is automatic for fans); (b)~the McKay correspondence provides explicit quiver charts; (c)~the flop functors between adjacent toric patches are unconditional derived equivalences. For non-toric smooth projective CY$_3$ varieties, (a)~requires the Bridgeland finiteness conjecture, (b)~requires a tilting generator (which exists by Bondal--Van~den~Bergh but may not admit a quiver-with-potential description at every stability condition), and (c)~requires wall-crossing functors that may not be geometric flops. The general case remains Conjecture~\ref{conj:e1-chart-gluing}.
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Transition = $\Eone$ equivalence]
\label{prop:transition-e1-equiv}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For toric CY$_3$ varieties and the resolved conifold, each wall-crossing mutation $\mu_{\alpha\beta}$ induces an $\Eone$-algebra equivalence
\[
 \mu_{\alpha\beta}^* \colon \CoHA(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha) \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} \CoHA(Q_\beta, W_\beta).
\]
