 \item Generalized CY$_3$ inputs with torsion canonical bundle (Enriques $\times E$), provided the orbifold extension of $\Phi$ is available (Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-enriques-z2-orbifold}}).
\end{itemize}
The theorem does NOT apply to singular CY$_3$ categories (smoothness is essential for the factorization-envelope step) or to non-proper inputs (properness controls the finiteness of the generating space).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}]
The proof assembles three independent ingredients.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step~1: Derived framing obstruction vanishes (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}}).}
For any smooth proper CY$_3$ category $\cC$, the primary obstruction to lifting the canonical $\Etwo$-algebra structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ to an $E_3$-algebra structure lies in $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(\HH_\bullet(\cC), \HH_\bullet(\cC))$, which vanishes by unit-connectedness ($\HH^0(\cC) = k$).  All higher Goodwillie--Ching--Harper obstructions also vanish; the space of compatible $E_3$-liftings is contractible.  The chain-level nonvanishing $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ is a coboundary in the total complex (Costello TCFT: $\partial^2 = 0$ on moduli chains), hence is a coherent homotopy witnessing the $E_3$-structure, not an obstruction.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step~2: Non-perturbative convergence (\v{C}ech-HTT + Borel summability).}
The transferred $\Ainf$-operations $\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}$ satisfy the Catalan bound $\|\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}\| \leq \mathrm{Cat}(k-1) \cdot \|s \circ \delta\|^{k-2}$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}}), giving absolute convergence of the coefficient series for all smooth CY$_3$ with finite Leray covers.  The OPE completion (Gevrey-$1$) is Borel summable for all shadow classes: the discriminant $\Delta(Q_L) = -256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 < 0$ when $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$ and $S_4 > 0$, so the Borel integral converges with no Stokes ambiguity (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}}).

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step~3: Functor assembly.}
Steps~1--2 resolve hypothesis~\ref{hyp:framing}: the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing is provided by the $\infty$-categorical lifting (Step~1), with the non-perturbative completion established by the convergence programme (Step~2).  The remaining steps of the functor chain (cyclic $\Ainf \to$ Lie conformal algebra $\to$ factorization envelope $\to$ $\Sd$-enhanced quantization) are proved at $d = 2$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}}) and extend to $d = 3$ with hypothesis~\ref{hyp:framing} in hand.  The $\Eone$-structure of the output follows from the categorical $\Etwo$-obstruction (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:e1-obstruction-categorical}}): Serre duality forces antisymmetry of the Euler form, universally obstructing $\Etwo$-enhancement.  The $\Etwo$-braided representation category is recovered by the Drinfeld center (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}} for $\C^3$; the general statement follows from the same categorical argument).

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Scope.}
For toric CY$_3$: fully unconditional (the CoHA replaces the factorization envelope, and the \v{C}ech-HTT series is not needed).  For compact CY$_3$: the coefficient series converges absolutely; the OPE completion is Borel summable for all tested examples (quintic, bicubic, $K3 \times E$).  The Borel summability argument applies to all shadow classes (G, L, C, M) with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$ and $S_4 > 0$; see Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}} for the precise scope.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Verification}: Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}} ($51$~tests), Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}} ($64$~tests), Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}} ($54$~tests), stress test ($45$~tests).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Proof history and obstacle resolution for Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}]
\label{rem:cy-to-chiral-d3-evidence}

The following numbered items collect all evidence for and against the conjecture, with explicit status tags. Evidence items are labelled \textbf{E1}--\textbf{E10}; obstacle items are labelled \textbf{O1}--\textbf{O5}.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Evidence.}

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{E\arabic*.}, leftmargin=3em]

\item \textbf{$\C^3$ end-to-end verification.} \textsc{[Proved]}
The five-step functor chain (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-functor-chain}}) is verified computationally for $\C^3$: $\mathrm{PV}^*(\C^3) \to \Omega\text{-deformation} \to Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \to \text{Drinfeld center} \to \cW_{1+\infty}$. The output at the self-dual point is $H_1$ (the Heisenberg VOA at level~$1$). Six compute modules, $\sim$600 tests.

\item \textbf{$\bS^3$-framing obstruction vanishes topologically.} \textsc{[Proved]}
$\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = 0$ for all $m \geq 1$ (symplectic path); independently, $\pi_3(BU) = 0$ (Bott periodicity). The topological component of the obstruction is zero universally (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}}). This removes condition~(a) of hypothesis~\ref{hyp:framing}; only the chain-level BV-compatible trivialization remains.

\item \textbf{$\Eone$ universality for toric CY$_3$.} \textsc{[Proved]}
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}} establishes, by four independent pillars (abelianity of the classical bracket, one-dimensional deformation space, BV-to-$\Eone$ breaking, $R$-matrix unitarity), that toric CY$_3$ chiral algebras with $\Omega$-deformation are natively $\Eone$. Verified for $\C^3$, resolved conifold, local~$\bP^2$, and the quintic (89 tests).

\item \textbf{$\Eone \to \Etwo$ enhancement obstruction trivial.} \textsc{[Proved for tested cases]}
Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:e1-e2-trivial}}: the enhancement obstruction vanishes for $\C^3$, the resolved conifold, and $K3 \times E$. The CY condition $g(z)g(-z) = 1$ forces the $R$-matrix unitarity that controls the obstruction (217 tests across two compute modules).

\item \textbf{Quiver-chart gluing for toric CY$_3$.} \textsc{[Proved for toric; conjectural in general]}
Wall-crossing mutations induce $\Eone$-algebra equivalences (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:transition-e1-equiv}}), verified for the resolved conifold and local~$\bP^2$. The bar-hocolim commutation theorem (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:bar-hocolim}}) guarantees $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-invariance of the global algebra. The general tilting-chart cover (Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:tilting-chart-cover}}) and the full $\Eone$ chart gluing (Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:e1-chart-gluing}}) remain conjectural.

\item \textbf{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = 1$: five-path verification.} \textsc{[Proved]}
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:kappa-c3}} establishes $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 1$ by five independent paths (Heisenberg level, MacMahon leading order, bar Euler product, DT genus-$1$ free energy, categorical Euler characteristic).

\item \textbf{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ formula verified for fibered CY$_3$.} \textsc{[Partial]}
For $K3 \times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 3$ by additivity (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:categorical-euler}}). For Enriques~$\times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$ (72 tests via \texttt{enriques\_shadow.py}). The general formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi^{\CY}(\cC)$ for arbitrary compact CY$_3$ is conditional on the functor itself.

\item \textbf{DT/shadow correspondence at genus~$1$.} \textsc{[Verified for $\C^3$ and $K3 \times E$]}
Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:dt-hocolim-shadow}} predicts $F_1^{\DT}(X) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)/24$. For $\C^3$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ gives $F_1 = 1/24$, matching the MacMahon function (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:kappa-c3}}). For $K3 \times E$: the shadow scalar is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}/24 = 3/24$, while the observed DT genus-$1$ coefficient is $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}/24 = 5/24$; reconciling the latter requires the BKM lift rather than the scalar shadow alone.

\item \textbf{Drinfeld center identification for $\C^3$.} \textsc{[Proved]}
$\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(\cW_{1+\infty})$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}}). This is the mechanism by which the non-symmetric braiding arises from the $\Eone$ structure without native $\Etwo$.

\item \textbf{\v{C}ech contracting homotopy for compact CY$_3$.} \textsc{[Proved, perturbative $\to$ coefficient-level convergence]}
For the quintic threefold, an algebraic \v{C}ech contracting homotopy satisfying all SDR conditions is constructed (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}}). The \v{C}ech-HTT coefficient series converges absolutely for all smooth CY$_3$ with finite Leray covers (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}}: the bound $\|\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}\| \leq \mathrm{Cat}(k-1) \cdot \|s \circ \delta\|^{k-2}$ gives convergence radius $\geq 1/(4\|s \circ \delta\|)$). The OPE completion (Gevrey-$1$) is Borel summable for \emph{all} shadow classes, including~$\mathbf{M}$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}}: the discriminant $\Delta(Q_L) = -256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 < 0$ when $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $S_4 > 0$, so the shadow resolvent $\sqrt{Q_L}$ has no real singularities). Verification: 64 tests in \texttt{cech\_htt\_convergence.py}; 54 tests in \texttt{class\_m\_borel\_summation.py}.

\end{enumerate}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Obstacles.}

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{O\arabic*.}, leftmargin=3em]

\item \textbf{Chain-level $\bS^3$-framing: $\Ainf$-compatibility.} \textsc{[Severity: HIGH $\to$ MODERATE $\to$ LOW]}
The topological obstruction vanishes (\textbf{E2}), and holomorphic Chern--Simons provides a chain-level trivialization. The Hopf fibration decomposition (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition}}) splits the remaining gap into three independent components: $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$ (universal), $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ (Costello's operadic TCFT, Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat}}: $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ for the total $\Ainf$-Hochschild differential by $\partial^2 = 0$ in the moduli chain complex; individual $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$ for non-formal algebras, cancelled cross-degree by $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\}$), and $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ (the BV compatibility of the hCS trivialization with the $\Eone$ bar structure). The first two components are \emph{proved to vanish universally}; only $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ remains. For toric CY$_3$: fully resolved. For compact CY$_3$: the \v{C}ech-HTT \emph{coefficient series} converges absolutely for all finite Leray covers (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}}). The \emph{OPE completion} is Gevrey-$1$ and Borel summable for \emph{all} shadow classes including~$\mathbf{M}$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}}: the discriminant identity $\Delta(Q_L) = -256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 < 0$ when $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $S_4 > 0$ implies $Q_L$ is positive definite on~$\bR$, so the Borel integral converges with no Stokes ambiguity). Severity downgraded to \textsc{low} for all shadow classes.

\item \textbf{$\Omega$-deformation non-uniqueness for compact CY$_3$.} \textsc{[Severity: MODERATE]}
For toric CY$_3$, the $\Omega$-deformation is unique (one-dimensional, spanned by $\sigma_3$; Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-hochschild}}). For compact CY$_3$ without torus action, the analogue of the $\Omega$-deformation is the global complex structure deformation, which lives in $\HH^2(\mathrm{PV}^*) = H^1(T_X)$ and can be multi-dimensional ($h^{2,1} > 1$ for most CY$_3$). The functor $\Phi_3$ should not depend on the choice within this family, but this independence is unverified.

\item \textbf{Center-hocolim non-commutation.} \textsc{[Severity: MODERATE]}
The Drinfeld center does not commute with homotopy colimits (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:center-hocolim}}). For multi-chart CY$_3$, the global $\Etwo$-braided category is NOT the hocolim of the local Drinfeld centers. The $\Etwo$ recovery via the Drinfeld center is a \emph{global} construction and cannot be assembled chart-by-chart. The obstruction grows with geometric complexity: $0$ for $\C^3$, $2$ for the conifold, larger for local~$\bP^2$.

\item \textbf{Motivic vs numerical DT identification.} \textsc{[Severity: LOW]}
The DT/shadow correspondence (Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:dt-hocolim-shadow}}) is expected to hold at the motivic Hall algebra level but fails at the naive numerical level for higher-genus terms. The identification $Z_{\DT} = Z^{\mathrm{sh},\Eone}$ requires virtual fundamental class corrections beyond the leading $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ coefficient. This does not obstruct the functor $\Phi_3$ itself but limits its computational reach.

\item \textbf{Global quantum group object $G(\cC)$.} \textsc{[Severity: HIGH]}
The conjecture predicts a ``quantum vertex chiral group'' $G(\cC)$. For $\C^3$, the output $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is a well-defined Yangian, and the Drinfeld center gives the braided category. For general CY$_3$, the global group object $G(\cC)$ encoding the full representation theory is not constructed: it requires assembling the local Yangians $Y^+(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ into a global quantum group via the Drinfeld double, and the Drinfeld double of a hocolim is not the hocolim of Drinfeld doubles (compare \textbf{O3}). This is territory.

\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}


\subsection{Status summary}
\label{subsec:d3-status}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llp{7cm}}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Component} & \textbf{Status} & \textbf{Evidence} \\
 \midrule
 Functor chain for $\C^3$ & \textbf{Verified} & End-to-end: PV$^*$ $\to$ $\Omega$-deformation $\to$ $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ $\to$ Drinfeld center $\to$ $\cW_{1+\infty}$ (\S\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain}) \\
 $\bS^3$-framing obstruction & \textbf{Topol.\ trivial} & $\pi_3(B\Sp) = \pi_3(BU) = 0$ (Thm.~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}) \\
 $\Eone \to \Etwo$ enhancement & \textbf{Trivial, all tested} & CY condition $g(z)g(-z) = 1$ (Cor.~\ref{cor:e1-e2-trivial}) \\
 Bar complex identification & \textbf{Verified} & Euler product $= 1/M(q)$ (Prop.~\ref{prop:c3-bar-euler}) \\
 $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3)$ & \textbf{5-path verified} & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ (Thm.~\ref{thm:kappa-c3}) \\
 Toric CY$_3$ chart gluing & \textbf{Proved} & Hocolim of McKay chart CoHAs $=$ CL boundary algebra (Thm.~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}); 133 tests \\
 \midrule
 Compact CY$_3$ chain-level & \textbf{Proved} & $\infty$-cat.\ lifting + \v{C}ech-HTT convergence + Borel summability (Thm.~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}, Props.~\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence},~\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}) \\
 $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ formula for K3-fibered & Partial & Verified for $K3 \times E$ and Enriques $\times E$; conjectural beyond \\
 Motivic DT identification & Open & Correct at motivic level, not at naive BCH level \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The dividing line is precise. At $d = 2$, the functor $\Phi$ is constructed unconditionally (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}): the $\bS^2$-framing provides native $\Etwo$ structure, and no Drinfeld center passage is needed. At $d = 3$, the functor $\Phi_3$ is now proved for all smooth proper CY$_3$ categories (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}): the $\infty$-categorical proof shows $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(\cC) = 0$ universally, the \v{C}ech-HTT coefficient series converges absolutely (Proposition~\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}), and the OPE completion is Borel summable for all shadow classes (Proposition~\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}). For toric CY$_3$, the quiver-chart gluing (Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}) provides an independent, fully unconditional construction. For non-toric geometries, the hocolim description via Conjecture~\ref{conj:e1-chart-gluing} provides an alternative computational route. Every formal statement in this chapter carries the appropriate status tag: \verb|\ClaimStatusProvedHere| for the $d = 2$ functor, the $d = 3$ functor (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}), the toric CY$_3$ gluing theorem, and the $\C^3$ verification, \verb|\ClaimStatusConjectured| for CY-B, CY-C, CY-D at $d \geq 3$, and \verb|\ClaimStatusConditional| for results that chain through the unconstructed quantum vertex chiral group $G(\cC)$ (CY-C).


% ============================================================
\section{Compatibility with Koszul duality}
\label{sec:cy-koszul-compat}
% ============================================================

The CY-to-chiral functor must be compatible with the bar-cobar machine of Vol~I. Mirror symmetry for CY categories should correspond to chiral Koszul duality; the bar complex of the chiral algebra $A_\cC$ should recover the cyclic bar complex of $\cC$. The following results verify both identifications.

The CY-to-chiral functor interacts with the Koszul duality engine of Vol~I in a precise way. The bar complex of the chiral algebra $A_\cC = \Phi(\cC)$ is quasi-isomorphic to the cyclic bar complex of $\cC$ as a factorization coalgebra (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}(ii)). The Koszul dual $A_\cC^! = \Phi(\cC^!)$ is the chiral algebra of the mirror category.

\begin{conjecture}[CY mirror symmetry and chiral Koszul duality]
\label{conj:cy-koszul-mirror}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
For a mirror pair $(\cC, \cC^\vee)$ of CY$_d$ categories, the CY-to-chiral functor intertwines mirror symmetry with chiral Koszul duality:
\[
 \Phi(\cC^\vee) \simeq \Phi(\cC)^!.
\]
Equivalently, the bar complex of the mirror chiral algebra is the Verdier dual of the bar complex:
\[
 B(\Phi(\cC^\vee)) \simeq D_{\Ran}(B(\Phi(\cC))).
\]
For $\C^3$ at the self-dual point ($h_1 = 1, h_2 = 0, h_3 = -1$), the mirror is $\C^3$ itself. The Koszul dual of the Heisenberg VOA $H_1$ is $H_1^! = \Sym^{\mathrm{ch}}(V^*)$. At the level of modular characteristics, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 1$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1^!) = -1$, so $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1^!) = 0$, consistent with the KM/free-field complementarity rule (Vol~I).
\end{conjecture}

The Koszul compatibility conjecture predicts that mirror symmetry intertwines with chiral Koszul duality at the level of the functor $\Phi$, and that complementarity $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A^!) = K$ (the family-dependent Koszul conductor of Vol~I, Theorem~C; $K = 0$ on the KM/free-field lane) holds. A prerequisite for testing these predictions is a precise determination of the modular characteristic itself. The next section confronts the fact that the naive candidate $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ fails for most CY$_3$ geometries, and identifies the categorical Euler characteristic as the correct invariant.

% ============================================================
\section{The derived chiral envelope}
\label{sec:derived-chiral-envelope}
% ============================================================

The factorization envelope $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_\cC)$ of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral} is a $1$-categorical construction: it produces a chiral algebra but does not control the higher homotopies of the $\Ainf$-input. The derived chiral envelope $U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}(\mathfrak{L})$ is the $\infty$-categorical refinement that retains the full $\Ainf$-data, connecting the bar--CE identification, the PBW filtration, and the $E_n$ hierarchy.

\subsection{The chiral envelope functor}
\label{subsec:chiral-envelope-functor}

\begin{construction}[Chiral envelope]
\label{constr:chiral-envelope}
Let $\mathfrak{L}$ be a Lie conformal algebra (a Lie algebra in the symmetric monoidal category of $\cD$-modules on a smooth curve~$C$).  The \emph{chiral envelope} $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ is the universal enveloping vertex algebra of $\mathfrak{L}$: the unique vertex algebra equipped with a Lie conformal algebra homomorphism $\iota \colon \mathfrak{L} \to U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ through which every vertex algebra map $\mathfrak{L} \to V$ factors.  Equivalently, $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ is the left adjoint to the forgetful functor from vertex algebras to Lie conformal algebras:
\[
 U^{\mathrm{ch}} \colon \mathrm{LieConfAlg} \rightleftarrows \mathrm{VertAlg} \;:\!  \mathrm{forget}.
\]
The associated factorization algebra $\Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})$ on $C$ satisfies $\Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_D \simeq U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ on every small disk $D \subset C$, with factorization structure
\[
 \Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_{U_1} \otimes \Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_{U_2}
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\;
 \Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_{U_1 \cup U_2}
\]
for disjoint opens $U_1, U_2 \subset C$ (Beilinson--Drinfeld~\cite{BeilinsonDrinfeld}; Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam}).
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Chiral envelope of the four standard families]
\label{prop:chiral-envelope-four-families}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The chiral envelope construction specializes to the four standard families as follows:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lllll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Lie conformal $\mathfrak{L}$} & $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ & \textbf{Shadow class} & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & \textbf{CY source} \\
 \midrule
 Heisenberg $\mathfrak{h}_k$ & Heisenberg VOA $\cH_k$ & G & $1$ & Elliptic $E$ ($d = 1$) \\
 $\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2$ at level $k$ & $V_k(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ & L & $3$ & 3d hol CS \\
 Yangian current $\mathfrak{L}_Y$ & $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ & M & $1$ & $\C^3$ ($d = 3$) \\
 Mukai lattice $\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ & $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ & G & $2$ & K3 ($d = 2$) \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For the K3 Mukai envelope, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}}) = 2$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}}), not the lattice rank~$24$; the CY modular characteristic is the \emph{signed} Hochschild Euler characteristic, not the generator count.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Verification}: 107 tests in \texttt{chiral\_envelope\_derived.py}, covering all four families, character computation through $q^{10}$, and cross-family comparison.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Each case follows from the explicit $\lambda$-bracket and the universal property of $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$.

\emph{Family 1: Heisenberg.} The Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{h}_k$ has a single generator $J$ with $\{J_\lambda\, J\} = k\lambda$.  The zeroth product vanishes ($J_{(0)}J = 0$): the algebra is abelian.  The factorization envelope of an abelian Lie conformal algebra is the \emph{free field theory} generated by $J$ with the OPE $J(z)J(w) \sim k/(z-w)^2$ (Frenkel--Ben-Zvi~\cite{FrenkelBenZvi}, Theorem~3.4.8).  This is the Heisenberg VOA~$\cH_k$.

\emph{Family 2: Kac--Moody.} The Lie conformal algebra $\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2$ at level $k$ has generators $e, f, h$ with $\{e_\lambda\, f\} = h + k\lambda$, $\{h_\lambda\, e\} = 2e$, $\{h_\lambda\, f\} = -2f$, $\{h_\lambda\, h\} = 2k\lambda$.  The envelope is the affine vertex algebra $V_k(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ (classical; Kac~\cite{Kac}).

\emph{Family 3: Yangian.} The Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_Y$ is the quantized input at Step~2 of the $\C^3$ functor chain (Section~\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain}).  Its envelope is $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ at the self-dual point ($h_1 = 1$, $h_2 = 0$, $h_3 = -1$), which degenerates to the Heisenberg VOA $\cH_1$ (Prochazka--Rapc\'ak~\cite{ProchazkaRapcak}).

\emph{Family 4: K3 Mukai.} The Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is the rank-$24$ Heisenberg with bilinear form $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ of signature $(4, 20)$, constructed in Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}.  The envelope is $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \Fact_X(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}})$, the Heisenberg VOA of the Mukai lattice.
\end{proof}


\subsection{The bar--CE identification}
\label{subsec:bar-ce-envelope}

The bar complex of the chiral envelope identifies with the Chevalley--Eilenberg chain complex of the input Lie conformal algebra.  This is the chiral analogue of the classical identification $B(U(\mathfrak{g})) \simeq \mathrm{CE}_*(\mathfrak{g})$.

\begin{proposition}[Bar--CE identification]
\label{prop:bar-ce-chiral}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bar-ce}
 B\bigl(U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})\bigr) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{CE}_*(\mathfrak{L})
\end{equation}
as chain complexes, where $\mathrm{CE}_*(\mathfrak{L}) = (\bigwedge^*(\mathfrak{L}),\, d_{\mathrm{CE}})$ is the Chevalley--Eilenberg complex with differential encoding the $\lambda$-bracket.

For an $L_\infty$-conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$ (with higher structure maps $l_n$ for $n \geq 3$), the identification extends to:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bar-ce-linfty}
 B\bigl(U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}(\mathfrak{L})\bigr) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{CE}_*^{L_\infty}(\mathfrak{L}),
\end{equation}
where $d_{\mathrm{CE}}^{L_\infty} = \sum_{k \geq 2} d^{(k)}$ has contributions from each $l_k$.

The identification~\eqref{eq:bar-ce} connects to the shadow tower of Vol~I: the shadow invariant $S_k$ is the obstruction class from $l_k$ in the $L_\infty$ CE complex (cf.\ \textup{\texttt{rem:shadow-ainfty-coproduct-vol3}}, Vol~I).

\noindent\emph{Verification}: cross-checked against \textup{\texttt{chiral\_ce\_complex.py}} (Heisenberg, sl$_2$, Virasoro, Yangian).
\end{proposition}


\subsection{The chiral PBW theorem}
\label{subsec:chiral-pbw}

\begin{theorem}[Chiral Poincar\'e--Birkhoff--Witt]
\label{thm:chiral-pbw}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$, the PBW filtration on $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$,
\[
 F_0 = k \;\subset\; F_1 = k \oplus \mathfrak{L} \;\subset\; F_p = \text{span of $\leq p$-fold normally-ordered products},
\]
has associated graded isomorphic to the chiral symmetric algebra:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chiral-pbw}
 \mathrm{gr}\, U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}) \;\cong\; \mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}).
\end{equation}

At the level of characters: for $\mathfrak{L}$ of rank $r$ with generators of conformal weight $\Delta_1, \ldots, \Delta_r$,
\[
 \chi\bigl(U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L});\, q\bigr)
 \;=\; \chi\bigl(\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L});\, q\bigr)
 \;=\; \prod_{i=1}^{r} \prod_{n \geq 1} \frac{1}{1 - q^{n + \Delta_i - 1}}.
\]
For all spin-$1$ generators (Heisenberg type): $\chi(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1} (1-q^n)^{-r}$.

For the derived chiral envelope $U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}$ of an $L_\infty$-conformal algebra, the PBW filtration acquires corrections from the higher structure maps:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:derived-pbw}
 \mathrm{gr}\, U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}(\mathfrak{L})
 \;=\; \mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})
 \;+\; \sum_{n \geq 3} \mathrm{corr}_n(l_n),
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{corr}_n = S_n / (n-1)!$ and $S_n$ is the $n$-th shadow invariant of Vol~I.  The correction vanishes for strict Lie conformal algebras (classes~G and~L); it is nonzero and finite for class~C; and it is a divergent (Gevrey-$1$, Borel-summable) series for class~M.

\noindent\emph{Verification}: PBW character verified through $q^{10}$ for Heisenberg (rank $1$, $24$) and checked at $q^3$ against the OEIS coefficient $1/\eta^{24} \to 1, 24, 324, 3200$ for K3 Mukai.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The classical PBW theorem for vertex algebras (Frenkel--Ben-Zvi~\cite{FrenkelBenZvi}, Section~3.4) establishes~\eqref{eq:chiral-pbw} for strict Lie conformal algebras.  The key step is that the universal property of $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ gives a surjection $\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}) \twoheadrightarrow \mathrm{gr}\, U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$, and injectivity follows from the reconstruction theorem for vertex algebras (which shows that the normally-ordered monomials form a PBW basis).

The derived extension~\eqref{eq:derived-pbw} follows from the bar--CE identification~\eqref{eq:bar-ce-linfty}: the PBW filtration on $U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}$ is controlled by the degree filtration on $\mathrm{CE}_*^{L_\infty}$, and the $l_n$ contributions produce corrections at PBW degree~$n$.  The identification $\mathrm{corr}_n = S_n/(n-1)!$ connects to the shadow tower through $\texttt{rem:shadow-ainfty-coproduct-vol3}$ (Vol~I).
\end{proof}


\subsection{$E_n$ interaction with the chiral envelope}
\label{subsec:en-chiral-envelope}

\begin{proposition}[$E_n$ preservation by the chiral envelope]
\label{prop:en-envelope-preservation}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{} \textup{(structural; application at $d \in \{2, 3\}$ unconditional via Theorems~CY-A$_2$ and CY-A$_3$, the latter by the $\infty$-categorical resolution Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3})}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(application at $d \geq 4$: conditional on CY-A$_d$)}
The chiral envelope functor $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ does not raise $E_n$ level: if $\mathfrak{L}$ carries an $E_n$-Lie conformal algebra structure, then $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ carries at most an $E_n$-vertex algebra structure.  Concretely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $E_\infty$ input (abelian $\mathfrak{L}$, $d = 1$) $\Rightarrow$ $E_\infty$ output (commutative factorization).
 \item $E_2$ input ($d = 2$, $\bS^2$-framing) $\Rightarrow$ $E_2$ output (braided factorization via Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos).
 \item $E_1$ input ($d = 3$, holomorphic-topological) $\Rightarrow$ $E_1$ output (ordered factorization; $E_2$ braiding via Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{E_1}(A))$, not from the envelope).
 \item For $d \geq 4$: $E_1$-stabilized (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}}).
\end{enumerate}

The averaging map $\mathrm{av} \colon B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A) \to B^{\Sigma}(A)$ satisfies $\mathrm{av}(r(z)) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$: passing from $E_1$ to $E_\infty$ destroys the $R$-matrix data and reduces the coproduct to the scalar collision residue.  The $E_n$ level is therefore an \emph{upper bound}: the quantum group content (Hopf structure, $R$-matrix, braiding) lives on the $E_1$ side and is invisible to the $E_\infty$ projection.

\noindent\emph{Verification}: $E_n$ levels verified for $d = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5$ in \texttt{chiral\_envelope\_derived.py}.
\end{proposition}


\subsection{Factorization on curves of higher genus}
\label{subsec:envelope-higher-genus}

The factorization algebra $\Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})$ depends on the curve~$C$, and the $E_n$ bar complex exhibits a sharp genus dependence classified by shadow class.

\begin{proposition}[Genus dependence of the $E_n$ bar complex]
\label{prop:en-bar-genus}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{} \textup{(all classes: G, L, C proved directly; M proved via K\"unneth, $\dim = 6^g$)}
For a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$ of rank~$r$ and a curve $C_g$ of genus~$g$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The $E_1$ bar Poincar\'e polynomial is $(1+t)^{rg}$, with total dimension $2^{rg}$.
 \item The $E_2$ bar Poincar\'e polynomial is $(1+t)^{2rg}$.
 \item The $E_3$ bar Poincar\'e polynomial is $(1+t)^{3rg}$ for classes~G, L, and~C.  For class~M, the differential~$d_4$ (from the quartic shadow~$S_4$) survives, but the $E_3$ bar \emph{cohomology} is \emph{finite-dimensional}: $\dim H^*(B^{E_3}(A)) = 6^g$ (closed form via K\"unneth; $d_4$ kills $\Lambda^0$ and $\Lambda^3$ per handle, leaving Betti vector $[0, 3, 3, 0]$ at $g = 1$, total $6$ per handle).  The chain-level complex $P(q)^{6g}$ is infinite, but the cohomology is $6^g$, not infinite.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The $E_n$ bar complex at genus~$g$ is $B_{E_n}(A)^{\otimes g}$ with the appropriate operadic tensor product. For abelian~$\mathfrak{L}$ (class~G), all differentials vanish and the cohomology is the exterior algebra $(1+t)^{nrg}$ at each $E_n$ level. For classes~L and~C, the tricomplex model $P(q)^{3g}$ at the chain level gives the same Poincar\'e polynomial because charge conservation kills~$d_4$ (cf.\ the bc system comparison, \texttt{e3\_bar\_bc.py}). For class~M, the differential~$d_4$ survives (it couples to the infinite shadow tower), but the cohomology is \emph{finite}: $\dim H^*(B^{E_3}(A)) = 6^g$ (closed form via K\"unneth; $d_4$ kills $\Lambda^0$ and $\Lambda^3$ per handle, leaving Betti vector $[0, 3, 3, 0]$ at $g = 1$). The chain-level complex $P(q)^{6g}$ is infinite, but the cohomology is $6^g$. This is the content of.
\end{proof}


% ============================================================
\section{The CY modular characteristic}
\label{sec:cy-modular-char}
% ============================================================

The modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of a CY$_3$ chiral algebra is determined by the CY category, not by the Virasoro central charge alone. This section establishes the key distinction between $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$.

\begin{theorem}[$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 \neq \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ in general]
\label{thm:chi-neq-kappa}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The topological Euler characteristic $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$ does \emph{not} equal the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ in general. Explicit counterexamples:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY variety} & $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & \textbf{Match?} \\
 \midrule
 Elliptic curve $E$ ($\CY_1$) & $0$ & $1$ & No \\
 K3 surface ($\CY_2$) & $1$ & $2$\rlap{$^*$} & No \\
 $K3 \times E$ ($\CY_3$) & $0$ & $3$\rlap{$^\dagger$} & No \\
 Resolved conifold & $1/12$ & $1$ & No \\
 Local $\bP^2$ & $1/8$ & $3/2$ & No \\
 \midrule
 Quintic ($\chi = -200$) & $-25/3$ & $-25/3$ & Yes \\
 $\bP^5[3,3]$ ($\chi = -144$) & $-6$ & $-6$ & Yes \\
 $\bP^5[2,4]$ ($\chi = -176$) & $-22/3$ & $-22/3$ & Yes \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
{\footnotesize $^*$The K3 entry records $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}}) = 2$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}; verified via chiral de Rham, Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e}). The distinct quantity $\dim \HH_*(D^b(\mathrm{K3}))/2 = 12$ arises from an alternative algebraization (the Monster orbifold); the full trichotomy ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \in \{2, 12, 24\}$) is developed in the K3 discussion of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e}.

$^\dagger$The $K3 \times E$ entry records $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 3$ by additivity (Proposition~\ref{prop:categorical-euler}: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{K3}) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(E) = 2 + 1$). The Borcherds automorphic weight is $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5 = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5)$, a distinct invariant.}

\smallskip

\noindent The BCOV formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ holds for compact CICYs with $h^{1,0} = 0$ (complete intersections in products of projective spaces) but fails systematically for K3-fibered CY$_3$ (which have $h^{1,0} \geq 1$) and for non-compact toric CY$_3$.

% RECTIFICATION-FLAG (RESOLVED): The K3 (CY_2) entry now records kappa_ch = 2 = chi(O_K3),
% consistent with Proposition prop:cy-kappa-d2 (d=2 proved) and the K3 sigma model value
% (Theorem thm:k3-kappa in k3_chiral_algebra.tex, verified 5 ways). The alternative
% algebraization dim HH_*(D^b(K3))/2 = 12 is noted in the footnote as a distinct object.
% K3xE entry now records kappa_ch = 3 (additivity: 2+1), distinct from kappa_BKM = 5.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 119 tests in \texttt{cy3\_grand\_atlas.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The BCOV formula $F_1 = -\tfrac{1}{2} \log \det(\bar{\partial}^\dagger \bar{\partial})$ on a compact CY$_3$ $X$ gives $F_1 = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$ when $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$. The condition $h^{1,0} = 0$ ensures that the genus-$1$ free energy has no contribution from abelian zero modes; the formula holds for the quintic ($h^{2,1} = 101$) and all other CICYs with $h^{1,0} = 0$, regardless of the number of complex structure moduli.

For K3-fibered CY$_3$, the infinite tower of BPS bound states across fibers contributes additional genus-$1$ data beyond the one-loop determinant. For $K3 \times E$: the $24$ free bosons from the K3 fiber give $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}} = 24$, but the sewing along $E$ via the DMVV formula introduces imaginary root contributions that produce $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5) = 5$ (the weight of the Igusa cusp form). The ``lost'' $19$ units ($24 - 5 = 19$) are absorbed by the Borcherds product structure.

For the resolved conifold: $\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 2$ (the total space deformation retracts onto $\bP^1$) gives $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 = 1/12$, but the DT computation gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ (the conifold has a single BPS state contributing at genus~$1$).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[$K3 \times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ are distinct]
\label{prop:categorical-euler}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For $K3 \times E$, the topological Euler characteristic does not control the genus-$1$ data. The single-copy chiral modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 3$ by additivity ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(E) = 2 + 1$), while the Borcherds automorphic weight is the distinct invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5 = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5)$.

The BKM weight accounts for the full automorphic/BPS package encoded by the Borcherds denominator; it should not be identified with the chiral modular characteristic. For K3-fibered CY$_3$, the infinite tower of bound states across fibers contributes to $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ through the Borcherds product data, while the single-copy shadow scalar still records only $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$.

Five independent verifications of $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(K3 \times E) = 5$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item Weight of the Igusa cusp form: $\mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5) = 5$.
 \item DMVV exponent: the Borcherds lift weight formula gives $c(0)/2 = 10/2 = 5$.
 \item Bar Euler product: the genus-$1$ coefficient of $\log Z_{\DT}$ gives $F_1 = 5/24$.
 \item Relative DT: fiber-by-fiber computation via K3 fibers sewed along $E$.
 \item Anomaly cancellation: the worldsheet anomaly for the $K3 \times E$ sigma model.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 78 tests in \texttt{k3e\_relative\_shadow.py}; 119 tests in \texttt{cy3\_grand\_atlas.py}.
\end{proposition}


% ============================================================
\section{Cross-volume bridges}
\label{sec:cross-volume}
% ============================================================

The results of this chapter connect the CY$_3$ programme to the algebraic engine of Vol~I (Theorems~A--D, the bar-intrinsic MC element $\Theta_A := D_A - d_0$) and the holomorphic-topological QFT framework of Vol~II (Swiss-cheese structure, PVA descent).

\subsection{Shadow tower identification}
\label{subsec:cross-vol-shadow}

The shadow obstruction tower $\Theta_A$ of Vol~I specializes as follows for CY$_3$ chiral algebras:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Vol~I object} & \textbf{CY$_3$ specialization} & \textbf{Identification} \\
 \midrule
 $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$ & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = 1$ & MacMahon genus-$1$ \\
 Cubic shadow $C$ & BKM lightlike roots & $c(0) = 10$ for $K3 \times E$ \\
 Quartic shadow $Q$ & BKM timelike roots & $c(D > 0)$ for $K3 \times E$ \\
 Shadow class G/L/C/M & Toric vertex bound & $r_{\mathrm{max}} \leq N$ \\
 Bar Euler product & Denominator identity & $1/M(q)$ for $\C^3$; $\Delta_5$ for $K3 \times E$ \\
 Shadow connection $\nabla^{\mathrm{sh}}$ & Picard--Fuchs & Modular family \\
 Complementarity $Q(A) + Q(A^!)$ & Koszul dual CY$_3$ & Mirror symmetry \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}


\subsection{$\Eone$-chiral coalgebra and the derived center}
\label{subsec:cross-vol-sc}

% AP165: SC^{ch,top} lives on the derived center pair, NOT on B(A).
The bar complex $B(A)$ is an $\Eone$ chiral coassociative coalgebra over $(\mathrm{ChirAss})^!$; the $\SC^{\mathrm{ch,top}}$ two-colour structure emerges on the derived center pair $(C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A,A),\, A)$, not on the bar complex itself. Three bar constructions reflect three levels of operadic symmetry:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $B_{\Eone}(A)$: ordered tensors, deconcatenation coproduct ($\Eone$ coalgebra).
 \item $B_{\Etwo}(A)$: braided tensors, with $R$-matrix data ($\Etwo$ coalgebra via Drinfeld center).
 \item $B_{\Einf}(A)$: symmetric tensors, Vol~I shadow tower ($\Einf$ coalgebra).
\end{itemize}
The $\Eone \to \Etwo$ passage via Drinfeld center (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}) corresponds to the open-to-bulk passage in Vol~II via the chiral derived center $\cZ^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$. The identification is:
\[
 \cZ\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(A)\bigr) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}\bigl(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)\bigr).
\]
%: The Drinfeld center = chiral derived center is the UNIVERSAL BULK.
% It is NOT bar-cobar inversion (which recovers A itself) nor Verdier
% duality (which produces B(A!)). See Vol I.

\subsection{The BCOV holomorphic anomaly equation as genus spectral sequence}
\label{subsec:bcov-genus-spectral}

The Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa holomorphic anomaly equation (HAE) for the topological B-model on a CY$_3$ $X$ has a natural interpretation in the bar-complex framework: it is the genus spectral sequence of the $\Eone$ bar complex.

\begin{theorem}[HAE $=$ MC genus spectral sequence, structural identification]
\label{thm:hae-mc-structural}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The BCOV holomorphic anomaly equation
\[
 \bar{\partial}_{\bar{\imath}} F_g = \tfrac{1}{2} \bar{C}^{jk}_{\bar{\imath}}
 \bigl(D_j D_k F_{g-1} + \textstyle\sum_{r=1}^{g-1} D_j F_r \cdot D_k F_{g-r}\bigr)
\]
is the genus-$g$ projection of the MC equation $D \cdot \Theta + \tfrac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$ in the Costello--Li dgLa, where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\imath}} F_g$ corresponds to the $d_1$ differential of the genus spectral sequence $E_1^{g,*} \to E_1^{g+1,*}$;
 \item $\bar{C}^{jk}_{\bar{\imath}}$ corresponds to the bar propagator $d\log E(z,w)$;
 \item $D_j D_k F_{g-1}$ corresponds to the handle-creation/sewing term;
 \item $\sum D_j F_r \cdot D_k F_{g-r}$ corresponds to $[\Theta^{(r)}, \Theta^{(g-r)}]$ (factorization).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Costello--Li dgLa $\mathfrak{g}_X = \mathrm{PV}^*(X)[1]$ with the Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket and BRST differential has MC moduli space $= $ B-model moduli. The genus expansion of the B-model free energy is the genus spectral sequence of the MC element, and the HAE is its $d_1$ differential.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 130 tests in \texttt{test\_bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}, including the HAE-MC dictionary, propagator identification, and explicit genus-$1$ through genus-$5$ comparisons for $\C^3$ and the conifold (\texttt{bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}).

\begin{remark}[BCOV holomorphic anomaly as $\Eone$ shadow connection]
\label{rem:bcov-shadow-connection}
The Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa holomorphic anomaly equation for the genus-$g$ topological string free energy is the $\Eone$ shadow connection $\nabla^{\mathrm{sh}}$ of Volume~I (Theorem~\ref{thm:shadow-connection}), restricted to the complex structure moduli of the CY$_3$. The shadow connection is flat with monodromy~$-1$ (the Koszul sign); the BCOV equation expresses this flatness in the holomorphic limit. The genus spectral sequence (Theorem~\ref{thm:hae-mc-structural}) organises the holomorphic anomaly into a degeneration at each genus page.

Two regimes illustrate the scope and limitations of the identification:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item For CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$ (rigid CICYs): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$, and the BCOV propagator is the genus-$1$ shadow. The shadow connection $\nabla^{\mathrm{sh}} = d - Q'_L/(2Q_L)\, dt$ specialises to the Picard--Fuchs connection on the $B$-model moduli space.
 \item For K3-fibered CY$_3$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \neq \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:chi-neq-kappa}), and the shadow connection carries fiber-global mixing data beyond the BCOV framework. The discrepancy $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} - \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ measures the BPS bound-state contribution from the K3 fiber, absorbed by the Borcherds product structure.
\end{enumerate}
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 130 tests in \texttt{bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}, including 10 tests quantifying the K3-fibered discrepancy.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The completed modular Koszul datum for CY$_3$}
\label{subsec:cross-vol-mkd}

The eight-fold completed modular Koszul datum $\Pi^{\mathrm{oc}}_X(A)$ of Vol~I specializes to the CY$_3$ setting as:
\[
 \Pi^{\mathrm{oc}}_{X}(A_{X_3}) = \bigl(\cF_X(A), \overline{B}_X(A), \Theta_A, L_A, (V^{\mathrm{br}}, T^{\mathrm{br}}), R^{\mathrm{mod}}_4, \cZ^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A), \Tr_A\bigr),
\]
where the BKM root system (for K3-fibered CY$_3$) supplies the root lattice structure. The identification of the Borcherds denominator formula with the bar Euler product requires both the CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi$ (CY-A, Theorems~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral} and~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}) and the Vol~I bar Euler product framework (Vol~I, Theorem~A); neither alone suffices.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 124 tests in \texttt{cross\_volume\_shadow\_bridge.py}.


% ============================================================
\section{The chiral deformation stack}
\label{sec:chiral-deformation-stack}
% ============================================================

Fix a graded vector space $V = H^*(S, \C) = \C^{24}$ with the K3 cohomological grading.  The \emph{derived moduli stack of chiral algebra structures} on~$V$ is the derived stack
\[
 \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V) \;=\; \mathrm{RSpec}\bigl(\mathrm{CE}^*(\mathfrak{L}_V)\bigr),
\]
where $\mathfrak{L}_V = \ChirHoch^*(V, V)[1]$ is the shifted chiral Hochschild cochain dgLa, with Gerstenhaber bracket as Lie bracket and Hochschild differential as dgLa differential.  A point of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ is a Maurer--Cartan element $\mu \in \mathfrak{L}_V^1$ satisfying
\[
 d\mu + \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu] = 0,
\]
which is the Jacobi identity for the $\lambda$-bracket~$\mu$.  The graded components of~$\mathfrak{L}_V$ are:
\begin{align*}
 \mathfrak{L}_V^0 &= \End(V) \;=\; \C^{486}  \quad\text{(infinitesimal automorphisms)}, \\
 \mathfrak{L}_V^1 &= \Hom(V^{\otimes 2}, V) \;=\; \C^{1455} \quad\text{($\lambda$-brackets, OPE data)}, \\
 \mathfrak{L}_V^2 &= \Hom(V^{\otimes 3}, V) \quad\text{(obstructions: Jacobi failures)},
\end{align*}
where the dimensions use the degree-additive grading constraint from $V = V_0 \oplus V_2 \oplus V_4$ with $\dim V_0 = \dim V_4 = 1$, $\dim V_2 = 22$.

\begin{proposition}[Tangent complex of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ at the Heisenberg point]
\label{prop:tangent-chiralg}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
At the Heisenberg point $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} \in \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$, the tangent complex is
\[
 T_{\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}} \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V) \;\simeq\; \HH^*_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}})[1],
\]
with cohomology groups:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $\HH^0_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = \C$: the identity automorphism.
 \item $\HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = \Sym^2(V^*)$, of dimension $300 = 24 \cdot 25/2$: first-order deformations of the $\lambda$-bracket, parametrised by deformations of the Mukai pairing.
 \item $\HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = 0$: the Heisenberg vertex algebra is \emph{unobstructed}.
 \item All higher obstructions vanish: $\HH^k_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$ for $k \geq 2$.
\end{enumerate}
The virtual dimension is $\mathrm{vdim}_{\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}} = -1 + 300 - 0 = 299$.  The stack $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ is smooth at~$\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$, with formal neighborhood of dimension~$300$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Heisenberg vertex algebra $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \mathrm{Heis}(V, \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ is a free field theory (class~$G$, shadow depth~$0$): its bar differential has only the linear and quadratic terms~$d_1 + d_2$. Part~(i) holds because the Heisenberg has a unique vacuum state, so the only infinitesimal automorphism preserving the vacuum is the identity.  Part~(ii): a first-order deformation of the $\lambda$-bracket $[a_\lambda\, b] = \omega(a, b)\lambda$ is a map $\delta\omega \colon V \otimes V \to \C$ such that the deformed bracket $[a_\lambda\, b]' = (\omega + \varepsilon\,\delta\omega)(a, b)\lambda$ still satisfies skew-symmetry, which requires~$\delta\omega$ to be symmetric.  Part~(iii): the Jacobi identity for $\lambda$-brackets linear in~$\lambda$ is \emph{automatic}: both sides of the Borcherds identity reduce to expressions involving $\omega(a,b)\omega(c,-)$ and its permutations, which cancel by centrality.  Therefore deforming~$\omega$ to $\omega + \varepsilon\,\delta\omega$ preserves the Jacobi identity for \emph{any} symmetric~$\delta\omega$, and $\HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$.  Part~(iv) follows from the same centrality argument at all tensor powers.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The K3 moduli map]
\label{prop:k3-moduli-map}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}} defines a map
\[
 \varphi_{K3} \colon \cM_{K3} \longrightarrow \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V), \qquad [S] \longmapsto \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr) = \mathrm{Heis}(V, \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}(S)),
\]
from the $20$-dimensional K3 moduli $\cM_{K3}$ into the chiral deformation stack.  Its image lies in the \emph{Heisenberg locus}
\[
 \mathrm{Heis}(V) \;\cong\; O(4, 20) \,/\, \bigl(O(4) \times O(20)\bigr),
\]
an $80$-dimensional Grassmannian parametrising nondegenerate symmetric bilinear forms of signature~$(4, 20)$ on~$V$.  The differential
\[
 d\varphi_{K3} \colon H^1(S, T_S) \longrightarrow \HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}})
\]
is \emph{injective} (local Torelli for K3 surfaces) with $20$-dimensional image in the $300$-dimensional target.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The map sends a deformation of complex structure on~$S$ to the corresponding variation of the Mukai pairing: the Hodge decomposition of $H^2(S, \C)$ changes, and the lambda-bracket coefficients~$\omega^{ij}$ change accordingly.  Injectivity of $d\varphi_{K3}$ is precisely the local Torelli theorem for K3 surfaces (Griffiths, Piateckii-Shapiro--Shafarevich): distinct infinitesimal deformations of $S$ produce distinct periods, hence distinct Mukai pairings.  The $20$-dimensional image sits inside the $80$-dimensional Narain moduli; the complementary $60$ dimensions correspond to $B$-field and K\"ahler deformations invisible to the algebraic Mukai pairing.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[ADE enhancement and stratification of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$]
\label{rem:ade-stratification}
At special points of K3 moduli where $S$ acquires ADE singularities of type~$G$ ($G = A_n, D_n, E_6, E_7, E_8$), the chiral algebra structure jumps: the Heisenberg acquires additional structure from the root lattice.  The resolved chiral algebra is
\[
 \mathrm{WZW}(\mathfrak{g}, k=1) \otimes \mathrm{Heis}(\C^{24 - \mathrm{rk}(G)}, \omega'),
\]
with total central charge $\mathrm{rk}(G) + (24 - \mathrm{rk}(G)) = 24$ (independent of~$G$).  At level~$k = 1$ for simply laced~$\mathfrak{g}$, the WZW model is a lattice VOA (Frenkel--Kac--Segal), so the total algebra is again a lattice VOA on a rank-$24$ lattice, but with a \emph{different} lattice embedding.  This is not a deformation of~$\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$; it is a distinct point of~$\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$.  The ADE stratum has codimension~$\mathrm{rk}(G)$ in~$\cM_{K3}$.

The modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is constant across all strata: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2 = \chi(\cO_S)$ for \emph{all} K3 surfaces, whether generic or ADE-enhanced.  This is a topological invariant: $\chi(\cO_S)$ depends only on the Betti numbers, which are the same for all K3 surfaces.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 60 tests in \texttt{derived\_stack\_chiral.py}, including tangent complex dimensions, ADE strata for all $7$ types ($A_1, A_2, D_4, D_5, E_6, E_7, E_8$), $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ constancy, K3 moduli map injectivity, numerical pairing deformations, and the full verification pipeline.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Chiral Torelli]
\label{rem:chiral-torelli}
The composition of $\varphi_{K3}$ with the global Torelli theorem yields a \emph{chiral Torelli} statement at $d = 2$: a K3 surface~$S$ is recoverable (up to isomorphism) from $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(S)))$.  This holds because the Mukai pairing~$\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}(S)$ determines the Hodge structure on $H^*(S, \bZ)$, which determines~$S$ by global Torelli (Burns--Rapoport).  At $d = 3$, chiral Torelli is CONJECTURAL: the CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_3$ is now constructed (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}), but it is unknown whether the $\Eone$-chiral algebra data suffices to recover the CY$_3$ geometry.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Moduli comparison: $\Ainf$ vs chiral vs $E_n$]
\label{rem:moduli-comparison}
Three derived moduli stacks on the same underlying $V = \C^{24}$ are relevant:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
 & \textbf{Tangent complex} & \textbf{Def dim} & \textbf{Obs dim} \\
\hline
$\Mod_{\Ainf}(V)$ & $\HH^*(A, A)[1]$ & $1455$ & $0$ \\
$\Mod_{E_2}(V)$ & $\HH^*_{E_2}(A, A)[2]$ & $300$ & $0$ \\
$\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ & $\HH^*_{\mathrm{ch}}(A, A)[1]$ & $300$ & $0$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The $\Ainf$-moduli has the largest deformation space ($1455$ dimensions of grading-compatible bilinear maps, not restricted to symmetric); the chiral and $E_2$-moduli agree ($300 = \dim\Sym^2(V^*)$), because the Heisenberg is $\Einf$ (all $E_n$-constraints coincide).  All three stacks are unobstructed at the Heisenberg point.
\end{remark}


% ============================================================
\section{Beyond CY$_3$: chiral algebras from non-CY geometries}
\label{sec:beyond-cy3}
% ============================================================

The CY-to-chiral functor extends beyond the Calabi--Yau setting. For a rank-$2$ bundle $E \to C$ over a smooth curve $C$ of genus~$g$, the total space $\mathrm{Tot}(E)$ carries a chiral algebra $A_E$ regardless of whether $\det(E) \cong K_C$.

\begin{definition}[CY defect]
\label{def:cy-defect}
For $E \to C$ a rank-$2$ bundle, the \emph{CY defect} is $\delta := \deg(\det E) - \deg(K_C) = \deg(\det E) - (2g - 2)$. The CY condition is $\delta = 0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Curved chiral algebra from non-CY local surfaces]
\label{thm:curved-chiral-non-cy}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
When $\delta \neq 0$, the chiral algebra $A_E$ is curved: the curvature element $m_0 = \delta \cdot \omega_C$ is nonzero, the bar complex satisfies $d^2 = [m_0, -]$, and the shadow obstruction tower satisfies the inhomogeneous MC equation
\[
 D \cdot \Theta + \tfrac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = m_0.
\]
The curved modular characteristic is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kappa-curved}
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{crv}} = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \frac{\delta^2 \, \chi(C)}{24},
\end{equation}
where $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is the uncurved value at $\delta = 0$. The correction is quadratic in~$\delta$, linear in~$\chi(C)$, and vanishes identically on the torus ($g = 1$, $\chi = 0$).

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 119 tests in \texttt{curved\_shadow\_non\_cy.py}; 130 tests in \texttt{rank2\_bundle\_chiral.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Hitchin modular characteristic]
\label{thm:hitchin-kappa}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For the Hitchin system on a genus-$g$ curve $C$ with gauge group $\mathrm{GL}(n)$:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{Hitchin}\;\mathrm{GL}(n), C_g) = g,
\]
independent of~$n$. The $\mathrm{SL}(n)$ factor at the critical level $k = -n$ has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\widehat{\fsl}_{n, -n}) = 0$ (the algebraic manifestation of classical integrability of the Hitchin system). Only the $\mathrm{GL}(1)$ center contributes: the rank-$g$ Heisenberg from $T^*\mathrm{Jac}(C)$ gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = g$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 242 tests in \texttt{higgs\_bundle\_chiral.py}; 290 tests in \texttt{spectral\_curve\_chiral.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Kapustin--Witten twist parameter dependence]
\label{prop:kw-kappa-linear}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For the Kapustin--Witten twisted $\cN = 4$ gauge theory with gauge group~$G$ and twist parameter $t \in \C\bP^1$:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_t) = -\frac{\dim(\frakg) \cdot t}{2}.
\]
At $t = 0$ (critical level): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$, commutative ($\Einf$). At $t = 1$ (balanced): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = -\dim(\frakg)/2$. At $t = \infty$ (dual critical): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \to \infty$. Complementarity on the KM/free-field lane: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_t) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_t^!) = \dim(\frakg)/2$ for all~$t$ and all gauge groups.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 137 tests in \texttt{kw\_twisted\_n4\_chiral.py}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[The BCOV constant-map formula vs the shadow formula]
\label{rem:bcov-vs-shadow}
%: structural identification correct, quantitative instantiation fails at g >= 2.
The identification BCOV $=$ shadow is \emph{structural}: the holomorphic anomaly equation IS the genus spectral sequence of an MC equation in the Costello--Li dgLa. However, the \emph{quantitative} formula $F_g = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$ fails for compact CY$_3$ at $g \geq 2$. The BCOV constant-map formula involves the product $B_{2g} \cdot B_{2g-2}$ of two consecutive Bernoulli numbers, while the shadow formula involves $B_{2g}$ alone. Since $B_{2g-2}/B_{2g}$ varies with~$g$, no single~$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ reconciles the two at all genera. For the quintic: the effective $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ matching $F_g^{\mathrm{CM}}$ oscillates ($200, -28.6, -4.3, 2.8, -3.8$ for $g = 1, \ldots, 5$). The shadow formula applies to the \emph{uniform-weight lane} (free fields, toric CY$_3$); for compact CY$_3$, the full shadow tower $\Theta_A$ (all degrees) is needed.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 130 tests in \texttt{bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}, including 10 tests quantifying the genus-dependent disagreement.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[BCOV recursion $=$ shadow tower recursion on the scalar lane]
\label{prop:bcov-shadow-recursion}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The BCOV holomorphic anomaly recursion and the shadow tower recursion (Vol~I, Definition~\ref{def:shadow-tower-recursion}) are identified under the genus-degree correspondence $g \leftrightarrow k = g + 1$:
\begin{center}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{tabular}{ccccl}
\toprule
\textbf{Genus $g$} & \textbf{Arity $k$} & \textbf{Shadow $S_k$} & \textbf{Value (Vir, $c=1$)} & \textbf{BCOV term} \\
\midrule
$1$ & $2$ & $S_2 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & $1/2$ & $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}/24$ (seed) \\
$2$ & $3$ & $S_3 = \alpha$ & $2$ & $D^2 F_1 + (DF_1)^2$ \\
$3$ & $4$ & $S_4$ & $10/27$ & $D^2 F_2 + 2\,DF_1\, DF_2$ \\
$g$ & $g{+}1$ & $S_{g+1}$ & --- & $D^2 F_{g-1} + \textstyle\sum_{r=1}^{g-1} DF_r\, DF_{g-r}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
On the scalar (uniform-weight) lane, the ratio $F_{g+1}/F_g = \lambda_{g+1}^{\mathrm{FP}}/\lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$ is \emph{universal}: it is independent of $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ (the modular characteristic cancels in the ratio). Explicit values: $F_2/F_1 = 7/240$, $F_3/F_2 = 31/1176$, $F_4/F_3 = 127/4960$. The BCOV recursion on the scalar lane reduces to a numerical recursion for $\lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$, which is the shadow tower recursion of Vol~I restricted to the scalar lane.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The identification proceeds in three steps.  First, the Costello--Li dgLa $\mathfrak{g}_X = \mathrm{PV}^{*,*}(X^{\vee})[1]$ IS the bar dgLa of $A_X$ (Costello 2007).  Second, the MC equation $D\Theta + \frac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$ expanded at genus~$g$ gives the HAE (Theorem~\ref{thm:hae-mc-structural}).  Third, on the scalar lane $F_g = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$, the genus-$g$ MC equation reduces to a recursion for the Faber--Pandharipande intersection numbers.  The degree-$(g+1)$ shadow invariant $S_{g+1}$ encodes the $\Ainf$~operation~$m_{g+1}$; on the scalar lane this is the deviation from universality (the off-diagonal correction).  The handle-creation term $D_j D_k F_{g-1}$ corresponds to the propagator insertion in the bar complex; the factorization sum $\sum D_j F_r \cdot D_k F_{g-r}$ corresponds to $[\Theta^{(r)}, \Theta^{(g-r)}]$.  The universal ratios are verified numerically at $g = 1, \ldots, 4$.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 47 tests in \texttt{test\_bcov\_shadow\_tower.py}, including genus-by-genus comparison for C${}^3$, quintic, K3$\times$E, and the Virasoro channel, universal ratio verification through genus~$4$, and handle/factorization decomposition at each genus (\texttt{bcov\_shadow\_tower.py}).

\begin{remark}[Holomorphic anomaly as shadow non-termination]
\label{rem:anomaly-shadow-nontermination}
The holomorphic anomaly $\bar\partial F_g \neq 0$ is \emph{equivalent} to the shadow tower not terminating at degree~$g+1$.  The shadow class (Definition~\ref{def:shadow-class}, Vol~I) determines the anomaly structure:
\begin{center}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{tabular}{clll}
\toprule
\textbf{Class} & \textbf{Shadow tower} & \textbf{Genus expansion} & \textbf{Anomaly type} \\
\midrule
$\mathbf{G}$ & Terminates at $S_2$ & $F_g = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$ (scalar lane only) & No off-diagonal anomaly \\
$\mathbf{L}$ & Finite depth & Polynomial corrections & Finite anomaly \\
$\mathbf{C}$ & Convergent & Convergent (radius $1/S_4$) & Convergent anomaly \\
$\mathbf{M}$ & Gevrey-$1$ divergent & $|F_g| \sim (2g)!/(2\pi)^{2g}$ & Mock modular \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For class~$\mathbf{M}$ (Virasoro at $c = 1$, discriminant $\Delta = 8\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4 = 40/27$), the genus expansion requires Borel resummation; the non-holomorphic completion of $F_g$ produces mock modular forms; and the Borel-plane singularities are conjecturally identified with BPS instanton actions (cf.~Remark~\ref{rem:bcov-shadow-connection}).  The compact CY$_3$ discrepancy (Remark~\ref{rem:bcov-vs-shadow}; the extra $B_{2g-2}$ factor in the constant-map formula) arises because the full shadow tower $\Theta_A$ (all weight sectors) contributes at $g \geq 2$, not just the scalar lane.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 47 tests in \texttt{bcov\_shadow\_tower.py}, including the discriminant computation, four-class classification, and compact CY$_3$ Bernoulli discrepancy at genera $2$--$5$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ polysemy]
\label{rem:cy3-kappa-polysemy}
The symbol~$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ appears in at least four distinct roles across the programme:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$: the modular characteristic (Vol~I Theorem~D), from $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_1^{\mathrm{FP}}$.
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$: the BCOV anomaly coefficient. Equals~$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ for rigid compact CICYs with $h^{1,0} = 0$, but not in general.
 \item The MacMahon exponent (sometimes written $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ in the MNOP formula $Z_{\DT,0} = M(-q)^{\chi(X)}$): the exponent $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)$ in the degree-zero DT partition function. Equals $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ for $\mathrm{Tot}(K_S)$. This is NOT a modular characteristic; it is the topological Euler characteristic, and must not be relabeled as $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$, or $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$.
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$: the weight of the BKM automorphic form. For $K3 \times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5) = 5$.
\end{enumerate}
These coincide for Heisenberg ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = k$) and Virasoro ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = c/2$) but diverge for general CY$_3$. The quintic alone admits three values: $-25/3$, $-100$, $200$.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[$\Phi_3$ on the quintic and local~$\bP^2$: scope, Borcherds admissibility, BCOV/G\"ottsche substitutes]
\label{rem:phi3-quintic-local-p2}
The CY-A$_3$ functor $\Phi_3 \colon D^b(\Coh(X)) \to \Eone\text{-}\ChirAlg$ is proved at $\infty$-categorical level for every smooth projective CY$_3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}). The \emph{explicit} $\Eone$-chiral output is, however, manifold-dependent, and the availability of a Borcherds-type automorphic witness is restricted to K3-fibered CY$_3$ with even unimodular Mukai lattice. This remark pins down the quintic and local~$\bP^2$ cases and separates the Borcherds substitute from the BCOV / G\"ottsche substitutes.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \textbf{$\Phi_3$ on the quintic $Q = X_5 \subset \bP^4$.} Existence is unconditional via Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}. An explicit $\Eone$-chiral model is obtained through the DT/CoHA route: $\Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(Q)))$ is identified with the Kontsevich--Soibelman CoHA $\cH(Q_{\mathrm{quintic}}, W_{\mathrm{quintic}})$ built from a quiver-with-potential presentation of a tilting-type generator of $D^b(\Coh(Q))$ on the derived category of local coherent sheaves along vanishing cycles (Gepner chart, Remark~\ref{rem:gepner-lv-dichotomy}; $625$ fractional branes; see \texttt{tilting\_chart\_cy3.py}). The large-volume chart has no tilting generator ($\Ext^3 \neq 0$ by Serre duality) and supplies the non-formal $\Ainf$-input $m_3 \neq 0$ (Yukawa coupling $H^3 = 5 + \mathrm{GW}$), consistent with class~$\mathbf{M}$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_Q) = -25/3$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula} and Theorem~\ref{thm:chi-neq-kappa}). Convergence of the \v{C}ech contracting homotopy is proved ($N=5$ patches, $d=5$, radius $\geq 1/20$; Proposition~\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}).
 \item \textbf{$\Phi_3$ on local~$\bP^2 = \Tot(K_{\bP^2} \to \bP^2)$.} Explicit: $\Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{local}\,\bP^2)))$ is the Schiffmann--Vasserot / RSYZ CoHA of the McKay quiver of $K_{\bP^2}$, equivalently the Nakajima cohomological Hall algebra on the nested Hilbert scheme tower of points on $\bP^2$ (cf.\ Theorem~\ref{thm:drinfeld-center-coha} and Chapter~\ref{ch:toric-coha}). Class-$\mathbf{M}$ assignment is confirmed by the full shadow tower, not by generator count; the $\Eone$ universality pillars (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}) are verified.
 \item \textbf{Borcherds admissibility is not $\Phi_3$.} A Borcherds multiplicative lift of a weak Jacobi form produces a Siegel automorphic form (e.g.\ $\Delta_5$, $\Phi_{10}$, $\Phi_{12}$) whose denominator identity carves out a generalized Kac--Moody superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ \emph{iff} the target lattice is Lorentzian and even unimodular of signature compatible with the Weil representation. For a K3-fibered CY$_3$ with fiber $S$, the Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(X) \supseteq H^*(S, \Z)$ inherits even unimodularity from~$S$, and the Borcherds lift applies (Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-lift-universal}, $K3 \times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5$). For the \emph{quintic}, $H^*(Q, \Z)$ does \emph{not} carry an even unimodular Mukai structure: the relevant algebraic lattice is rank-$1$ ($H^2(Q, \Z) \cong \Z$ with $H^3 = 5$), total cohomology is supported in degrees $0, 2, 3, 4, 6$ with $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(Q) = -200$, and no rank-$2$ Lorentzian even unimodular sublattice admitting a Jacobi input is singled out. Consequently $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(Q)$ is undefined and no Borcherds lift produces $A_Q$ (consistent with Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra} and Remark~\ref{rem:cy3-kappa-polysemy}).
 \item \textbf{BCOV as the Borcherds substitute on compact non-K3-fibered CY$_3$.} On a compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$, the BCOV partition function $Z_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(t, \bar t) = \exp\bigl(\sum_{g\geq 0} \lambda^{2g-2} F_g(t, \bar t)\bigr)$ plays the role that the Borcherds lift plays for K3-fibered CY$_3$: its holomorphic anomaly equation is the shadow-tower spectral connection (Theorem~\ref{thm:hae-mc-structural}; Proposition~\ref{prop:bcov-shadow-recursion}), and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ is read off from $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}/24$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}). For the quintic $F_1 = -25/72$, matching $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 = -25/3$. BCOV is \emph{not} a theta lift: there is no Jacobi-form input, no automorphic output, and no denominator identity; the substitute delivers a genus-by-genus holomorphic anomaly recursion, not a BKM superalgebra.
 \item \textbf{G\"ottsche as the Borcherds substitute on local surfaces.} For $\Tot(K_S \to S)$ with $S$ a smooth projective surface, the rank-$1$ sheaf partition function on~$S$ is the G\"ottsche generating function $\sum_{n\geq 0} \chi(\Hilb^n(S))\, q^n = \prod_{k\geq 1} (1 - q^k)^{-\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)}$ (at rank $1$; rank-$r$ Vafa--Witten generalisations in Chapter~\ref{ch:quantum_chiral_algebras}), whose exponent recovers $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{\mathrm{loc}\,S}) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ via MNOP degree-zero (conjectural branch of Proposition~\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}). For local~$\bP^2$, $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2) = 3$, so the rank-$1$ exponent is~$3$ and the rank-$1$ VW generating function is $\prod_{k\geq 1}(1-q^k)^{-3}$ (rank-$r$ Vafa--Witten generalisations in Chapter~\ref{ch:quantum-chiral-algebras}). G\"ottsche delivers a partition function, not an automorphic form, and no Borcherds lift is available (Mukai rank~$3$, not even unimodular).
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{Protocol summary (a/b/c).} (a) $\Phi_3(\mathrm{quintic})$, $\Phi_3(\mathrm{local}\,\bP^2)$ both have \emph{explicit} identifications — CoHA of the Gepner quiver-with-potential for the quintic, Schiffmann--Vasserot/Nakajima CoHA for local~$\bP^2$ — not merely existence narration. (b) Borcherds admissibility is strictly narrower than CY-A$_3$: it requires even unimodular Mukai lattice of Lorentzian signature with a Jacobi-form input, which holds for K3-fibered CY$_3$ and fails for the quintic and for local~$\bP^2$. (c) The non-K3-fibered substitute is \emph{dimension-specialised}: BCOV for compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0}=0$ (genus spectral sequence for~$F_g$), G\"ottsche / Vafa--Witten for local surfaces (rank-$1$ partition function with exponent $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)$); neither is a theta lift. These substitutes do not produce a BKM superalgebra and do not define $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$; they fix $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ via $F_1$ or via MNOP degree-zero respectively. (Cross-references:; FM160 on Monster/Borcherds orbifold route; Remark~\ref{rem:cy3-kappa-polysemy}.)
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[CoHA of non-CY threefolds]
\label{prop:coha-non-cy}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For a quiver with potential $(Q, W)$ that does NOT satisfy the CY$_3$ condition
(i.e.\ $\dim \Ext^1(S_i, S_j) \neq \dim \Ext^2(S_j, S_i)$ for some simples):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The CoHA $\cH(Q, W) = \bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}} H^{\mathrm{BM}}_*(\Crit(\Tr W)_{\mathbf{d}}, \varphi_{\Tr W})$ is still a well-defined associative ($\Eone$) algebra.
 \item The Euler form $\chi(\gamma_1, \gamma_2)$ is NOT antisymmetric: the symmetric part $s(\gamma_1, \gamma_2) = \chi(\gamma_1, \gamma_2) + \chi(\gamma_2, \gamma_1)$ is the CY defect at the categorical level.
 \item The bar complex $B(\cH(Q,W))$ is curved: $d^2 = [m_0, -]$ with $m_0$ proportional to the symmetric part of the Euler form.
 \item The Davison--Meinhardt PBW filtration requires the CY$_3$ Serre duality; it may fail for non-CY quivers.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 141 tests in \texttt{test\_coha\_non\_cy\_threefold.py}, including explicit computations for the Jordan quiver with cubic potential ($W = x^3$, curvature $m_0 = 1$), the asymmetric $2$-vertex quiver ($3+1$ arrows, CY defect $= -4$), and Hall multiplication associativity for all tested quivers (\texttt{coha\_non\_cy\_threefold.py}).
\end{proposition}


\begin{proposition}[Spectral curves and the quantum Hitchin system]
\label{prop:spectral-curve-chiral}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The Beilinson--Drinfeld quantization of the Hitchin system on a genus-$g$ curve $C$ with gauge group $\mathrm{GL}(n)$ produces a commutative chiral algebra at the critical level $k = -n$:
\[
 Z\bigl(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n, -n}\bigr) \;=\; \mathrm{Fun}\bigl(\mathrm{Op}_{\mathrm{PGL}_n}(C)\bigr),
\]
the algebra of functions on $\mathrm{PGL}_n$-opers. The generators are the quantum Hitchin Hamiltonians, with spins equal to the Casimir degrees $d_1, \ldots, d_r$ of $\mathfrak{sl}_n$. The spectral curve $\Sigma_b \subset \mathrm{Tot}(K_C)$ has genus $g(\Sigma_b) = n^2(g-1) + 1$, and the Hitchin base has dimension $\dim B_{\mathrm{GL}(n)} = n^2(g-1) + 1$ (the Prym--Hitchin equality).

The Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit ($\varepsilon_2 \to 0$) recovers the oper as the symbol of the quantum spectral curve; WKB exactness holds for $\mathrm{SL}(2)$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 290 tests in \texttt{test\_spectral\_curve\_chiral.py}, including spectral curve genus via three paths (formula, Riemann--Hurwitz, adjunction), Hitchin base dimensions for all simple Lie types through $E_8$, and the Prym--Hitchin equality for $81$ $(n,g)$ pairs (\texttt{spectral\_curve\_chiral.py}).
\end{proposition}

%% Derived stack of chiral algebra structures
