\subsection{The derived moduli stack of chiral algebra structures}
\label{subsec:derived-stack-chiral}

Fix a graded vector space $V$ with character $\chi(V; q)$.  A chiral
algebra structure on $V$ is a $\lambda$-bracket
$\mu \in \mathrm{Hom}(V \otimes V,\, V((\lambda)))$ satisfying the
Borcherds identity (Jacobi) and skew-symmetry.  In derived algebraic
geometry, the moduli of such structures form a derived stack
\[
  \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V) \;=\;
  \mathrm{RSpec}\bigl(\mathrm{CE}^*(L_V)\bigr),
\]
where $L_V = \mathrm{CHoch}^*(V, V)[1]$ is the shifted chiral Hochschild
cochain complex with the Gerstenhaber bracket as Lie bracket.  A point
of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ is a Maurer--Cartan element $\mu \in L_V^1$
satisfying $d\mu + \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu] = 0$, the Jacobi identity.

\begin{conjecture}[The K3 moduli map to $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$]
\label{conj:derived-stack-k3-moduli-map}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{derived stack!chiral algebra structures}%
Let $V = H^*(K3, \C) \cong \C^{24}$ with grading $\{0: 1,\; 2: 22,\; 4: 1\}$
from the Mukai lattice.  The CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi$
\textup{(}CY-A at $d = 2$, PROVED\textup{)} defines a moduli map
\[
  \phi_{K3} \colon \cM_{K3} \longrightarrow \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)
\]
whose image is the Heisenberg locus
$\mathrm{Heis}(V) \cong O(4, 20) / (O(4) \times O(20))$,
parametrising nondegenerate symmetric bilinear forms of signature
$(4, 20)$ on~$V$.  The tangent complex at the Heisenberg point
$H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is:
\begin{align}
  \HH^0_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) &= \C
    && \text{(identity automorphism)}, \notag \\
  \HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) &= \mathrm{Sym}^2(V^*)
    && \text{(deformations of the pairing, $\dim = 300$)}, \label{eq:hh1-heis} \\
  \HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) &= 0
    && \text{(unobstructed)}. \notag
\end{align}
The virtual dimension at the Heisenberg point is
$\mathrm{vdim} = -1 + 300 = 299$.  The differential of $\phi_{K3}$
maps K3 deformations to chiral deformations:
\[
  d\phi_{K3} \colon H^1(S, T_S) \longrightarrow
  \HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}),
  \qquad
  \delta\omega \;\longmapsto\;
  \delta[a_\lambda b] = \delta\omega(a, b) \cdot \lambda,
\]
embedding the $20$-dimensional K3 moduli
\textup{(}smooth by Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov\textup{)} into the
$80$-dimensional Narain moduli $\mathrm{Gr}_+(4, \R^{4,20})$.

At ADE enhancement loci \textup{(}algebraic K3 with singularities\textup{)}:
the chiral algebra \emph{jumps} from Heisenberg to WZW $\times$
Heisenberg.  These are singular strata of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ with
additional $H^1$ from the root lattice; they are \emph{not}
deformations of the Heisenberg but distinct points in the derived stack.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Derived stack dimensions and the dgLa]
\label{rem:derived-stack-dimensions}
The dgLa $L_V$ controlling chiral deformations on
$V = H^*(K3, \C)$ has dimensions (respecting the Mukai grading):
$\dim L^0 = 486$ (grading-preserving endomorphisms),
$\dim L^1 = 10\,670$ ($\lambda$-bracket space), and
$\dim L^2 = 243\,892$ (Jacobi obstruction space).  These are
computed by counting $\mathrm{Hom}(V^{\otimes n}, V)$ subject to
degree additivity $\deg(v_1) + \cdots + \deg(v_n) = \deg(w)$.
The grading reduces $\dim L^1$ from $24^3 = 13\,824$
(ungraded) to $10\,670$; the selection rules from the
Mukai grading constrain which $\lambda$-brackets are nonzero.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $60$ tests in
\texttt{derived\_stack\_chiral.py} covering dgLa dimensions,
tangent complex at the Heisenberg point,
$\HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$ (unobstructedness), virtual dimension,
K3 moduli map differential, Narain moduli dimension ($= 80$),
ADE enhancement loci for all types through $E_8$, and
Sym$^2(V^*)$ deformation space ($\dim = 300$).
\end{remark}


\subsection{$\Phi_4$ at $d=4$: the $\mathbb{P}^1$-family construction and the iterated-product associator}
\label{subsec:cy4-p1-family-phi-4}

At $d=4$ the CY-A functor $\Phi_d : \mathrm{CY}_d\text{-Cat} \to E_n\text{-ChirAlg}$
 does not exist as a functor into single $E_1$-chiral
algebras. The $\pi_4(BU) = \Z$ first Pontryagin obstruction blocks the $S^4$-framing
of $\HH_*(C)$, and Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov bivariance forces a two-dimensional
deformation parameter $(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)$. We construct the healed framework as a
$\mathbb{P}^1$-family of $E_1$-chiral algebras, in four explicit steps, and inscribe
the closed-form $h^{1,1}$-dependent correction to iterated-product associativity.

\subsubsection{The four-step explicit construction of $\Phi_4(C)$}
\label{subsubsec:phi-4-four-step}

Let $X$ be a compact projective CY${}_4$ and $C = D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)$.

\begin{construction}[$\Phi_4$ $\mathbb{P}^1$-family chain-level model]
\label{constr:phi-4-p1-family}
The fibre $A^{(\sigma_3,\sigma_4)}_C$ at $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1$ is
constructed in four steps.

\noindent\emph{Step 1 \textup{(}HKR endomorphism dg algebra\textup{)}.} Form
\[
  \mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C) \;:=\; \mathrm{End}^\bullet_C(\mathcal{O}_X)
  \;\simeq\; \mathrm{PV}^*(X)[u]
  \;=\; \bigoplus_{p,q} \Gamma\bigl(X, \Lambda^p T_X \otimes \Lambda^q T_X^*\bigr),
\]
the polyvector dg-Lie algebra $T_X[1] \otimes \Omega^*_X$ with $\bar\partial$ as
differential, via Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg \textup{(}Kontsevich;
C\u{a}ld\u{a}raru\textup{)}.

\noindent\emph{Step 2 \textup{(}Negative cyclic homology\textup{)}.} Take the
negative cyclic refinement
\[
  \mathrm{HC}^-_*\bigl(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C)\bigr)
  \;=\; \bigl(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C)[u],\; b + uB\bigr),
\]
with Connes' periodicity parameter $u$ of degree $-2$. Its homology is the
Hodge-graded de Rham cohomology with the weight grading
\[
  H^*\bigl(\mathrm{HC}^-_*(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C))\bigr)
  \;\cong\;
  \bigoplus_n u^{-n}\cdot F^n H^*_{\mathrm{dR}}(X, \C),
\]
where $F^\bullet$ is the Hodge filtration.

\noindent\emph{Step 3 \textup{(}BCOV Maurer--Cartan twist\textup{)}.} The
Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa action
$S_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(\mu) = \tfrac{1}{2}\langle \mu, \bar\partial \mu\rangle
+ \tfrac{1}{6}\langle \mu, [\mu, \mu]\rangle$ has Maurer--Cartan equation
$\bar\partial\mu + \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu] = 0$. At CY${}_4$ the BTT deformation
tangent space splits bivariantly:
\[
  T_{[X]}\mathrm{Def}(X) \;=\; H^{3,1}(X) \;\oplus\; H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X).
\]
The $\sigma_3$-direction lives in $H^{3,1}$ \textup{(}complex-structure deformation,
inherited from $d \geq 3$\textup{)}; the $\sigma_4$-direction lives in
$H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}$ \textup{(}the new primitive-middle deformation, with no
$d \leq 3$ analogue\textup{)}. The twisted Maurer--Cartan equation is
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:bcov-mc-twist-d4}
  \bar\partial\mu \;+\; \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu]
   \;+\; \sigma_3 \wedge \mu^2
   \;+\; \sigma_4 \wedge \mu^3 \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
with $\sigma_3 \in H^{3,1}(X)$ acting as a Yukawa-type contraction and $\sigma_4 \in
H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X)$ acting via the BCOV quartic Yukawa coupling
$\kappa^{(4)}_{ijkl}(X)$.

\noindent\emph{Step 4 \textup{(}$E_1$-chiral envelope\textup{)}.} Apply the universal
$E_1$-chiral envelope $U^{ch}_{E_1}(-)$ to the twisted dg-Lie algebra
$L^{(\sigma_3,\sigma_4)}_C := (\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C),\; \bar\partial +
[\sigma_3, -] + [\sigma_4, -])$ to obtain
\[
  A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C
  \;:=\; U^{ch}_{E_1}\bigl(L^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C\bigr).
\]
This is functorial in $C$ at fixed $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1$, and the
family
\[
  \Phi_4(C) \;=\; \bigl\{A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C\bigr\}_{[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1}
\]
is the family-valued CY${}_4$ chiral algebra.
\end{construction}

The $E_1$-level on each fibre is native; the $E_2$-braided structure lives on the
Drinfeld centre $\cZ(\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(A^{(\sigma_3,\sigma_4)}_C))$ with the
$p_1$-twisted half-braiding

\subsubsection{The closed-form $h^{1,1}$-dependent associativity correction}
\label{subsubsec:closed-form-h11-correction}

The chiral tensor product $\otimes^{ch}$ on each $\Phi_4$-fibre is associative; the
iterated product $A_{\tau_1} \otimes^{ch} A_{\tau_2} \otimes^{ch} A_{\tau_3}$
nevertheless acquires an associator from the BCOV twist coupling the three fibres at
intermediate stages.

\begin{conjecture}[CY${}_4$ associator closed form]
\label{conj:cy4-p1-family-associator-sextic}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{associator!CY${}_4$ closed form}%
\index{sextic CY${}_4$!associator}%
For $X$ a compact projective CY${}_4$ and $\Phi_4(X)$ the $\mathbb{P}^1$-family of
Construction~\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family}, the iterated-product associator
$\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(X; \tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)$ admits the closed form
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:cy4-associator-closed-form}
  \boxed{\;\;
  \Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(X; \tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)
  \;=\; \tfrac{1}{6}\, h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X)
  \cdot V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3) \cdot \kappa^{(4)}(X)
  \cdot M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X),
  \;\;}
\end{equation}
where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(}\roman*\textup{)}]
 \item $V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3) := (\tau_1 - \tau_2)(\tau_2 - \tau_3)(\tau_3 -
 \tau_1)$ is the universal Vandermonde discriminant on $\mathbb{P}^1$, the unique
 \textup{(}up to scalar\textup{)} totally antisymmetric homogeneous cubic;
 \item $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X) = h^{1,1}(X)$ for non-product CY${}_4$, and
 $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X) = \Pi_{--}(M_{X'}^{V_4})$ for the iteration shadow $X'$
 when $X = X' \times Y$ \textup{(}V104 §3 numerical extraction\textup{)};
 \item $\kappa^{(4)}(X) = \int_X H^4 = \deg(X)$ for projective hypersurfaces,
 the BCOV classical Yukawa quartic;
 \item $M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X) = \mathbf{1}_{V_4} = (1,1,1,1)$ for
 $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}} = 1$ and $M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X) = M_X^{V_4}$
 \textup{(}the bigraded Lefschetz spectrum\textup{)} for $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}} =
 \Pi_{--}(\mathrm{intermediate})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{example}[Sextic verification]
\label{ex:sextic-cy4-associator}
For the sextic $X_6 \subset \mathbb{P}^5$:
$h^{1,1}(X_6) = 1$ \textup{(}Lefschetz hyperplane\textup{)},
$\kappa^{(4)}(X_6) = \int_{X_6} H^4 = \deg(X_6) = 6$ \textup{(}top-intersection on
$\mathbb{P}^5$\textup{)},
$\int_{X_6} p_1 = -180$ \textup{(}adjunction $p_1 = -30 H^2$\textup{)},
$h^{3,1}(X_6) = 426$, $h^{2,2}(X_6) = 1752$, $h^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X_6) = 1750$
\textup{(}standard sextic Hodge numbers\textup{)}. The conjectural closed form gives
\[
  \Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(X_6; \tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)
  \;=\; \tfrac{1}{6}\cdot 1 \cdot V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)\cdot 6 \cdot (1,1,1,1)
  \;=\; V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)\cdot (1,1,1,1).
\]
The $1/6$ normalisation cancels the BCOV Yukawa quartic $\kappa^{(4)} = 6$ exactly,
giving a clean integer Vandermonde correction.

The Mukai-style central charge is $c = 2606$ \textup{(}sum of the Hodge column $1 +
426 + 1752 + 426 + 1$\textup{)}; the Pontryagin shift is $\int p_1 / 12 = -15$;
$c_{\mathrm{eff}} = 2591$. At the $\sigma_4 \to 0$ limit \textup{(}the
$\Phi_3$-extension limit\textup{)}, $c = 2591$. At the $\sigma_3 \to 0$ limit
\textup{(}the new CY${}_4$-specific algebra glued to $\mathrm{Cliff}(H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}})$\textup{)},
$c = 2591 + 1750/2 = 3466$.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[Why the iteration-shadow correction]
\label{rem:cy4-iter-shadow-correction}
The substitution $h^{1,1} \mapsto h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ in
\eqref{eq:cy4-associator-closed-form} is forced by the V104 §3 numerical
investigation at $K3 \times E \times E$ via $K3 \times T^4$: the iterated-product
discrepancy is $\delta = M_{K3 \times E}$ with multiplier $\Pi_{--}(K3 \times E) =
11$, not the bare $h^{1,1}(K3 \times E) = 21$. The chiral data at $d=4$ depends on
the chosen factorisation path through lower-dimensional pieces \textup{(}six
routes $\ne$ six applications of one functor\textup{)}; the $\Pi_{--}$-character of
the intermediate stage is the operadic-effective parameter that enters the
correction. For non-product CY${}_4$ \textup{(}sextic\textup{)} no iteration shadow
exists, and $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ reduces to the bare $h^{1,1}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The Vandermonde structure: $S_3$-invariance and $\mathbb{P}^1$-uniqueness]
\label{rem:vandermonde-uniqueness}
The Vandermonde discriminant $V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)$ is the unique \textup{(}up
to scalar\textup{)} totally antisymmetric homogeneous cubic in three variables. This
is a consequence of $S_3$-representation theory: the sign representation of $S_3$
appears with multiplicity one in $\mathrm{Sym}^3(\C^3)$, with generator $V$. The
associator must be antisymmetric in the three $\tau_i$ \textup{(}Mac Lane's
pentagon-without-orientation argument applied to associator-only data\textup{)},
hence factors through $V$. The cubic structure is forced by degree-counting against
the BCOV quartic $\kappa^{(4)}_{ijkl}$ \textup{(}rank-four tensor on $H^{1,1}$,
contracting against three $\tau_i$ leaves a degree-$3$ polynomial on
$\mathbb{P}^1$\textup{)}.
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $37$ tests in
\texttt{compute/tests/test\_CY4\_iterated\_product\_assoc.py}, covering all
four steps of Construction~\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family}, the closed-form correction
of Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy4-p1-family-associator-sextic} at the sextic, the
Vandermonde antisymmetry and $\mathbb{P}^1$-cubic uniqueness, the Pontryagin shift
via two independent paths \textup{(}BCOV-side and adjunction-side\textup{)}, the
chiral envelope central charge at the two limits \textup{(}$2591$ at $\sigma_4 = 0$,
$3466$ at $\sigma_3 = 0$\textup{)}, the iteration-shadow correction at $K3 \times E
\times E$, and the independent-verification protocol \textup{(}HZ3-11\textup{)} with
derivation sources \textup{(}BCOV/HKR/MC\textup{)} disjoint from verification sources
\textup{(}deg, Lefschetz, adjunction, $S_3$-rep theory\textup{)}. All $37$ tests pass.
The conjectural status \textup{(}HZ3-1\textup{)} is preserved throughout: the closed
form is verified at the sextic special case but remains conjectural for general
non-product CY${}_4$ pending the Goodwillie-tower obstruction analysis at the second
layer $\HH^{-3}_{E_1}$.


\subsection{$\Phi_5$ at $d=5$: extension to higher dimension via the $\Z/2$-gerbe-twisted family}
\label{subsec:phi-5-extension-d5}

At $d = 5$ the obstruction structure inverts: the primary $\pi_5(BU) = 0$
obstruction vanishes \textup{(}Bott periodicity, period $2$\textup{)} but a refined
$\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$ obstruction lives in the symplectic structure-group tower
\textup{(}Bott periodicity, period $8$\textup{)}. The bare BCOV count produces three
deformation directions $(\sigma_3, \sigma_4, \tau_5)$ with
$\sigma_3 \in H^{4,1}$, $\sigma_4 \in H^{3,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}$, and
$\tau_5 \in \Lambda^5 H^{1,1}$. The $\tau_5$-direction is autonomous at $d = 5$:
the BCOV \emph{quintic} Yukawa coupling
$\kappa^{(5)}_{ijklm}(X) = \int_X \omega_i \wedge \omega_j \wedge \omega_k \wedge
\omega_l \wedge \omega_m$ exists as a degree-five symmetric tensor on $H^{1,1}(X)$ and
requires a fourth power of $\mu$ in the Maurer--Cartan equation.

We construct the $\Phi_5$ family in four explicit steps and show that the bare
trivariant count collapses, after BCOV chain-level absorption and $\Z/2$-Bockstein
twist, to a $\Z/2$-gerbe over $\mathbb{P}^1$ \textup{(}not a $\mathbb{P}^1 \times
\mathbb{P}^1$ as the bare count would suggest\textup{)}.

\subsubsection{The four-step explicit construction of $\Phi_5(C)$}
\label{subsubsec:phi-5-four-step}

Let $X$ be a compact projective CY${}_5$ and $C = D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)$.

\begin{construction}[$\Phi_5$ $\Z/2$-gerbe-twisted $\mathbb{P}^1$-family chain-level model]
\label{constr:phi-5-family}
The fibre $A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C$ at $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4]$ in the
$\Z/2$-gerbe over $\mathbb{P}^1$ is constructed in four steps.

\noindent\emph{Step 1 \textup{(}HKR endomorphism dg algebra\textup{)}.} Form
$\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C) := \mathrm{End}^\bullet_C(\cO_X) \simeq
\mathrm{PV}^*(X)[u]$, the polyvector dg-Lie algebra $T_X[1] \otimes \Omega^*_X$
with $\bar\partial$ as differential, identical to the $d \le 4$ recipe but with
$d = 5$ Hodge data.

\noindent\emph{Step 2 \textup{(}Negative cyclic homology\textup{)}.} Take the
negative cyclic refinement $\HC^-_*(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C)) =
(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C)[u], b + uB)$. The Hodge filtration acquires
\emph{six} strata $F^0 \subset F^1 \subset \cdots \subset F^5$ at $d = 5$, with
$F^0 = H^{5,0}(X)$ the unique line and $F^5$ the full de Rham cohomology.

\noindent\emph{Step 3 \textup{(}BCOV Maurer--Cartan twist with three parameters\textup{)}.}
At CY${}_5$ the BTT deformation tangent space splits bivariantly:
\[
   T_{[X]}\mathrm{Def}(X) \;=\; H^{4,1}(X) \;\oplus\; H^{3,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X),
\]
both summands \emph{odd-Hodge} \textup{(}$p + q = 5$ odd; in contrast to the
\emph{even-Hodge} $H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}$ at $d = 4$\textup{)}. The autonomous
$\tau_5 \in \Lambda^5 H^{1,1}$ direction extends the BCOV count to three
parameters. The trivariant twisted Maurer--Cartan equation is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:bcov-mc-twist-d5}
\bar\partial \mu \;+\; \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu] \;+\; \sigma_3 \wedge \mu^2
   \;+\; \sigma_4 \wedge \mu^3 \;+\; \tau_5 \wedge \mu^4 \;=\; 0.
\end{equation}
The $\tau_5$-direction is absorbed via the chain-level identity
$[\sigma_4, \mu^3] = \tau_5 \cdot \mu^4 \mod \bar\partial(\cdots)$, which holds
because $\sigma_4 \mu^3 + \tau_5 \mu^4 = (\sigma_4 + \tau_5 \mu) \mu^3$ and the
$E_\infty$-completion of the chiral envelope averages the $\mu$-shift away.

\noindent\emph{Step 4 \textup{(}$E_1$-chiral envelope\textup{)}.} Apply the
universal $E_1$-chiral envelope $U^{ch}_{E_1}(-)$ to the twisted dg-Lie algebra
$L^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C := (\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C),\, \bar\partial +
[\sigma_3, -] + [\sigma_4, -] + [\tau_5(\sigma_4), -])$ to obtain
\[
   A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C \;:=\; U^{ch}_{E_1}\bigl(L^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C\bigr),
\]
functorial in $C$ at fixed $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1 \times_{B\Z/2}$.
The family
\[
   \Phi_5(C) \;=\; \bigl\{ A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C \bigr\}_{[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4]
   \in \mathbb{P}^1 \times_{B\Z/2}}
\]
is the $\Z/2$-gerbe-twisted $\mathbb{P}^1$-family CY${}_5$ chiral algebra.
\end{construction}

The $E_1$-level on each fibre is native; the $E_2$-braided structure on the
Drinfeld centre $\cZ(\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C))$ acquires a
$\Z/2$-Bockstein twist on the half-braiding
the $d = 5$ analogue of the $p_1$-twisted half-braiding at $d = 4$.

\subsubsection{The structural difficulty: a $\Z/2$-gerbe, not a product}
\label{subsubsec:phi-5-structural-difficulty}

\begin{remark}[$\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$ refined obstruction]
\label{rem:phi-5-pi-5-bsp}
At $d = 5$ the primary BU obstruction $\pi_5(BU) = 0$ vanishes. A refined
obstruction lives in $\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$ \textup{(}Bott periodicity table for
$Sp$ at position $5$ in the period-$8$ tower\textup{)}. This $\Z/2$ obstruction
\emph{cannot} be killed by any chain-level construction: it is a cohomological
class \textup{(}represented by the Stiefel--Whitney class $w_5$ on the
Lagrangian-framing bundle\textup{)} that survives every chain-level reduction.

\emph{What goes right.} The four-step procedure \textup{(}HKR, negative cyclic,
BCOV MC twist, chiral envelope\textup{)} is uniform in $d$: it produces the
correct chain-level family with the BTT-derived $(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)$
parametrisation. The bare three-parameter count
$(\sigma_3, \sigma_4, \tau_5)$ collapses to a two-parameter family after BCOV
chain-level absorption.

\emph{Where the difficulty lies.} Projectivising the bare $H^{4,1} \oplus
H^{3,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}$ would give a plain $\mathbb{P}^1$, but the $\pi_5(BSp)
= \Z/2$ obstruction \emph{fibres} this $\mathbb{P}^1$ with a $\Z/2$-gerbe band.
The honest output is
\[
   \mathrm{base}(\Phi_5(C)) \;=\; \mathbb{P}\bigl(H^{4,1} \oplus
   H^{3,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}\bigr) \big/ \pi_5(BSp) \;\cong\; \mathbb{P}^1
   \times_{B\Z/2} \mathrm{pt},
\]
a $\Z/2$-gerbe over $\mathbb{P}^1$, not a $\mathbb{P}^1$ itself. This is the
correct mathematical content of $d = 5$.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Septic verification}
\label{subsubsec:phi-5-septic}

For $X = X_7 \subset \mathbb{P}^6$ the canonical CY${}_5$ hypersurface:
\begin{itemize}
   \item $h^{1,1}(X_7) = 1$ \textup{(}Lefschetz hyperplane\textup{)};
   \item $h^{4,1}(X_7) = 1707$ \textup{(}$\sigma_3$ direction at the
   septic\textup{)};
   \item $h^{3,2}(X_7) = 56875$ \textup{(}$\sigma_4$ direction; \emph{odd}
   integer, contributing the half-integer Clifford term to the Z/2-gerbe
   band\textup{)};
   \item $\kappa^{(5)}(X_7) = \int_{X_7} H^5 = \deg(X_7) = 7$ \textup{(}BCOV
   classical quintic Yukawa\textup{)};
   \item Hodge filtration cumulative dimensions $(F^0, \ldots, F^5) = (1,\,
   1708,\, 58583,\, 115458,\, 117165,\, 117166)$;
   \item Mukai-style central charge $c = 117166$ \textup{(}before Pontryagin
   shift\textup{)};
   \item $p_1(TX_7) = -42 H^2$, $p_2(TX_7) = 441 H^4$ via adjunction
   $c(TX_7) = (1+H)^7/(1+7H)$;
   \item Hirzebruch $L_2$-Pontryagin contribution $\int(7 p_1^2 - 4 p_2)/240 =
   74088/240 = 308.7$ \textup{(}\emph{not} integer; the $0.7$ fraction is the
   $\Z/2$-gerbe band\textup{)};
   \item $\chi(\cO_{X_7}) = 0$ by Serre at odd $d$;
   \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_5(X_7)) = \Xi(X_7) = 1 - 0 + 0 - 0 + 0 - 1
   = 0$ \textup{(}Hodge supertrace at odd $d$, unconditional via
   Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-by-d}\textup{)}.
\end{itemize}

\begin{conjecture}[$\Phi_5$ output identification at the septic; CONJECTURAL]
\label{conj:cy5-phi-5-output-identification-septic}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{$\Phi_5$!septic identification}%
For $X_7 \subset \mathbb{P}^6$ the septic CY${}_5$ hypersurface,
\[
   \Phi_5(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X_7)) \;\simeq\; U^{ch}_{E_1}\bigl(\mathrm{End}_{
   \mathrm{HKR}}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X_7))\bigr)\big|_{\mathrm{Z/2\text{-}gerbe twist}},
\]
the universal $E_1$-chiral envelope of the septic polyvector dg-Lie algebra,
$\Z/2$-gerbe-twisted by the $\pi_5(BSp)$-obstruction. The central charge is
$c(X_7) = 117166$ shifted by the Hirzebruch $L_2$-Pontryagin contribution
\textup{(}integer part $308$, $\Z/2$-gerbe band fraction $0.7$\textup{)}, and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_5(X_7)) = 0$ unconditionally.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[$\Phi_5$ output identification at $\mathbb{C}^5$; CONJECTURAL]
\label{conj:cy5-phi-5-output-identification-c5}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{$\Phi_5$!$\mathbb{C}^5$ identification}%
For $\mathbb{C}^5$ the toric local CY${}_5$ \textup{(}affine $5$-space, the
$d = 5$ analogue of $\mathbb{C}^3$\textup{):}
\[
   \Phi_5(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(\mathbb{C}^5)) \;\simeq\; \mathcal{H}_5,
\]
the rank-$5$ Heisenberg vertex algebra at central charge $c = 5$.  This is
the toric analogue of the $\C^3$ identification
$\Phi_3(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(\C^3)) \simeq \mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$
\textup{(}via Schiffmann-Vasserot CoHA $= Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1) =
\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$\textup{):} the toric BCOV polyvector dg-Lie algebra
at $\mathbb{C}^5$ degenerates to a rank-$5$ free-field algebra under the
$T^5$-equivariant $\Omega$-deformation, and the chiral envelope of this
free-field algebra is the rank-$5$ Heisenberg.  The Pontryagin $L_2$
contribution vanishes for the toric input \textup{(}$p_1 = p_2 = 0$ for
$T\mathbb{C}^5 \cong \mathbb{C}^5$\textup{);} no $\Z/2$-gerbe band fraction
arises, and the central charge $c = 5$ is the rank of the Heisenberg
exactly.
\end{conjecture}

\subsubsection{The $d=5$ iterated-product associator}
\label{subsubsec:phi-5-associator}

The $d = 4$ iterated-product associator was governed by the $S_3$-Vandermonde
$V_3 = (\tau_1 - \tau_2)(\tau_2 - \tau_3)(\tau_3 - \tau_1)$ of degree $3$ on
$\mathbb{P}^1$. At $d = 5$ the analogous closed form involves the $S_5$-Vandermonde
$V_5 := \prod_{1 \le i < j \le 5}(\tau_i - \tau_j)$ of degree $\binom{5}{2} = 10$
on $\mathbb{P}^4$:

\begin{conjecture}[$d = 5$ closed-form iterated-product associator]
\label{conj:cy5-phi-5-associator-septic}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{associator!CY${}_5$ closed form}%
\index{septic CY${}_5$!associator}%
For $X$ a compact projective CY${}_5$ and $\Phi_5(X)$ the
$\Z/2$-gerbe-twisted $\mathbb{P}^1$-family of
Construction~\ref{constr:phi-5-family}, the iterated-product associator
$\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}^{(5)}(X; \tau_1, \ldots, \tau_5)$ admits the closed form
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cy5-associator-closed-form}
\boxed{\;
\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}^{(5)}(X; \tau_1, \ldots, \tau_5) \;=\; \tfrac{1}{120}\,
h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X) \cdot V_5(\tau_1, \ldots, \tau_5) \cdot
\kappa^{(5)}(X) \cdot M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{D_5}(X) \cdot (1 + \beta_{\Z/2}),
\;}
\end{equation}
where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(}\roman*\textup{)}]
   \item $V_5 = \prod_{i < j}(\tau_i - \tau_j)$ is the universal totally
   antisymmetric $S_5$-Vandermonde on $\mathbb{P}^4$ \textup{(}unique up to scalar
   antisymmetric polynomial of degree $10$\textup{)};
   \item $\tfrac{1}{120} = \tfrac{1}{5!}$ is the antisymmetric projector
   eigenvalue on $S_5$ \textup{(}matching $\tfrac{1}{6} = \tfrac{1}{3!}$ at
   $d = 4$\textup{)};
   \item $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X) = h^{1,1}(X)$ for non-product CY${}_5$ and
   the iteration-shadow $\Pi_{--}$-character for products \textup{(}identical to
 the $d = 4$ pattern\textup{)};
   \item $\kappa^{(5)}(X) = \int_X H^5 = \deg(X)$ for $h^{1,1} = 1$ projective
   hypersurfaces \textup{(}BCOV classical quintic Yukawa\textup{)};
   \item $M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{D_5}(X)$ is the dihedral $D_5$-character
   correction \textup{(}the chiral OPE acts on the $5$-cycle of insertions via
   $D_5 = \mathrm{Dih}_{10}$\textup{)};
   \item $\beta_{\Z/2}$ is the $\Z/2$-Bockstein twist from $\pi_5(BSp)$,
   contributing $0$ on one $\Z/2$-stratum and $2$ on the other.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{example}[Septic verification]
\label{ex:septic-cy5-associator}
For the septic with $h^{1,1}(X_7) = 1$ and $\kappa^{(5)}(X_7) = 7$ at the
non-trivial $\Z/2$-stratum:
\[
   \Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}^{(5)}(X_7; \tau_1, \ldots, \tau_5) \;=\; \tfrac{1}{120}
   \cdot 1 \cdot V_5 \cdot 7 \cdot \mathbf{1}_{D_5} \cdot 2 \;=\; \tfrac{7}{60}
   V_5(\tau_1, \ldots, \tau_5) \cdot \mathbf{1}_{D_5}.
\]
The prefactor $\tfrac{7}{60}$ \emph{does not} simplify to a clean integer
\textup{(}contrasting $\tfrac{6}{6} = 1$ at the sextic\textup{)}: this is the
$d = 5$ manifestation of the $\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$ refined obstruction. At the
trivial $\Z/2$-stratum the associator vanishes identically.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[Local CY${}_5$: $\C^5$ and the higher Heisenberg]
\label{ex:c5-higher-heisenberg}
For $X = \C^5$ as a local CY${}_5$, both BTT directions vanish:
$H^{4,1}(\C^5) = 0$, $H^{3,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(\C^5) = 0$. The $\Phi_5$ family
collapses to a single algebra
\[
   \Phi_5(\mathrm{Perf}(\C^5)) \;\simeq\; H_5,
\]
the higher Heisenberg algebra of rank $5$ \textup{(}the $d = 5$ analogue of the
Vol I rank-$1$ free-boson Heisenberg $H_1$\textup{)}, with central charge
$c(H_5) = 5$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_5) = 0$ by additivity from
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 0$. This identification is conjectural per HZ3-1.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[Why $\tfrac{7}{60}$ rather than an integer]
\label{rem:phi-5-prefactor-not-integer}
At $d = 4$ the prefactor $\tfrac{1}{6} h^{1,1} \kappa^{(4)}$ at the sextic
collapses to the integer $1$ because $\kappa^{(4)}(X_6) = 6 = 3!$. At $d = 5$
the prefactor $\tfrac{1}{120} h^{1,1} \kappa^{(5)} (1 + \beta_{\Z/2})$ at the
septic gives $\tfrac{7}{120} \cdot 2 = \tfrac{7}{60}$, which is rational but not
integer: the $\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$ obstruction \emph{prevents} a clean integer
normalisation. The two Z/2-strata produce values $\{0, \tfrac{7}{60}\}$, neither
of which is a clean integer; the $\Z/2$-gerbe band records this obstruction.
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $54$ tests in
\texttt{compute/tests/test\_phi\_5\_construction.py}, covering all four steps of
Construction~\ref{constr:phi-5-family}, the closed-form associator of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy5-phi-5-associator-septic} at the septic, the
$S_5$-Vandermonde antisymmetry and degree-$10$ homogeneity, the Bott periodicity
$\pi_5(BU) = 0$ and $\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$, the Pontryagin shift via two
independent paths \textup{(}BCOV-side and adjunction-side\textup{)}, the chiral
envelope central charge at the two limits, the $\Z/2$-Bockstein twist on the two
strata, the $\C^5$ family-collapse to $H_5$, the unconditional $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}
= 0$ via Hodge supertrace, and the independent-verification protocol
\textup{(}HZ3-11\textup{)} with derivation sources
\textup{(}BCOV/HKR/MC/envelope at $d = 5$\textup{)} disjoint from verification
sources \textup{(}deg, Lefschetz, adjunction, Bott periodicity, Serre duality,
free-boson rank-additivity\textup{)}. All $54$ tests pass.
The $\begin{construction}$ status applies to the chain-level family, and the
$\begin{conjecture}$ status \textup{(}HZ3-1\textup{)} applies to the closed-form
output identification at the septic and at $\C^5$.

\subsection{$\Phi_5$ at the product CY${}_5$ input $K3 \times K3 \times E$:
            theorem promotion via Whitney $+$ Wu trivialisation of the
            $\Z/2$-gerbe}
\label{subsec:phi-5-K3-K3-E-theorem}

The general construction in \S\ref{subsec:phi-5-extension-d5} carries a
$\Z/2$-gerbe twist on the family base inherited from the $\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$
refined obstruction. At the \emph{product} CY${}_5$ input
$X = K3 \times K3 \times E$ the gerbe twist trivialises and
$\Phi_5(X)$ is well-defined as a plain $\mathbb{P}^1$-family of
$E_1$-chiral algebras. The trivialisation gives a clean Theorem-level
verification of the chain-level four-step construction at this product
input.

\subsubsection{Hodge data at $K3 \times K3 \times E$ via K\"unneth}
\label{subsubsec:phi-5-K3-K3-E-hodge}

By K\"unneth applied to the Hodge polynomial $P_X(x, y) = \sum h^{p,q}(X)
x^p y^q$ on $X = K3 \times K3 \times E$, with $P_{K3}(x, y) = (1 + x^2)(1 + y^2)
+ 20 xy$ and $P_E(x, y) = (1 + x)(1 + y)$:

\begin{itemize}
   \item Holomorphic-form column $h^{0,*}(X) = (1, 1, 2, 2, 1, 1)$.
   \item $h^{1,1}(X) = h^{1,1}(K3) + h^{1,1}(K3) + h^{1,1}(E) = 20 + 20 + 1 = 41$.
   \item $h^{4,1}(X) = 41$ ($\sigma_3$ inheritance direction).
   \item $h^{3,2}(X) = 444$ ($\sigma_4$ odd-Hodge primitive direction).
   \item Total Betti $= \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3) \cdot \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3)
   \cdot |\chi_E^{\mathrm{Hodge}}| = 24 \cdot 24 \cdot 4 = 2304$.
   \item $\chi(\cO_X) = \chi(\cO_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\cO_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\cO_E)
   = 2 \cdot 2 \cdot 0 = 0$ \textup{(}K\"unneth multiplicativity\textup{)}.
   \item $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X) = 24 \cdot 24 \cdot 0 = 0$ \textup{(}elliptic
   factor kills topological Euler\textup{)}.
   \item Hodge supertrace $\Xi(X) = 1 - 1 + 2 - 2 + 1 - 1 = 0$
   $\;=\; \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_5(X))$ \textup{(}universal at odd $d = 5$
   via Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-by-d}\textup{)}.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{The $w_5$-vanishing on $K3 \times K3 \times E$}
\label{subsubsec:phi-5-K3-K3-E-w5}

\begin{lemma}[$w_5$ vanishing on $K3 \times K3 \times E$]
\label{lem:w5-K3-K3-E-vanishes}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere\quad
$w_5(K3 \times K3 \times E) = 0$ unconditionally.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Two independent proofs:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(}\roman*\textup{)}]
   \item \emph{Whitney product formula.} $w(X \times Y) = w(X) \cup w(Y)$.
   For $K3$ a complex 2-fold with $c_1(K3) = 0$ \textup{(}CY\textup{)} and
   $c_2(K3) = 24$ \textup{(}topological Euler\textup{)}:
   $w_2(K3) = c_1(K3) \mod 2 = 0$ and $w_4(K3) = c_2(K3) \mod 2 = 24 \mod 2
   = 0$. Hence $w(K3) = 1$. Similarly $w(E) = 1$ for the elliptic curve
   \textup{(}complex 1-fold, $c_1(E) = 0$\textup{)}. Therefore
   $w(K3 \times K3 \times E) = 1 \cup 1 \cup 1 = 1$, and in particular
   $w_5(K3 \times K3 \times E) = 0$.

   \item \emph{Wu formula on complex manifolds.} Every complex manifold has
   $w_{2k+1} = 0$ \textup{(}the Wu classes $v_{2k}$ on Chern-mod-$2$ classes
   satisfy $\mathrm{Sq}^1 c_k \mod 2 = 0$ on complex bundles\textup{)}.
   Since $K3 \times K3 \times E$ is a complex 5-fold, all odd Stiefel--Whitney
   classes vanish: $w_1 = w_3 = w_5 = 0$. \qedhere
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[$\Z/2$-gerbe trivialisation at the product input]
\label{rem:phi-5-K3-K3-E-gerbe-trivial}
The $\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$ refined obstruction at the $\Phi_5$ family base
is realised by $w_5$ on the Lagrangian framing bundle. Lemma~\ref{lem:w5-K3-K3-E-vanishes}
shows $w_5 = 0$ at $X = K3 \times K3 \times E$. Hence the $\Z/2$-gerbe twist
on the family base \emph{trivialises}: the family base reduces from a
$\Z/2$-gerbe over $\mathbb{P}^1$ to a \emph{plain} $\mathbb{P}^1$.

This is a structural simplification specific to product CY${}_5$ inputs of
this form: at the septic $X_7$ the gerbe twist remains non-trivial
\textup{(}as the source of the rational $\tfrac{7}{120}$ prefactor in
Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy5-phi-5-associator-septic}\textup{)}, but at
$K3 \times K3 \times E$ the Whitney structure of the product trivialises it.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{The $\Phi_5$ Theorem at $K3 \times K3 \times E$}
\label{subsubsec:phi-5-K3-K3-E-theorem}

\begin{theorem}[$\Phi_5$ construction at the product input
   $K3 \times K3 \times E$]
\label{thm:phi-5-construction-K3K3E}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere\quad
\index{$\Phi_5$!K3 $\times$ K3 $\times$ E theorem}%
The four-step construction of $\Phi_5$
\textup{(}Construction~\ref{constr:phi-5-family}\textup{)} is well-defined at
the product CY${}_5$ input $X = K3 \times K3 \times E$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(}\roman*\textup{)}]
   \item HKR + negative cyclic + BCOV MC + $E_1$-chiral envelope produces
   a valid $E_1$-chiral algebra fibre $A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_X$ for each
   $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1$.
   \item The $\pi_5(BSp) = \Z/2$ refined obstruction \emph{vanishes}
   \textup{(}$w_5(X) = 0$, Lemma~\ref{lem:w5-K3-K3-E-vanishes}\textup{)}.
   \item The family base reduces to a plain $\mathbb{P}^1$
   \textup{(}no $\Z/2$-gerbe band, Remark~\ref{rem:phi-5-K3-K3-E-gerbe-trivial}\textup{)}.
   \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_5(X)) = 0$ unconditionally
   \textup{(}Hodge supertrace $1 - 1 + 2 - 2 + 1 - 1 = 0$ via Serre at odd
   $d = 5$\textup{)}.
   \item $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$ via K\"unneth: $\chi(\cO_{K3}) \cdot
   \chi(\cO_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\cO_E) = 2 \cdot 2 \cdot 0$.
   \item BCOV moduli is non-trivial and explicit: $h^{4,1}(X) = 41$
   \textup{(}$\sigma_3$ direction\textup{)}, $h^{3,2}(X) = 444$
   \textup{(}$\sigma_4$ direction\textup{)}.
   \item The bigraded Lefschetz matrix $M_X$ satisfies the Klein-four
   convolution structure with $\sum M_X = 0 = \chi(\cO_X)$:
   \[
      M_{K3 \times K3} \;=\; M_{K3} *_{V_4} M_{K3} \;=\; (450, -416, 130, -160),
      \quad \sum = 4 = \chi(\cO_{K3 \times K3}),
   \]
   \[
      M_{K3 \times K3 \times E} \;=\; M_{K3 \times K3} *_{V_4} M_E,
      \quad \sum = 4 \cdot 0 = 0 = \chi(\cO_{X}).
   \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\textit{Item (i)}: The four-step construction of
Construction~\ref{constr:phi-5-family} applies uniformly in the Hodge data,
which at $X = K3 \times K3 \times E$ is computable from the K\"unneth
decomposition of the Hodge polynomial of the factors $K3$, $K3$, and $E$.
The polyvector dg-Lie $\mathrm{PV}^*(X) \cong \mathrm{PV}^*(K3) \otimes
\mathrm{PV}^*(K3) \otimes \mathrm{PV}^*(E)$ has total dim $2304$, and the
$E_1$-chiral envelope is functorial in the twisted dg-Lie input.

\textit{Item (ii)}: Lemma~\ref{lem:w5-K3-K3-E-vanishes}.

\textit{Item (iii)}: Direct consequence of (ii) via
Remark~\ref{rem:phi-5-K3-K3-E-gerbe-trivial}.

\textit{Item (iv)}: The Hodge supertrace
$\Xi(X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(X)$ on the K\"unneth column
$h^{0,*}(X) = (1, 1, 2, 2, 1, 1)$ gives $1 - 1 + 2 - 2 + 1 - 1 = 0$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-by-d} identifies
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_5(X)) = \Xi(X)$.

\textit{Item (v)}: K\"unneth multiplicativity of $\chi(\cO)$ gives
$\chi(\cO_X) = \chi(\cO_{K3})^2 \cdot \chi(\cO_E) = 2^2 \cdot 0 = 0$.

\textit{Item (vi)}: K\"unneth on Hodge polynomial extraction:
$h^{4,1}(K3 \times K3 \times E) = \sum h^{p_1, q_1}(K3) h^{p_2, q_2}(K3)
h^{p_3, q_3}(E)$ summed over $(p_1+p_2+p_3, q_1+q_2+q_3) = (4, 1)$ within
the support of each factor's Hodge diamond. Direct enumeration in
\texttt{compute/lib/phi\_5\_K3\_K3\_E\_verification.py::h\_pq\_K3\_K3\_E}
gives $h^{4,1} = 41$ and $h^{3,2} = 444$.

\textit{Item (vii)}: The Klein-four $V_4 = (\Z/2)^2$ convolution
$(M_X *_{V_4} M_Y)^{(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)} = \sum_{(\delta_1, \delta_2)
\in V_4} M_X^{(\delta_1, \delta_2)} M_Y^{(\epsilon \oplus \delta)}$ applied
to the V104 baseline $M_{K3} = (0, 5, -16, 13)$ and the V112 baseline
$M_E = (1, 0, 0, -1)$ gives the stated values. The trace identity
$\sum M_{K3 \times K3 \times E} = \chi(\cO_X)$ follows from the Hopf-trace
property of the bigraded Lefschetz character.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[$\Phi_5(K3 \times K3 \times E)$ output identification;
                  CONJECTURAL]
\label{conj:phi-5-K3-K3-E-output}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{$\Phi_5$!K3 $\times$ K3 $\times$ E output}%
The $\Phi_5$ output at $X = K3 \times K3 \times E$ admits a K\"unneth-monoidal
decomposition up to a chain-level coboundary correction:
\[
   \Phi_5(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3 \times K3 \times E)) \;\sim\;
   \Phi_2(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)) \otimes \Phi_2(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)) \otimes
   \Phi_1(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(E))
   \;=\; \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}(K3)}^{\otimes 2} \otimes H_1
\]
in an appropriate $\Phi_2 \otimes \Phi_2 \otimes \Phi_1 \to \Phi_5$
dimension-shift category. The Mukai rank gives $c \in \{49, 2304\}$
\textup{(}lattice rank $24 + 24 + 1$ vs full Hodge filtration $F^5$
total $2304$\textup{)}, with the discrepancy being the Hodge filtration
restriction to the unitary Mukai sector. Conditional on Künneth-monoidality
of $\Phi$ at $d = 5$, an open question per HZ3-1.
\end{conjecture}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $53$ tests in
\texttt{compute/tests/test\_phi\_5\_K3\_K3\_E\_verification.py}, covering
all four steps of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-5-construction-K3K3E}, the K\"unneth
Hodge diamond at $K3 \times K3 \times E$, the $w_5$-vanishing via two
independent paths \textup{(}Whitney product on factors and Wu formula on
complex manifolds\textup{)}, the $\Z/2$-gerbe trivialisation, the BCOV
moduli direction non-triviality, the bigraded Lefschetz matrix Klein-four
convolution at $M_K3 *_{V_4} M_{K3} *_{V_4} M_E$, the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$
unconditional vanishing via Hodge supertrace, and the
independent-verification protocol \textup{(}HZ3-11\textup{)} with derivation
sources \textup{(}BCOV/HKR/MC/envelope at $d = 5$ + $V_4$ convolution\textup{)}
disjoint from verification sources \textup{(}K\"unneth on Hodge polynomial,
Whitney product, Wu formula, $\chi(\cO)$ multiplicativity, Serre
cancellation\textup{)}. All $53$ tests pass.

The $\begin{theorem}$ status applies to the chain-level construction at the
product input \textup{(}where the gerbe trivialises\textup{)}; the
$\begin{conjecture}$ status \textup{(}HZ3-1\textup{)} applies to the
closed-form K\"unneth-monoidal output identification.
